. In general the pregluing is unique only up to
\mathbb{R}
-translation.
Let
F^{\circ}_{+}:=\dot{F}_{+}-\mathcal{E}_{+,T}
and
F^{\circ}_{-}:=\dot{F}_{-}-\mathcal{E}_{-,T}
. The domain
(\dot{F}=F-{\bf p},j)
is then obtained by gluing
(F^{\circ}_{+},j_{+}|_{F^{\circ}_{+}})
,
(F^{\circ}_{-},j_{-}|_{F^{\circ}_{-}})
, and
A_{[-T,T]}
in the expected way. The map
u^{*}
agrees with
u_{+,T}
on
F^{\circ}_{+}
and with
u_{-,T}
on
F^{\circ}_{-}
, and is given by

(7.1.1)

u_{*}(s,t)=(s,t,\beta_{+,T}(s)\eta_{+,T}(s,t)+\beta_{-,T}(s)\eta_{-,T}(s,t))

on
A_{[-T,T]}
.

(2)

Given
(\mathcal{F}_{i},u_{i})\in V_{i}/\mathbb{R}
,
i=1,\dots,m
, with a prescribed identification of the ends and gluing parameters
T_{1},\dots,T_{m-1}
, we can analogously define a pregluing
u^{*}((\mathcal{F}_{1},u_{1})_{\mathbb{R}},\dots,(\mathcal{F}_{m},u_{m})_{\mathbb{R}},T_{1},\dots,T_{m-1})
which is unique up to
\mathbb{R}
-translation.

7.2.
Gluing of domain complex structures

We consider the following domain gluing, which is close to (but not quite the same as) the gluing of the domains in Section
7.1
. The reason for the slight discrepancy is that each of
F^{\circ}_{+}
and
F^{\circ}_{-}
is obtained from
\dot{F}_{+}
and
\dot{F}_{-}
by removing a cusp that is close to but not necessarily the same as a
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{\pm}
-thin cusp for some
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{\pm}
.

Fix
\dot{F}_{-}
,
\dot{F}_{+}
, and
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}>0
small. We are gluing
\dot{F}_{-}
and
\dot{F}_{+}
along one “positive end” of
\dot{F}_{-}
and one “negative end” of
\dot{F}_{+}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{\pm}
be a Teichmüller slice for a small open set
\mathcal{U}_{\pm}\subset\operatorname{Teich}(\dot{F}_{\pm})
and let
g(j_{\pm})
be the complete finite-volume hyperbolic metric compatible with
j_{\pm}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{\pm}
. We also fix
j^{0}_{\pm}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}
. Let
\mathcal{E}_{\pm}^{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}(j_{\pm})
be the (interior of the)
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
-thin component of
g(j_{\pm})
corresponding to the end that is glued and let
F_{\pm}^{\bullet}(j_{\pm}):=\dot{F}_{\pm}-\mathcal{E}_{\pm}^{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}(j_{\pm})
. Also choose
r_{\pm}(j_{\pm})\in\partial F_{\pm}^{\bullet}(j_{\pm})
smoothly with respect to
j_{\pm}
.

Given
(T,\tau)\in[R,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}
, we glue

(F_{-}^{\bullet}(j_{-}),j_{-})\cup([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z},j_{std})\cup(F_{+}^{\bullet}(j_{+}),j_{+})

so that
r_{-}(j_{-})
is identified with
(0,0)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}
and
r_{+}(j_{+})
is identified with
(T,\tau)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}
.
We denote the resulting Riemann surface by
(\dot{F}(j_{-},j_{+},T,\tau),j(j_{-},j_{+},T,\tau))
. We also write
\dot{F}=\dot{F}(j_{-}^{0},j_{+}^{0},R,0)
; this is our reference surface. (Note that when
(T,\tau_{1}),(T,\tau_{2})\in[R,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}
and
\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}
, the complex structures are diffeomorphic, but differ by a Dehn twist about a separating surface and correspond to distinct points in Teichmüller space.)

This defines a map

\kappa:\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{-}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{+}\times[R,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\to\operatorname{Teich}(\dot{F}),

(j_{-},j_{+},T,\tau)\mapsto(\dot{F}(j_{-},j_{+},T,\tau),j(j_{-},j_{+},T,\tau)).

Theorem 7.2.1
.

For
R\gg 0
,
\kappa
is a
C^{1}
-embedding.

Idea of proof.

This follows from Kazdan-Warner
[
KW1
,
KW2
]
. For
R\gg 0
, we can down a metric with negative curvature on
\dot{F}(j_{-},j_{+},T,\tau)
which is conformally equivalent to
j(j_{-},j_{+},T,\tau)
, agrees with
g(j_{\pm})
on
F_{\pm}^{\bullet}(j_{\pm})
, and whose curvature is very close to
-1
on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}
. In order to find a hyperbolic metric in the same conformal class we solve an equation of type
\Delta u=1-he^{u}
, where
u:\dot{F}\to\mathbb{R}
,
\Delta
is a Laplacian, and
h:\dot{F}\to\mathbb{R}
is close to
1
(and approaches
1
as
R\to\infty
); see
[
KW1
, Section 2]
. When
h
is close to
1
, this can be solved using the inverse function theorem; see
[
KW2
]
. We remark that negative curvature implies that the linearized operator is invertible and also that solving the equation can be done in a differentiable family.

The above discussion implies that the Fenchel-Nielsen coordinates for
j_{\pm}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{\pm}
are very close to the corresponding Fenchel-Nielsen coordinates for
\kappa(j_{-},j_{+},T,\tau)\in\operatorname{Teich}(\dot{F})
when
R\gg 0
, implying the theorem.
∎

7.3.
Banach spaces

The function spaces that we use are
Morrey spaces
, following
[
HT2
, Section 5.5]
.
10
10

10

This is rather nonstandard and we chose to adopt it to avoid redoing some work in
[
HT2
]
for
W^{k,p}
-spaces.
Let
u:\dot{F}\to\mathbb{R}\times M
be a finite energy holomorphic curve. On
\dot{F}
we choose a Riemannian metric so that the ends are cylindrical and we use the
\mathbb{R}
-invariant Riemannian metric on
\mathbb{R}\times M
.

The
Morrey space

\mathcal{H}_{0}(\dot{F},\wedge^{0,1}T^{*}\dot{F}\otimes u^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M))
is the Banach space which is the completion of the compactly supported sections of
\wedge^{0,1}T^{*}\dot{F}\otimes u^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M)
with respect to the norm

\|\xi\|=\left(\int_{\dot{F}}|\xi|^{2}\right)^{1/2}+\left(\sup_{x\in\dot{F}}\sup_{\rho\in(0,1]}\rho^{-1/2}\int_{B_{\rho}(x)}|\xi|^{2}\right)^{1/2},

where
B_{\rho}(x)\subset\dot{F}
is the ball of radius
\rho
about
x
. Similarly,
\mathcal{H}_{1}(\dot{F},u^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M))
is the completion of the compactly supported sections of
u^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M)
with respect to

\|\xi\|_{*}=\|\nabla\xi\|+\|\xi\|.

The analog of the usual Sobolev embedding theorem is the following:

Lemma 7.3.1
.

There is a bounded linear map

\mathcal{H}_{1}(\dot{F},u^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M))\to C^{0,1/4}(\dot{F},u^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M))\cap L^{\infty}(\dot{F},u^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M)),\quad\xi\mapsto\xi,

where
C^{0,1/4}
denotes the space of Hölder continuous functions with exponent
\frac{1}{4}
.

We also define the weighted Morrey spaces
\mathcal{H}_{i,\delta}(*)=\mathcal{H}_{0,\delta}(\dot{F}_{\pm},\wedge^{0,1}T^{*}\dot{F}_{\pm}\otimes(u_{\pm})^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M))
and
\mathcal{H}_{1,\delta}(\dot{F}_{\pm},(u_{\pm})^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M))
with weight
\delta>0
as the space of sections
\psi
such that
\psi\cdot f_{\delta}^{\pm}\in\mathcal{H}_{i}(*)
. Here
f_{\delta}^{\pm}:\mathbb{R}\times M\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
is the weight function for
u_{\pm}
which agrees with
e^{\delta|s|}
at the ends and is normalized so that
f_{\delta}^{\pm}(s,x)=e^{\delta|s|}
for
\mp s\geq 0
. Their norms are denoted by
\|\cdot\|_{\delta}
and
\|\cdot\|_{*,\delta}
. We will also write
\|\cdot\|_{\delta}^{\pm,T}
and
\|\cdot\|_{*,\delta}^{\pm,T}
with respect to the translates
f_{\delta}^{\pm,T}
.

7.4.
The setup

Let
\psi_{\pm}
be sections of
u_{\pm}^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M)
and let
\psi_{\pm,T}=\tau_{\pm(T+T_{0})}\circ\psi_{\pm}
be sections of
u^{*}_{\pm,T}T(\mathbb{R}\times M)
. The exponential maps
\exp_{u_{\pm,T}}
can be chosen such that

\exp_{u_{\pm,T}}\psi_{\pm,T}=(s,t,\eta_{\pm,T})+\psi_{\pm,T}

on
\mathcal{E}_{\pm,T+T_{0}}
, by taking the Riemannian metric
g_{0}
on
\mathbb{R}\times M
to be
\mathbb{R}
-invariant and equal to the standard flat metric on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}/\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}(\gamma_{1})\mathbb{Z}\times D
.

We then deform
u_{*}
to

(7.4.1)

u=\exp_{u_{*}}(\beta_{+,T}\psi_{+,T}+\beta_{-,T}\psi_{-,T}),

where
\exp_{u_{*}}
agrees with
\exp_{u_{\pm,T}}
on
\dot{F}_{\pm}-\mathcal{E}_{\pm,T+T_{0}}
and with

\exp_{u_{*}}(\beta_{+,T}\psi_{+,T}+\beta_{-,T}\psi_{-,T})=(s,t,\eta_{*})+\beta_{+,T}\psi_{+,T}+\beta_{-,T}\psi_{-,T}

on
A_{[-(T+T_{0}),T+T_{0}]}
.
Here
\eta_{*}=\beta_{+,T}\eta_{+,T}+\beta_{-,T}\eta_{-,T}
. We want to solve for
\psi_{+}
and
\psi_{-}
in the equation

(7.4.2)

\overline{\partial}u\in E^{\prime}_{(\mathcal{F},u)},

with
(\mathcal{F},u)=(F,j,{\bf p},u)
. Here
J
is understood and
E^{\prime}
is as given in Equation (6.3.1).

We identify

\Phi=\Phi_{T}:\wedge^{0,1}u^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M)\xrightarrow{\sim}\wedge^{0,1}(u_{*})^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M)

as follows: away from the neck region
A_{[-(T+2T_{0}),T+2T_{0}]}
we parallel transport using a complex linear connection induced by the Levi-Civita connection of an adapted Riemannian metric (see
[
MS
, p.39]
); on
A_{[-(T+T_{0}),T+T_{0}]}
we map
\eta\otimes(ds-idt)\mapsto\eta\otimes(ds-idt)
; and on
A_{[-(T+2T_{0}),-(T+T_{0})]}
and
A_{[T+T_{0},T+2T_{0}]}
we interpolate between the two identifications.
Similarly, we identify

\Phi_{\pm}=\Phi_{\pm}^{\psi_{\pm,T}}:\wedge^{0,1}(\exp_{u_{\pm,T}}\psi_{\pm,T})^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M)\xrightarrow{\sim}\wedge^{0,1}u_{\pm,T}^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M).

The equation
\overline{\partial}u\in E^{\prime}_{(\mathcal{F},u)}
, or more precisely the equation

(7.4.3)

{\Phi}\overline{\partial}u\in{\Phi}E^{\prime}_{(\mathcal{F},u)},

can then be written as

(7.4.4)

\beta_{-,T}\left(D_{-,T}\psi_{-,T}+e_{-,T}+\mathcal{L}_{-}+\mathcal{R}_{-}\right)+\beta_{+,T}\left(D_{+,T}\psi_{+,T}+e_{+,T}+\mathcal{L}_{+}+\mathcal{R}_{+}\right)\in{\Phi}E_{(\mathcal{F},u)}^{\prime},

where
D_{\pm}
(resp.
D_{\pm,T}
) is the linearization of
\overline{\partial}
for
u_{\pm}
(resp.
u_{\pm,T}
),
e_{\pm}:=\overline{\partial}u_{\pm}
(resp.
e_{\pm,T}:=\overline{\partial}u_{\pm,T}
), and
\mathcal{L}_{\pm}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\pm}
are described below. (The descriptions of
\mathcal{L}_{\pm}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\pm}
on
A_{[-T,T]}
are obtained by expanding
\tfrac{\partial u}{\partial s}+J(u)\tfrac{\partial u}{\partial t}
, where we write

u(s,t)=\begin{pmatrix}s\\
t\\
\beta_{+,T}\eta_{+,T}+\beta_{-,T}\eta_{-,T}\end{pmatrix}+\beta_{+,T}\psi_{+,T}+\beta_{-,T}\psi_{-,T}

and

J(u)=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1&0\\
1&0&0\\
X\eta^{\prime}&-j_{0}X\eta^{\prime}&j_{0}\end{pmatrix},

where:

•

we are using simple coordinates
(s,t,(x,y))
written as column vectors;

•

\eta^{\prime}=\beta_{+,T}(\eta_{+,T}+\psi_{+,T}^{N})+\beta_{-,T}(\eta_{-,T}+\psi_{-,T}^{N})
;

•

X
is the constant matrix defined in the paragraph after Definition 3.1.1;

•

j_{0}
is the standard complex structure on
\mathbb{R}^{2n}
;

•

\psi^{N}_{\pm,T}
is the
(x,y)
-component of
\psi_{\pm,T}
.

The details are left to the reader.)

1.
\mathcal{L}_{\pm}=\mathcal{L}_{\pm}(\psi_{-,T},\psi_{+,T})
has support on
A_{[-T,T]}
and is given by

\displaystyle 2\mathcal{L}_{\pm}=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0\\
0\\
\frac{\partial\beta_{\mp,T}}{\partial s}\eta_{\mp,T}\end{array}\right)+\frac{\partial\beta_{\mp,T}}{\partial s}\psi_{\mp,T}

\displaystyle+\widetilde{\beta}_{\pm,T}\beta_{\mp,T}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
X\eta_{\pm,T}&-j_{0}X\eta_{\pm,T}&0\end{array}\right)\frac{\partial\psi_{\mp,T}}{\partial t}

\displaystyle+\widetilde{\beta}_{\pm,T}\beta_{\mp,T}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
X\psi^{N}_{\pm,T}&-j_{0}X\psi^{N}_{\pm,T}&0\end{array}\right)\frac{\partial\psi_{\mp,T}}{\partial t}

\displaystyle+\widetilde{\beta}_{\pm,T}(\beta_{\pm,T}-1)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
X\eta_{\pm,T}&-j_{0}X\eta_{\pm,T}&0\end{array}\right)\frac{\partial\psi_{\pm,T}}{\partial t}.

Here:

•

the factor
\otimes(ds-idt)
is omitted from each term;

•

\widetilde{\beta}_{-,T}(s)=\beta(\frac{T+hr-s}{hr})
and
\widetilde{\beta}_{+,T}(s)=\beta(\frac{T+hr+s}{hr})
;

Terminology 7.4.1
.

(1)

We say that a function
F(\psi)
is “linear” if there exists a constant
c>0
such that
|F(\psi)(x)|<c|\psi(x)|
at every point
x
of the domain of
\psi
. We will use the shorthand
{\mathfrak{l}}(\psi)
to denote an unspecified “linear” map.

(2)

Following
[
HT2
, Definition 5.1]
we say that
F(\psi)
is
type
1
quadratic
if it can be written as

F(\psi)=P(\psi)+Q(\psi)\cdot\nabla\psi,

where there exists a constant
c>0
such that
|P(\psi)(x)|\leq c|\psi(x)|^{2}
and
|Q(\psi)(x)|\leq c|\psi(x)|
at every point
x
of the domain of
\psi
.

2. The remaining terms of
{\Phi}\overline{\partial}u
are grouped into
\beta_{-}\mathcal{R}_{-}
and
\beta_{+}\mathcal{R}_{+}
.
One can see that
\mathcal{R}_{\pm}=\mathcal{R}_{\pm}(\psi_{\pm,T})
is type
1
quadratic. On
A_{[-T,T]}
, we can explicitly write

(7.4.17)

\mathcal{R}_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\pm,T}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
X\psi_{\pm,T}^{N}&-j_{0}X\psi_{\pm,T}^{N}&0\end{array}\right)\frac{\partial\psi_{\pm,T}}{\partial t}.

We also write
E^{\prime}_{(\mathcal{F},u)}=E^{\prime}_{\psi_{\pm},T}
and decompose

E_{\psi_{\pm},T}^{\prime}=(E^{\prime}_{\psi_{\pm},T})_{+}\oplus(E^{\prime}_{\psi_{\pm},T})_{-},

where
(E^{\prime}_{\psi_{\pm},T})_{\pm}
corresponds to
u_{\pm,T}
. In order to solve Equation (
7.4.4
) we solve for
(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})
in the pair of equations

(7.4.18)

D_{\pm,T}\psi_{\pm,T}+e_{\pm,T}+\mathcal{L}_{\pm}(\psi_{-,T},\psi_{+,T})+\mathcal{R}_{\pm}(\psi_{\pm,T})\in\Phi_{\pm}^{\psi_{\pm,T}}(E^{\prime}_{\psi_{\pm},T})_{\pm}.

Let

(7.4.19)

\Pi_{\pm}^{\psi_{\pm},T}:\mathcal{H}_{0,\delta}(\dot{F},\wedge^{0,1}u_{\pm,T}^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M))\to(\Phi_{\pm}^{\psi_{\pm,T}}(E^{\prime}_{\psi_{\pm},T})_{\pm})^{\perp}

be the projection onto the
L^{2}
-orthogonal complement of
\Phi_{\pm}^{\psi_{\pm,T}}(E^{\prime}_{\psi_{\pm},T})_{\pm}
.
Then Equation (
7.4.18
) is equivalent to

(7.4.20)

\Theta_{\pm}(\psi_{-},\psi_{+}):=\Pi_{\pm}^{\psi_{\pm},T}(D_{\pm,T}\psi_{\pm,T}+e_{\pm,T}+\mathcal{L}_{\pm}(\psi_{-,T},\psi_{+,T})+\mathcal{R}_{\pm}(\psi_{\pm,T}))=0.

Since
D_{\pm,T}
is transverse to
E_{\pm}
and
\Phi_{\pm}^{\psi_{\pm,T}}(E^{\prime}_{\psi_{\pm},T})_{\pm}
is close to
E_{\pm}
when
T\gg 0
and
\|\psi_{\pm}\|_{*,\delta}
is small, it follows that
D_{\pm,T}
is transverse to
\Phi_{\pm}^{\psi_{\pm,T}}(E^{\prime}_{\psi_{\pm},T})_{\pm}
, i.e.,

\Pi_{\pm}^{\psi_{\pm},T}D_{\pm,T}:\mathcal{H}_{1,\delta}(\dot{F},u_{\pm,T}^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M))\to(\Phi_{\pm}^{\psi_{\pm,T}}(E^{\prime}_{\psi_{\pm},T})_{\pm})^{\perp}

is surjective.

7.5.
Definition of gluing map

In this subsection we make some estimates and define the gluing map at the end. We use the convention that constants such as
C
may change from line to line when making estimates.

Let
\lambda=\min\{\lambda_{1},|\lambda_{-1}|\}
. Suppose that
\delta
satisfies
0<100\cdot\delta<\lambda
. Let
\mathcal{H}_{\pm}=\mathcal{H}_{1,\delta}(\dot{F},u_{\pm}^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M))
, let
\mathcal{H}_{\pm}^{\perp,\psi_{\pm},T}
be the
L^{2}
-orthogonal complement of

\ker\left(\Pi_{\pm}^{\psi_{\pm},T}\circ D_{\pm,T}\circ\tau_{\pm(T+T_{0})}\right)

in
\mathcal{H}_{\pm}
, where
\tau_{\pm(T+T_{0})}
is the map induced by
\tau_{\pm(T+T_{0})}
, and let
\mathcal{B}_{\pm}
be the closed ball of radius
\widetilde{\varepsilon}
in
\mathcal{H}_{\pm}
centered at
0
.

The following lemma is modeled on
[
HT2
, Proposition 5.6]
, but the estimates are slightly different.

Lemma 7.5.1
.

There exist
r\gg 0
and
C,\widetilde{\varepsilon}>0
such that for
T\gg 0
and
\|e_{+}\|_{*,\delta}
small the following holds:

(1)

There is a map
P_{+}:\mathcal{B}_{-}\to\mathcal{H}_{+}
such that
\Theta_{+}(\psi_{-},P_{+}(\psi_{-}))=0
and
\psi_{+}=P_{+}(\psi_{-})\in\mathcal{H}_{+}^{\perp,\psi_{+},T}
.

(2)

\|P_{+}(\psi_{-})\|_{*,\delta}\leq C(\|e_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+r^{-1/2}e^{-2(\lambda-\delta)T}+(e^{-2\delta T}+r^{-1})\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta})
.

(3)

\|P_{+}(\psi_{-})-P_{+}(\psi^{\prime}_{-})\|_{*,\delta}\leq C\|\psi_{-}-\psi_{-}^{\prime}\|_{*,\delta}(e^{-2\delta T}+r^{-1}+\widetilde{\varepsilon})
.

Let
\mathcal{I}_{+}:\mathcal{B}_{-}\times\mathcal{B}_{+}\to\mathcal{H}_{+}
be the map given by:

(7.5.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{+}(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})=

\displaystyle-\tau_{+(T+T_{0})}^{-1}(\Pi_{+}^{\psi_{+},T}D_{+,T})^{-1}\Pi_{+}^{\psi_{+},T}(e_{+,T}+\mathcal{L}_{+}(\psi_{-,T},\psi_{+,T})+\mathcal{R}_{+}(\psi_{+,T})).

Here
(\Pi_{+}^{\psi_{+},T}D_{+,T})^{-1}
is the bounded inverse of
\Pi_{+}^{\psi_{+},T}D_{+,T}
.

Proof.

(1) We are trying to solve for
\psi_{+}
in

\Pi_{+}^{\psi_{+},T}(D_{+,T}\psi_{+,T}+e_{+,T}+\mathcal{L}_{+}(\psi_{-,T},\psi_{+,T})+\mathcal{R}_{+}(\psi_{+,T}))=0.

Using the function
\mathcal{I}_{+}
we define
P(\psi_{-})
as the unique fixed point, i.e.,
\psi_{+}
satisfying

(7.5.2)

\mathcal{I}_{+}(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})=\psi_{+}.

The unique fixed point is guaranteed by the contraction mapping theorem, which follows from Claims
7.5.2
and
7.5.4
.

Claim 7.5.2
.

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{I}_{+}(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})\|_{*,\delta}\leq

\displaystyle(\widetilde{C}_{1}(\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta})+\widetilde{C}_{2}(T))\|e_{+}\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle+\widetilde{C}(\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta})(r^{-1/2}e^{-2(\lambda-\delta)T}+(e^{-2\delta T}+r^{-1})\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+e^{-2\lambda T}\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\psi_{+}\|^{2}_{*,\delta}),

where
\widetilde{C}(\sharp)
,
\widetilde{C}_{1}(\sharp)
,
\widetilde{C}_{2}(\sharp)
are continuous functions of
\sharp
;
\widetilde{C}_{1}(\sharp)\to 0
as
\sharp\to 0
; and
\widetilde{C}_{2}(\sharp)\to 0
as
\sharp\to\infty
.

Proof of Claim
7.5.2
.

We will be using Lemma
7.3.1
(more precisely
|\zeta|_{C^{0}}\leq C\|\zeta\|_{*,\delta}
) several times without explicit mention.

We first obtain

(7.5.3)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{L}_{+}(\psi_{-,T},\psi_{+,T})\right\|_{\delta}^{+,T}\leq

\displaystyle C(r^{-1/2}e^{-2(\lambda-\delta)T}+r^{-1}\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}+e^{-2\delta T}\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+e^{-2\lambda T}\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}),

where each term on the right-hand side corresponds to the terms on the right-hand side in Equation (
7.4
). The first term has bound

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{\partial\beta_{-,T}}{\partial s}

\displaystyle\eta_{-,T}\|_{\delta}^{+,T}=\|\tfrac{\partial\beta_{-,T}}{\partial s}\eta_{-,T}f_{\delta}^{+,T}\|=\|\tfrac{\partial\beta_{-,T}}{\partial s}\eta_{-,T}e^{-\delta(s-(T+T_{0}))}\|

\displaystyle\leq C(r^{-1}\cdot e^{-\lambda\cdot 2T}\cdot e^{\delta\cdot 2T_{0}}\cdot\sqrt{hr}\cdot+r^{-1}\cdot e^{-\lambda\cdot 2T}\cdot e^{\delta\cdot 2T_{0}})

\displaystyle\leq C(r^{-1/2}e^{-2(\lambda-\delta)T}),

where we are using the bound
|\frac{\partial\beta_{-}}{\partial s}|<cr^{-1}
for some
c>0
and exponential decay bounds for
\eta_{-}
. The second term has bound

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{\partial\beta_{-,T}}{\partial s}\psi_{-,T}\cdot f_{\delta}^{+,T}\|

\displaystyle=\|\tfrac{\partial\beta_{-,T}}{\partial s}\psi_{-,T}\cdot e^{-\delta(s-(T+T_{0}))}\|\leq\|\tfrac{\partial\beta_{-,T}}{\partial s}\psi_{-,T}\cdot e^{2\delta T}\|

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{-1}\|\psi_{-}\|_{\delta}\leq Cr^{-1}\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}.

The third term has bound

\displaystyle C\|\widetilde{\beta}_{+,T}\beta_{-,T}

\displaystyle e^{\lambda(s-(T+T_{0}))}\tfrac{\partial\psi_{-,T}}{\partial t}e^{-\delta(s-(T+T_{0}))}\|

\displaystyle=C\|\widetilde{\beta}_{+,T}\beta_{-,T}e^{(\lambda-2\delta)s-\lambda(T+T_{0})}\tfrac{\partial\psi_{-,T}}{\partial t}e^{\delta(s+(T+T_{0}))}\|

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-2\delta(T+T_{0})}\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}\leq Ce^{-2\delta T}\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}.

The fourth term has bound

\displaystyle C\|\widetilde{\beta}_{+,T}\beta_{-,T}

\displaystyle\psi_{+,T}\psi_{-,T}e^{-\delta(s-(T+T_{0}))}\|

\displaystyle\leq C|\psi_{+,T}e^{-\delta(s-(T+T_{0}))}|_{C^{0}}\cdot\|\widetilde{\beta}_{+,T}\beta_{-,T}\psi_{-,T}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}\|\psi_{-}\|_{\delta}\leq C\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}.

The last term has bound

\displaystyle C\|(\widetilde{\beta}_{+,T})(\beta_{+,T}-1)\eta_{+,T}\tfrac{\partial\psi_{+,T}}{\partial t}e^{-\delta(s-(T+T_{0}))}\|

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-2\lambda T}\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}.

Also we obtain

(7.5.4)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}_{+}(\psi_{+,T})\|^{+,T}_{\delta}\leq C\|\psi_{+}\|^{2}_{*,\delta},

since
\mathcal{R}_{+}(\psi_{+,T})
is type 1 quadratic.

Next we consider the
L^{2}
-projection
\Pi_{+}
to
(\Phi_{+}E^{\prime}_{+})^{\perp}
, where we are suppressing
{\psi_{\pm},T}
from the notation. Let
e_{1}(\psi_{+},T),\dots,e_{k}(\psi_{+},T)
be an orthonormal basis for
\Phi_{+}E^{\prime}_{+}
; it is not hard to see that
e_{i}(\psi_{+},T)
can be taken to be continuous with respect to
\psi_{+}\in\mathcal{B}_{+}
. Then

\displaystyle\Pi_{+}(\zeta)=\zeta-\sum_{i}\langle e_{i}(\psi_{+},T),\zeta\rangle e_{i}(\psi_{+},T),

where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
is the
L^{2}
-inner product.
This allows us to estimate

(7.5.5)

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{+}(\zeta)\|_{\delta}\leq\|\zeta\|_{\delta}+\sum_{i}\|\zeta\|_{\delta}\cdot\|e_{i}(\psi_{+},T)\|_{\delta}\leq\widetilde{C}(\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta})\|\zeta\|_{\delta}.

The first inequality follows from
|\langle e_{i}(\psi_{+},T),\zeta\rangle|\leq\|e_{i}(\psi_{+})\|_{L^{2}}\cdot\|\zeta\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\zeta\|_{\delta}
, where
\|\cdot\|_{L^{2}}
is the
L^{2}
-norm, and the second inequality follows from the continuity of
e_{i}(\psi_{+},T)
with respect to
\psi_{+}
.

Claim 7.5.3
.

(\Pi_{+}D_{+})^{-1}
depends continuously on
\psi_{+}\in\mathcal{B}_{+}
.

Observe that the domain of
(\Pi_{+}D_{+})^{-1}
uses the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\delta}
and the range of
(\Pi_{+}D_{+})^{-1}
uses
\|\cdot\|_{*,\delta}
.

Sketch of proof of Claim
7.5.3
.

\Pi_{+}
depends continuously on
\psi_{+}\in\mathcal{B}_{+}
and hence so does
\Pi_{+}D_{+}
. The inverse hence also depends continuously on
\psi_{+}
.
∎

We also obtain

(7.5.6)

\displaystyle\|\tau_{+(T+T_{0})}^{-1}(\Pi_{+}^{\psi_{+},T}D_{+,T})^{-1}\Pi_{+}^{\psi_{+},T}(e_{+,T})\|_{\delta}\leq(\widetilde{C}_{1}(\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta})+\widetilde{C}_{2}(T))\|e_{+}\|_{*,\delta}.

Claim
7.5.2
then follows from combining Estimates (
7.5.3
), (
7.5.4
), (
7.5.5
), (
7.5.6
), and Claim
7.5.3
.
∎

If
r,T\gg 0
,
\widetilde{\varepsilon}>0
is small, and
\|e_{+}\|_{*,\delta}
is small, then
\|\mathcal{I}(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})\|_{*,\delta}<\widetilde{\varepsilon}
whenever
\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta},\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}<\widetilde{\varepsilon}
. Hence
\mathcal{I}_{+}(\psi_{-},\cdot)
maps a radius
\widetilde{\varepsilon}
ball into itself.

Claim 7.5.4
.

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{I}_{+}(

\displaystyle\psi_{-},\psi_{+})-\mathcal{I}_{+}(\psi_{-},\psi_{+}^{\prime})\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}+e^{-2\lambda T}+\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\psi_{+}^{\prime}\|_{*,\delta})\cdot\|\psi_{+}-\psi^{\prime}_{+}\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle+C\|\psi_{+}-\psi_{+}^{\prime}\|_{*,\delta}\cdot(\|e_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+r^{-1/2}e^{-2(\lambda-\delta)T}+(e^{-2\delta T}+r^{-1})\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+e^{-2\lambda T}\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\psi_{+}\|^{2}_{*,\delta}).

Proof of Claim
7.5.4
.

This follows from the estimates

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}_{+}(\psi_{-,T},\psi_{+,T})-\mathcal{L}_{+}(\psi_{-,T},\psi_{+,T}^{\prime})\|_{\delta}^{+,T}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}+e^{-2\lambda T})\|\psi_{+}-\psi_{+}^{\prime}\|_{*,\delta},

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}_{+}(\psi_{+,T})-\mathcal{R}_{+}(\psi_{+,T}^{\prime})\|_{\delta}^{+,T}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\psi_{+}^{\prime}\|_{*,\delta})\|\psi_{+}-\psi_{+}^{\prime}\|_{*,\delta},

as well as the calculations from Claim
7.5.2
.
∎

Hence if
r,T\gg 0
,
\widetilde{\varepsilon}>0
is small, and
\|e_{+}\|_{*,\delta}
is small, then
\mathcal{I}_{+}(\psi_{-},\cdot)
gives a contraction mapping, provided
\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta},\|\psi_{+}^{\prime}\|_{*,\delta}\leq\widetilde{\varepsilon}
. This proves (1). We also prove the following, which is used in (3):

Claim 7.5.5
.

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{I}_{+}(\psi_{-},\psi_{+}^{\prime})

\displaystyle-\mathcal{I}_{+}(\psi_{-}^{\prime},\psi_{+}^{\prime})\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{C}(\|\psi_{+}^{\prime}\|_{*,\delta})(e^{-2\delta T}+r^{-1}+\|\psi_{+}^{\prime}\|_{*,\delta})\|\psi_{-}-\psi_{-}^{\prime}\|_{*,\delta}.

Proof of Claim
7.5.5
.

This follows from the estimate

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}_{+}(\psi_{-,T},\psi_{+,T}^{\prime})-\mathcal{L}_{+}(\psi_{-,T}^{\prime},\psi_{+,T}^{\prime})\|^{+,T}_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq C(e^{-2\delta T}+r^{-1}+\|\psi_{+}^{\prime}\|_{*,\delta})\|\psi_{-}-\psi_{-}^{\prime}\|_{*,\delta}.

as well as the calculations from Claim
7.5.2
.
∎

Now we continue the proof of Lemma
7.5.1
.

(2)
Since
\psi_{+}=P_{+}(\psi_{-})
satisfies
\mathcal{I}_{+}(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})=\psi_{+}
, Claim
7.5.2
gives:

\displaystyle\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}\leq

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}(\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta})(\|e_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+r^{-1/2}e^{-2(\lambda-\delta)T}+(e^{-2\delta T}+r^{-1})\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle+\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+e^{-2\lambda T}\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\psi_{+}\|^{2}_{*,\delta}).

By moving the last three terms on the right-hand side to the left, we obtain (2) since
\widetilde{C}(\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta})(\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}+e^{-2\lambda T}+\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta})<1
for
\widetilde{\varepsilon}>0
small and
r>0
large.

(3) Letting
\psi_{+}=P_{+}(\psi_{-})
and
\psi_{+}^{\prime}=P_{+}(\psi_{-}^{\prime})
,

\displaystyle\|P_{+}(\psi_{-})-

\displaystyle P_{+}(\psi_{-}^{\prime})\|_{*,\delta}=\|\mathcal{I}_{+}(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})-\mathcal{I}_{+}(\psi_{-}^{\prime},\psi_{+}^{\prime})\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{I}_{+}(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})-\mathcal{I}_{+}(\psi_{-},\psi^{\prime}_{+})\|_{*,\delta}+\|\mathcal{I}_{+}(\psi_{-},\psi^{\prime}_{+})-\mathcal{I}_{+}(\psi_{-}^{\prime},\psi_{+}^{\prime})\|_{*,\delta},

and the two terms on the right-hand side are bounded using Claims
7.5.4
and
7.5.5
. The terms from the right-hand side of the inequality in Claim
7.5.4
can be moved to the left.
\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}\leq\widetilde{\varepsilon}
by assumption. If
r,T\gg 0
,
\widetilde{\varepsilon}>0
is small, and
\|e_{+}\|_{*,\delta}
is small, then
\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}=\|P_{+}(\psi_{-})\|_{*,\delta}\leq\widetilde{\varepsilon}
by (2). (3) then follows.
∎

Analogously, we have the following for
P_{-}
:

Lemma 7.5.6
.

There exist
r\gg 0
and
C,\widetilde{\varepsilon}>0
such that for
T\gg 0
and
\|e_{-}\|_{*,\delta}
small the following holds:

(1)

There is a map
P_{-}:\mathcal{B}_{+}\to\mathcal{H}_{-}
such that
\Theta_{-}(P_{-}(\psi_{+}),\psi_{+}))=0
and
\psi_{-}=P_{-}(\psi_{+})\in\mathcal{H}_{-}^{\perp,\psi_{-},T}
.

(2)

\|P_{-}(\psi_{+})\|_{*,\delta}\leq C(\|e_{-}\|_{*,\delta}+r^{-1/2}e^{-2(\lambda-\delta)T}+(e^{-2\delta T}+r^{-1})\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta})
.

(3)

\|P_{-}(\psi_{+})-P_{-}(\psi^{\prime}_{+})\|_{*,\delta}\leq C\|\psi_{+}-\psi_{+}^{\prime}\|_{*,\delta}(e^{-2\delta T}+r^{-1}+\widetilde{\varepsilon})
.

Lemma 7.5.7
.

There exist
r\gg 0
,
\widetilde{\varepsilon}>0
, and
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}>0
such that for
T\gg 0
there is a unique solution
(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})\in\mathcal{B}_{-}\times\mathcal{B}_{+}
to the equations
\Theta_{+}(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})=0
and
\Theta_{-}(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})=0
subject to the constraints
\psi_{\pm}\in\mathcal{H}_{\pm}^{\perp,\psi_{\pm},T}
.

Alternatively, we can view the desired
(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})
as the unique fixed point of the map

\mathcal{I}=(\mathcal{I}_{-},\mathcal{I}_{+}):\mathcal{B}_{-}\times\mathcal{B}_{+}\to\mathcal{H}_{-}\times\mathcal{H}_{+},

(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})\mapsto(\mathcal{I}_{-}(\psi_{-},\psi_{+}),\mathcal{I}_{+}(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})),

subject to the constraints
\psi_{\pm}\in\mathcal{H}_{\pm}^{\perp,\psi_{\pm},T}
.

Proof.

We are looking for the unique solution
\psi_{-}
to
P_{-}\circ P_{+}(\psi_{-})=\psi_{-}
subject to the constraints. The existence follows from the contraction mapping principle and Lemmas
7.5.1
and
7.5.6
. Then
(\psi_{-},\psi_{+}=P_{+}(\psi_{-}))
is the unique solution to
\Theta_{\pm}(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})=0
.
∎

Remark 7.5.8
.

In more practical terms, the fixed point can be obtained by starting with

(\psi_{-}^{(0)},\psi_{+}^{(0)})=(0,0),

applying the iteration

(\psi_{-}^{(i+1)},\psi_{+}^{(i+1)})=\mathcal{I}(\psi_{-}^{(i)},\psi_{+}^{(i)}),

and taking the limit
i\to\infty
. By Claim
7.5.2
and its analog for
\mathcal{I}_{-}
,
\|\psi_{\pm}^{(1)}\|_{*,\delta}\to 0
as
T\to\infty
and similarly
\|\psi_{\pm}^{(i)}\|_{*,\delta}\to 0
as
T\to\infty
.

The above remark implies the following lemma:

Lemma 7.5.9
.

If
(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})\in\mathcal{B}_{-}\times\mathcal{B}_{+}
is the unique solution to
\Theta_{+}(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})=0
and
\Theta_{-}(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})=0
subject to the constraints
\psi_{\pm}\in\mathcal{H}_{\pm}^{\perp,\psi_{\pm},T}
, then
\|\psi_{\pm}\|_{*,\delta}\to 0
as
T\to\infty
.

We can finally define the gluing map as

G:V_{-}/\mathbb{R}\times V_{+}/\mathbb{R}\times[T_{0},\infty)\to\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}^{E^{\prime}}_{\delta}(V_{-}/\mathbb{R},V_{+}/\mathbb{R})

(u_{-},u_{+},T)\mapsto u=\exp_{u_{*}}(\beta_{+,T}\psi_{+,T}+\beta_{-,T}\psi_{-,T}),

where
(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})
is the solution from Lemma
7.5.7
.

7.6.
C^{1}
-smoothness and injectivity of the gluing map

The goal of this subsection is to prove:

Theorem 7.6.1
.

The gluing map
G
is a
C^{1}
-smooth embedding.

We start by observing that:

Lemma 7.6.2
.

The solution
(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})
from Lemma
7.5.7
is
C^{\infty}
-smooth.

Sketch of proof.

The solution
(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})
is in Morrey class
\mathcal{H}_{1,\delta}
and hence is in Sobolev class
W^{1,2}_{\delta}
and in
C^{0}
. We apply the usual elliptic bootstrapping technique, where the necessary estimates for Morrey spaces are given in
[
Mo
]
: First observe that
(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})
satisfies a nonlinear Cauchy-Riemann type operator. We can then view
(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})
as satisfying a
linear
Cauchy-Riemann type operator whose coefficients depend on
(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})
. Differentiating Equation (
7.4.18
) with respect to
s
, we also see that
(\frac{\partial\psi_{-}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\psi_{+}}{\partial s})
satisfies a linear Cauchy-Riemann type operator such that the coefficients of the first order part are in
\mathcal{H}_{1,\delta}
and the coefficients of the zeroth order part are in
\mathcal{H}_{0,\delta}
; the same holds for
\frac{\partial\psi_{\pm}}{\partial t}
. With the above conditions on the coefficients, the relevant elliptic estimates are given by the last two inequalities on
[
Mo
, p. 145]
to improve
\nabla\psi_{\pm}
from Sobolev class
W^{0,2}_{\delta}
to
W^{1,2}_{\delta}
and
[
Mo
, Theorem 5.4.1]
to improve
\nabla\psi_{\pm}
from
W^{1,2}_{\delta}
to
\mathcal{H}_{1,\delta}
.
∎

Let
\mathcal{S}_{\pm}\subset V_{\pm}/\mathbb{R}
be small neighborhoods of
u_{\pm}
in
V_{\pm}/\mathbb{R}
. Each point of
\mathcal{S}_{\pm}
is given by the pair
(\exp_{u_{\pm}}\phi_{\pm},j_{\pm}^{\prime})
, where
\phi_{\pm}=(\phi^{\circ}_{\pm},\mathfrak{a}_{\pm})\in\mathcal{H}_{\pm}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{2l^{\pm}}
,
j_{\pm}^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{\pm}
,
l^{\pm}
is the total number of punctures of
\dot{F}_{\pm}
(see Section 4.2.1), and
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{\pm}
is a Teichmüller slice containing
j_{\pm}
(the complex structure for
u_{\pm}
). We will usually write
(\phi_{\pm},j_{\pm}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{S}_{\pm}
.

By abuse of notation we write
\|\phi_{\pm}^{\circ}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{\pm}\|_{*,\delta}
or
\|\phi_{\pm}\|_{*,\delta}
for the sum of
\|\phi_{\pm}^{\circ}\|_{*,\delta}
on
\mathcal{H}_{\pm}
and the standard norm
|{\mathfrak{a}}_{\pm}|
on
\mathbb{R}^{2l^{\pm}}
.

We assume that:

(T
1
)

the slice
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{\pm}
is smooth; and

(T
2
)

there exists a small disk
D_{\pm}\subset\dot{F}_{\pm}-\mathcal{E}_{\pm,T+T_{0}}
such that all
j^{\prime}_{\pm}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{\pm}
agree on
\dot{F}_{\pm}-D_{\pm}
.

We may additionally assume that, for any
{\mathfrak{a}}
in the summand
\mathbb{R}^{2}
of
\mathbb{R}^{2l^{+}}
(resp.
\mathbb{R}^{2l^{-}}
) corresponding to the end that is glued,
{\mathfrak{a}}
is a linear combination of
\partial_{s}
and
\partial_{t}
on
s\leq 0
for
u_{+}
(resp. on
s\geq 0
for
u_{-}
).

We say that
\phi_{\pm}=(\phi_{\pm}^{\circ},{\mathfrak{a}}_{\pm})\in\mathcal{H}_{\pm}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{2l^{\pm}}
or
\phi_{\pm}^{\circ}\in\mathcal{H}_{\pm}

satisfies
(
\#_{k}
) if the following holds:

(
\#_{k}
)

\phi_{\pm}^{\circ}
is in class
C^{k}
and
\|\nabla^{i}\phi_{\pm}^{\circ}|_{-2(T+T_{0})-1<\pm s<1}\|_{\delta}
,
\forall i\leq k
, is bounded above by
C\|\phi_{\pm}^{\circ}\|_{*,\delta}
.

Here
C
is a constant that does not depend on
\phi_{\pm}^{\circ}
. Note that (
\#_{k}
) for
\phi_{\pm}^{\circ}
implies (
\#_{k}
) for
\phi_{\pm}^{\circ}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{\pm}
, since
{\mathfrak{a}}_{\pm}
lives in the finite-dimensional vector space
\mathbb{R}^{2l^{\pm}}
. In what follows we assume that
k\gg 0
.

Our gluing setup is slightly more complicated than one initially expects: this is to get around the loss of one derivative as explained in Lemma
7.6.6
. Let

\mathcal{J}=(\mathcal{J}_{-},\mathcal{J}_{+}):\mathcal{S}_{-}\times\mathcal{S}_{+}\times[T_{0},\infty)\to\mathcal{B}_{-}\times\mathcal{B}_{+}

be a
C^{1}
-smooth function such that
\operatorname{Im}(\mathcal{J})
is bounded and each
(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})\in\operatorname{Im}(\mathcal{J})
satisfies (
\#_{k}
). We then consider the function

\mathcal{I}^{\mathcal{J}}=(\mathcal{I}^{\mathcal{J}}_{-},\mathcal{I}^{\mathcal{J}}_{+}):\mathcal{S}_{-}\times\mathcal{S}_{+}\times[T_{0},\infty)\to\mathcal{H}_{-}\times\mathcal{H}_{+},

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\mathcal{J}}_{+}

\displaystyle((\phi_{-},j_{-}^{\prime}),(\phi_{+},j_{+}^{\prime}),T)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tau^{-1}_{\pm(T+T_{0})}(\Pi_{+}^{\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},j_{+}^{\prime},T}D_{+,T}^{j_{+}^{\prime}})^{-1}\Pi_{+}^{\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},j_{+}^{\prime},T}[D_{+,T}^{j_{+}^{\prime}}(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})+e_{+,T}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\mathcal{L}_{+}(\psi_{-,T}+\phi_{-,T},\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})+\mathcal{R}_{+}(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})],

where
\psi_{\pm}=\mathcal{J}_{\pm}((\phi_{-},j_{-}^{\prime}),(\phi_{+},j_{+}^{\prime}),T)
,
D_{\pm,T}^{j_{\pm}^{\prime}}
is
D_{\pm,T}
with respect to
j_{\pm}^{\prime}
,
j^{\prime}_{+}
in
\Pi_{+}^{\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},j_{+}^{\prime},T}
indicates the dependence on
j_{+}^{\prime}
, and
e_{+,T}=\overline{\partial}u_{\pm,T}
.

\mathcal{I}^{\mathcal{J}}_{-}
is defined analogously. We will later apply an iteration scheme similar to the one described in Remark
7.5.8
; see the proof of Theorem
7.6.1
at the end of this subsection.

Remark 7.6.3
.

Observe that
\mathcal{I}^{\mathcal{J}}_{+}
has the term
D_{+,T}^{j_{\pm}^{\prime}}(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})
unlike the expression for
\mathcal{I}_{+}
which does not have
D_{+,T}\psi_{+,T}
.

The expression inside the brackets
[\cdot]
can be written as

\displaystyle D_{+,T}^{j_{\pm}^{\prime}}\psi_{+,T}+e_{+,T}(\phi_{+})+\mathcal{L}_{+}(\psi_{-,T}+\phi_{-,T},\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})+\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{+}(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T}),

where
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{+}(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})=\mathcal{R}_{+}(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})-\mathcal{R}_{+}(\phi_{+,T})

and
e_{+,T}(\phi_{+}):=\overline{\partial}\exp_{u_{+,T}}(\phi_{+,T})
.

Remark 7.6.4
.

In order to avoid the (already cumbersome) notation, in the rest of the subsection we omit
j^{\prime}_{\pm}
from the notation and also will not explicitly treat variations of
j_{\pm}
in the proof of Theorem
7.6.1
. The derivatives of the gluing map with respect to
j^{\prime}_{\pm}
are straightforward to control in view of (T
1
) and (T
2
).

Lemma 7.6.5
.

Assuming
\mathcal{S}_{\pm}
are sufficiently small, all
\phi_{\pm}\in\mathcal{S}_{\pm}
satisfy
(\#_{k})
.

Proof.

This follows from the elliptic bootstrapping estimates used in the proof of Lemma
7.6.2
.
∎

Next let us write
B(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T)=(\Pi_{+}^{\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T}D_{+})^{-1}\Pi_{+}^{\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T}.

Lemma 7.6.6
.

With the above conditions on
\mathcal{J}
,
B(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T)
is sufficiently differentiable with respect to
\phi_{+}\in\mathcal{S}_{+}
and
T\in[T_{0},\infty)
.

Proof.

This uses the arguments used in Claim
7.5.3
as well as Lemma
7.6.5
. The reason for requiring (
\#_{k}
) is that when we differentiate
e_{i}(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T)
with respect to
\phi_{+}
or
T
, we lose one derivative, which must be recovered using (
\#_{k}
).
∎

Lemma 7.6.7
.

If
\mathcal{S}_{\pm}
and
\widetilde{\varepsilon}>0
are sufficiently small,
T,r\gg 0
,
\mathcal{J}
is
C^{1}
-smooth, and
\operatorname{Im}(\mathcal{J})
is bounded, then
\mathcal{I}^{\mathcal{J}}
is
C^{1}
-smooth. Moreover, if
\operatorname{Im}(\mathcal{J})
lies in a sufficiently small ball about the origin and the derivative
D\mathcal{J}
is small, then the derivative
D\mathcal{I}^{\mathcal{J}}
is small.

Proof.

We write
\psi_{\pm}=\mathcal{J}_{\pm}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)
. Suppose that
\mathcal{J}_{\pm}
is a constant function; we later explain how to modify the proof in the general case.

We first consider the partial derivative
D^{\flat}_{1}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}
, where the superscript
\flat
indicates that we are assuming that
\mathcal{J}_{\pm}
is constant. Let
\zeta_{\pm}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{\pm}\in T_{u_{\pm}}\mathcal{S}_{\pm}
, where
\zeta_{\pm}\in\mathcal{H}_{\pm}
and
{\mathfrak{a}}_{\pm}\in\mathbb{R}^{2l^{\pm}}
. We compute

(7.6.1)

\displaystyle D^{\flat}_{1}

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)(\zeta_{-}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-})

\displaystyle=\tfrac{d}{d\tau}|_{\tau=0}B(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T)(\mathcal{L}_{+}(\psi_{-,T}+\phi_{-,T}+\tau(\zeta_{-,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-,T}),\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T}))

\displaystyle=B(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T)(\tfrac{\partial\beta_{-,T}}{\partial s}(\zeta_{-,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-,T})+{\mathfrak{l}}(\eta_{+,T})\tfrac{\partial\zeta_{-,T}}{\partial t}+{\mathfrak{l}}(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})\tfrac{\partial\zeta_{-,T}}{\partial t}),

using the assumption that
{\mathfrak{a}}_{-}
is constant for
s\geq 0
.
Estimates similar to those of Claim
7.5.2
imply that

(7.6.2)

\displaystyle\|D^{\flat}_{1}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)(\zeta_{-}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-})\|_{*,\delta}\leq c(r^{-1}+\widetilde{C}(T)+\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\phi_{+}\|_{*,\delta})\cdot\|\zeta_{-}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-}\|_{*,\delta},

where
\widetilde{C}(T)\to 0
as
T\to\infty
. Hence the partial derivative
D^{\flat}_{1}
exists. We are assuming that
\|\phi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}
is sufficiently small and
T,r\gg 0
. If we assume that
\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}
in addition, then there exists
0<C\ll 1
such that

\|D^{\flat}_{1}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)(\zeta_{-}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-})\|_{*,\delta}\leq C\cdot\|\zeta_{-}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-}\|_{*,\delta}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle(D^{\flat}_{1}

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)-D^{\flat}_{1}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi_{-}^{\prime},\phi_{+}^{\prime},T^{\prime}))(\zeta_{-}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle B(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T)(\tfrac{\partial\beta_{-,T}}{\partial s}(\zeta_{-,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-,T})+{\mathfrak{l}}(\eta_{+,T})\tfrac{\partial\zeta_{-,T}}{\partial t}+{\mathfrak{l}}(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})\tfrac{\partial\zeta_{-,T}}{\partial t})

\displaystyle-B(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+}^{\prime},T^{\prime})(\tfrac{\partial\beta_{-,T^{\prime}}}{\partial s}(\zeta_{-,T^{\prime}}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-,T^{\prime}})+{\mathfrak{l}}(\eta_{+,T^{\prime}})\tfrac{\partial\zeta_{-,T^{\prime}}}{\partial t}+{\mathfrak{l}}(\psi_{+,T^{\prime}}+\phi^{\prime}_{+,T^{\prime}})\tfrac{\partial\zeta_{-,T^{\prime}}}{\partial t}),

and in view of Lemma
7.6.6
and the (
\#_{k}
)-condition applied to
\zeta_{-}
there exists a constant
C>0
such that:

\displaystyle\|((D^{\flat}_{1}

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)-D^{\flat}_{1}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi_{-}^{\prime},\phi_{+}^{\prime},T^{\prime}))(\zeta_{-}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-})\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\phi_{+}-\phi_{+}^{\prime}\|_{*}^{c}+|T-T^{\prime}|)\cdot\|\zeta_{-}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-}\|_{*,\delta}.

(Moreover,
0<C\ll 1
.) Here the superscript
c
means restriction of the function to

-(\max(T,T^{\prime})+T_{0})\leq s\leq(\max(T,T^{\prime})+T_{0}).

This proves that
D^{\flat}_{1}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}
is in
C^{0}
.

Next we compute

(7.6.3)

\displaystyle D^{\flat}_{2}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi_{-},

\displaystyle\phi_{+},T)(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+})=\tfrac{dB(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+}+\tau(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}),T)}{d\tau}|_{\tau=0}(v(\psi_{-}+\phi_{-},\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T))

\displaystyle+B(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T)(D^{\flat}_{+,T}(\zeta_{+,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T})

\displaystyle\quad+D^{\flat}_{2}\mathcal{L}_{+}(\psi_{-,T}+\phi_{-,T},\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})(\zeta_{+,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T})

\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{d}{d\tau}|_{\tau=0}e_{+,T}(\phi_{+}+\tau(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}))+D^{\flat}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{+}(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})(\zeta_{+,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T})),

where we are writing

\displaystyle v(\psi_{-}+\phi_{-},\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T)

\displaystyle=D^{\flat}_{+,T}\psi_{+,T}+e_{+,T}(\phi_{+})+\mathcal{L}_{+}(\psi_{-,T}+\phi_{-,T},\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{+}(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T}).

We compute

(7.6.4)

\displaystyle D^{\flat}_{2}\mathcal{L}_{+}(

\displaystyle\psi_{-,T}+\phi_{-,T},\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})(\zeta_{+,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T})

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{l}}(\tfrac{\partial(\psi_{-,T}+\phi_{-,T})}{\partial t})(\zeta_{+,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T})+{\mathfrak{l}}(\eta_{+,T})\tfrac{\partial\zeta_{+,T}}{\partial t},

(7.6.5)

\displaystyle D^{\flat}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{+}(\psi_{+,T}

\displaystyle+\phi_{+,T})(\zeta_{+,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{l}}(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})(\zeta_{+,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T})+{\mathfrak{l}}(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})(\nabla\zeta_{+,T})

\displaystyle+{\mathfrak{l}}(\nabla(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T}))(\zeta_{+,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T}).

Combining Equations (
7.6.3
), (
7.6.4
), and (
7.6.5
) we obtain

(7.6.6)

\displaystyle D^{\flat}_{2}

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{dB(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+}+\tau(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}),T)}{d\tau}|_{\tau=0}(v(\psi_{-}+\phi_{-},\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T))

\displaystyle+B(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T)[\tfrac{d}{d\tau}|_{\tau=0}e_{+,T}(\phi_{+}+\tau(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}))

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathfrak{l}}(\tfrac{\partial(\psi_{-,T}+\phi_{-,T})}{\partial t})(\zeta_{+,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T})+{\mathfrak{l}}(\eta_{+,T})\tfrac{\partial\zeta_{+,T}}{\partial t}+{\mathfrak{l}}(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})(\zeta_{+,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T})

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathfrak{l}}(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})(\nabla\zeta_{+,T})+{\mathfrak{l}}(\nabla(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T}))(\zeta_{+,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T})].

Let
[\cdot]
denote the expression inside the brackets. We first bound

\|\tfrac{d}{d\tau}|_{\tau=0}e_{+,T}(\phi_{+}+\tau(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}))\|_{\delta}^{+,T}\leq c\|e_{+}\|_{*,\delta}\cdot\|\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}\|_{*,\delta},

where
c>0
. The remainder of the terms can be estimated as in Claim
7.5.2
and:

(7.6.7)

\displaystyle\|[\cdot]\|_{\delta}^{+,T}

\displaystyle\leq c(\|e_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+\widetilde{C}(T)+\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\|\phi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\phi_{-}\|_{*,\delta})\cdot\|\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}\|_{*,\delta},

where
\widetilde{C}(T)\to 0
as
T\to\infty
.

We also compute

\displaystyle\tfrac{d}{d\tau}|_{\tau=0}

\displaystyle\Pi_{+}^{\psi_{+}+\phi_{+}+\tau(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}),T}(v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\langle\tfrac{d}{d\tau}|_{\tau=0}e_{i}(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+}+\tau(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}),T),v\rangle e_{i}(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i}\langle e_{i}(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T),v\rangle\tfrac{d}{d\tau}|_{\tau=0}e_{i}(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+}+\tau(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}),T),

where
\tfrac{d}{d\tau}|_{\tau=0}e_{i}(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+}+\tau(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}),T)
has terms of the form

\langle\nabla e_{i}(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T)\cdot(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}),e_{j}(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T)\rangle.

We then bound

(7.6.8)

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{d}{d\tau}|_{\tau=0}

\displaystyle\Pi_{+}^{\psi_{+}+\phi_{+}+\tau(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}),T}(v)\|_{\delta}^{T}\leq c\|v\|_{\delta}^{T}\cdot\|\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\leq c(\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\phi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\phi_{-}\|_{*,\delta})\|\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}\|_{*,\delta}.

Combining Equations (
7.6.7
) and (
7.6.8
) we obtain

(7.6.9)

\displaystyle\|D^{\flat}_{2}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+})\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\leq c(\|e_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+\widetilde{C}(T)+\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|\phi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\phi_{-}\|_{*,\delta})\cdot\|\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}\|_{*,\delta}.

Hence the partial derivative
D^{\flat}_{2}\mathcal{I}_{+}
exists. Assuming that
\|\psi_{\pm}\|_{*,\delta}
,
\|\phi_{\pm}\|_{*,\delta}
, and
\|e_{+}\|_{*,\delta}
are sufficiently small and
T\gg 0
, it follows that there exists
0<C\ll 1
such that

\displaystyle\|D^{\flat}_{2}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+})\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}\|_{*,\delta}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle(D^{\flat}_{2}

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)-D^{\flat}_{2}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi^{\prime}_{-},\phi^{\prime}_{+},T^{\prime}))(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{dB(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+}+\tau(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}),T)}{d\tau}|_{\tau=0}(v(\psi_{-}+\phi_{-},\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T))

\displaystyle-\tfrac{dB(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+}^{\prime}+\tau(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}),T^{\prime})}{d\tau}|_{\tau=0}(v(\psi_{-}+\phi_{-}^{\prime},\psi_{+}+\phi_{+}^{\prime},T^{\prime}))

\displaystyle+B(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T)({\mathfrak{l}}(\tfrac{\partial(\psi_{-,T}+\phi_{-,T})}{\partial t})(\zeta_{+,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T})+{\mathfrak{l}}(\eta_{+,T})\tfrac{\partial\zeta_{+,T}}{\partial t}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathfrak{l}}(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})(\zeta_{+,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T})+{\mathfrak{l}}(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})(\nabla\zeta_{+,T})

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathfrak{l}}(\nabla(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T}))(\zeta_{+,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T}))

\displaystyle-B(\psi_{+}+\phi^{\prime}_{+},T^{\prime})({\mathfrak{l}}(\tfrac{\partial(\psi_{-,T^{\prime}}+\phi^{\prime}_{-,T^{\prime}})}{\partial t})(\zeta_{+,T^{\prime}}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T^{\prime}})+{\mathfrak{l}}(\eta_{+,T^{\prime}})\tfrac{\partial\zeta_{+,T^{\prime}}}{\partial t}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathfrak{l}}(\psi_{+,T^{\prime}}+\phi^{\prime}_{+,T^{\prime}})(\zeta_{+,T^{\prime}}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T^{\prime}})+{\mathfrak{l}}(\psi_{+,T^{\prime}}+\phi_{+,T^{\prime}}^{\prime})(\nabla\zeta_{+,T^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathfrak{l}}(\nabla(\psi_{+,T^{\prime}}+\phi^{\prime}_{+,T^{\prime}}))(\zeta_{+,T^{\prime}}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T^{\prime}})).

We have bounds

\displaystyle\|{\mathfrak{l}}(\tfrac{\partial(\psi_{-,T}+\phi_{-,T})}{\partial t})

\displaystyle(\zeta_{+,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T})-{\mathfrak{l}}(\tfrac{\partial(\psi_{-,T^{\prime}}+\phi^{\prime}_{-,T^{\prime}})}{\partial t})(\zeta_{+,T^{\prime}}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T^{\prime}})\|_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\phi_{-}-\phi_{-}^{\prime}\|_{*,\delta}^{c}+|T-T^{\prime}|)\cdot\|\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}\|_{*,\delta},

\displaystyle\|{\mathfrak{l}}(\eta_{+,T})\tfrac{\partial\zeta_{+,T}}{\partial t}-{\mathfrak{l}}(\eta_{+,T^{\prime}})\tfrac{\partial\zeta_{+,T^{\prime}}}{\partial t}\|_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq C|T-T^{\prime}|\cdot\|\zeta_{+}\|_{*,\delta},

\displaystyle\|{\mathfrak{l}}(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})

\displaystyle(\zeta_{+,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T})-{\mathfrak{l}}(\psi_{+,T^{\prime}}+\phi^{\prime}_{+,T^{\prime}})(\zeta_{+,T^{\prime}}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T^{\prime}})\|_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\phi_{+}-\phi_{+}^{\prime}\|_{*,\delta}^{c}+|T-T^{\prime}|)\cdot\|\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}\|_{*,\delta},

\displaystyle\|{\mathfrak{l}}(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T})

\displaystyle(\nabla\zeta_{+,T})-{\mathfrak{l}}(\psi_{+,T^{\prime}}+\phi_{+,T^{\prime}}^{\prime})(\nabla\zeta_{+,T^{\prime}})\|_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\phi_{+}-\phi_{+}^{\prime}\|_{*,\delta}^{c}+|T-T^{\prime}|)\cdot\|\zeta_{+}\|_{*,\delta},

\displaystyle\|{\mathfrak{l}}(\nabla(\psi_{+,T}+\phi_{+,T}))

\displaystyle(\zeta_{+,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T})-{\mathfrak{l}}(\nabla(\psi_{+,T^{\prime}}+\phi^{\prime}_{+,T^{\prime}}))(\zeta_{+,T^{\prime}}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+,T^{\prime}}))\|_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\phi_{+}-\phi_{+}^{\prime}\|_{*,\delta}^{c}+|T-T^{\prime}|)\cdot\|\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}\|_{*,\delta}.

Together with the bounds for

B(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T)-B(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T^{\prime})~\mbox{ and }~\tfrac{dB(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+}+\tau(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}),T)}{d\tau}|_{\tau=0},

we obtain

\displaystyle\|(D^{\flat}_{2}

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)-D^{\flat}_{2}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi^{\prime}_{-},\phi^{\prime}_{+},T^{\prime}))(\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+})\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\phi_{+}-\phi_{+}^{\prime}\|_{*,\delta}^{c}+\|\phi_{-}-\phi_{-}^{\prime}\|_{*,\delta}^{c}+|T-T^{\prime}|)\cdot\|\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}\|_{*,\delta},

where
C>0
. Hence
D^{\flat}_{2}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}
is in
C^{0}
.

Next consider

(7.6.10)

\displaystyle D^{\flat}_{3}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)(\mathfrak{t})=

\displaystyle\tfrac{d}{d\tau}|_{\tau=0}B(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T+\tau{\mathfrak{t}})(v(\psi_{-}+\phi_{-},\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T))

\displaystyle+B(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T)\tfrac{d}{d\tau}|_{\tau=0}v(\psi_{-}+\phi_{-},\psi_{+}+\phi_{+},T+\tau{\mathfrak{t}}).

The bound

(7.6.11)

\displaystyle\|D^{\flat}_{3}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)(\mathfrak{t})\|_{*,\delta}\leq c(\|\psi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\psi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\phi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\phi_{-}\|_{*,\delta})\cdot|{\mathfrak{t}}|,

follows from applying Lemma
7.6.6
to the first line and (
\#_{k}
) to the second. Its differentiability also follows in a similar manner and is left to the reader.

Consider the general case where
\mathcal{J}_{\pm}
. One can then verify that

(7.6.12)

\displaystyle D_{1}

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)(\zeta_{-}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-})-D_{1}^{\flat}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)(\zeta_{-}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-})

\displaystyle=D_{1}^{\flat}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)\circ D\mathcal{J}_{-}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)(\zeta_{-}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-}).

The second row of Equation (
7.6.12
) can be bounded above by
C\cdot\|\zeta_{-}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-}\|_{*,\delta}
with
0<C\ll 1
if
\|\psi_{\pm}\|_{*,\delta}
and
D\mathcal{J}
are sufficiently small. The situation for
D_{2}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}
and
D_{3}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}
are similar.

This completes the proof that
\mathcal{I}_{+}^{\mathcal{J}}
and hence
\mathcal{I}^{\mathcal{J}}
are in
C^{1}
. Moreover, if
\operatorname{Im}(\mathcal{J})
lies in a sufficiently small ball about the origin and the derivative
D\mathcal{J}
is small, then
D\mathcal{I}^{\mathcal{J}}
is small.
∎

We are now in a position to prove Theorem
7.6.1
.

Proof of Theorem
7.6.1
.

C^{1}
-smoothness.
Recall that we are assuming that
\mathcal{S}_{\pm}
are sufficiently small neighborhoods of
u_{\pm}
. By Lemmas
7.5.1
–
7.5.7
we see that the unique solution
(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})
to
\Theta_{\pm}(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})=0
for
(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)\in\mathcal{S}_{-}\times\mathcal{S}_{+}\times[T_{0},\infty)
is obtained as follows: Let
\mathcal{J}_{0}=0
,
\mathcal{J}_{1}=\mathcal{I}^{\mathcal{J}_{0}}
, and
\mathcal{J}_{i}=\mathcal{I}^{\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}\mathcal{J}_{j}}
. Also let us write
(\mathcal{J}_{i})_{\pm}
as the
\mathcal{H}_{\pm}
component of
\mathcal{J}_{i}
. Then

(7.6.13)

(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{J}_{j}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T),

and

(7.6.14)

G(\psi_{-},\psi_{+},T):=\exp_{u_{*}}(\beta_{-,T}(\phi_{-,T}+\psi_{-,T})+\beta_{+,T}(\phi_{+,T}+\psi_{+,T})).

Each
\mathcal{J}_{j}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)
is in
C^{1}
by Lemma
7.6.7
and it remains to show a bound of type
\|D\mathcal{J}_{i}\|\leq C^{i}
, where
0<C\ll 1
.

We will check the easiest case
\mathcal{J}_{2}=\mathcal{I}^{\mathcal{J}_{1}}
, leaving the higher
\mathcal{J}_{i}
to the reader. Let
\psi^{1}_{\pm}=(\mathcal{J}_{1})_{\pm}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)
. Then

(7.6.15)

\displaystyle\Pi_{+}^{\phi_{+},T}D_{+,T}(\psi_{+,T}^{1})=-\Pi_{+}^{\phi_{+},T}(

\displaystyle D_{+,T}(\phi_{+,T})+e_{+,T}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{L}_{+}(\phi_{-,T},\phi_{+,T})+\mathcal{R}_{+}(\phi_{+,T})).

Using Equation (
7.6.15
) we can write:

(7.6.16)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{J}_{2})_{\pm}

\displaystyle(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\Pi_{+}^{\psi_{+}^{1}+\phi_{+},T}D_{+,T})^{-1}(\Pi_{+}^{\psi_{+}^{1}+\phi_{+},T}-\Pi_{+}^{\phi_{+},T})(D_{+,T}(\psi_{+,T}^{1}+\phi_{+,T})+e_{+,T}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\mathcal{L}_{+}(\psi_{-,T}^{1}+\phi_{-,T},\psi_{+,T}^{1}+\phi_{+,T})+\mathcal{R}_{+}(\psi_{+,T}^{1}+\phi_{+,T}))

\displaystyle+(\Pi_{+}^{\psi_{+}^{1}+\phi_{+},T}D_{+,T})^{-1}\Pi_{+}^{\phi_{+},T}(\mathcal{L}_{+}(\psi_{-,T}^{1}+\phi_{-,T},\psi_{+,T}^{1}+\phi_{+,T})-\mathcal{L}_{+}(\phi_{-,T},\phi_{+,T})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\mathcal{R}_{+}(\psi_{+,T}^{1}+\phi_{+,T})-\mathcal{R}_{+}(\phi_{+,T})).

Calculations similar to those of Lemma
7.6.7
imply that:

\displaystyle\|D\mathcal{J}_{2}

\displaystyle(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)(\zeta_{-}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-},\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+},{\mathfrak{t}})\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\leq c(\|\psi_{-}^{1}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\psi_{+}^{1}\|_{*,\delta})(\|\zeta_{-}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+|{\mathfrak{t}}|).

[Sketch of calculation: The derivative of Equation (
7.6.16
) consists of six terms since Equation (
7.6.16
) is the sum of two terms, each of which is a product of three terms. The six terms can all be estimated in the same manner and we calculate some representative terms. We estimate

(7.6.17)

\displaystyle\tfrac{d}{d\tau}|_{\tau=0}(\mathcal{L}_{+}(\psi_{-,T}^{1}+\phi_{-,T}+

\displaystyle\tau\zeta_{-,T},\psi_{+,T}^{1}+\phi_{+,T}+\tau\zeta_{+,T})

\displaystyle-\mathcal{L}_{+}(\phi_{-,T}+\tau\zeta_{-,T},\phi_{+,T}+\tau\zeta_{+,T}))

as follows. Observe that
\mathcal{L}_{+}
is bilinear in each variable. Hence we have an expression of the form

\displaystyle(\psi_{-}+\phi_{-}+\tau\zeta_{-})(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+}+\tau\zeta_{+})-(\phi_{-}+\tau\zeta_{-})(\phi_{+}+\tau\zeta_{+})

\displaystyle=(\psi_{-}+\phi_{-})(\psi_{+}+\phi_{+})-\phi_{-}\phi_{+}+\tau(\zeta_{-}\psi_{+}+\zeta_{+}\psi_{-})+\mbox{h.o.}

Hence Equation (
7.6.17
) is bounded above by

c(\|\psi_{-}^{1}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\psi_{+}^{1}\|_{*,\delta})(\|\zeta_{-}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\zeta_{+}\|_{*,\delta}).

Similarly,

(7.6.18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{+}(\psi_{-,T}^{1}+\phi_{-,T},\psi_{+,T}^{1}+\phi_{+,T})-\mathcal{L}_{+}(\phi_{-,T},\phi_{+,T})

is bounded above by

c(\|\psi_{-}^{1}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\psi_{+}^{1}\|_{*,\delta})(\|\phi_{-}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\phi_{+}\|_{*,\delta}).

This concludes the sketch.]
We also calculate:

\displaystyle\|(D\mathcal{J}_{2}

\displaystyle(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)-D\mathcal{J}_{2}(\phi_{-}^{\prime},\phi_{+}^{\prime},T^{\prime}))(\zeta_{-}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-},\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+},{\mathfrak{t}})\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\leq c(\|\psi_{-}^{1}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\psi_{+}^{1}\|_{*,\delta})(\|\phi_{+}-\phi^{\prime}_{+}\|^{c}_{*}+\|\phi_{-}-\phi_{-}^{\prime}\|^{c}_{*}+|T-T^{\prime}|)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot(\|\zeta_{-}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-}\|_{*,\delta}+\|\zeta_{+}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{+}\|_{*,\delta}+|{\mathfrak{t}}|).

This completes the proof of Theorem
7.6.1
.

Local embedding.
It suffices to show that
G
is a local
C^{1}
-embedding, i.e.,
DG
is an isomorphism at any
(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)
, provided
T_{0}\gg 0
. (It is not hard to see that, for any
d>0
, there exists
T_{0}\gg 0
such that if
(\phi_{-},\phi_{-},T)
and
(\phi_{-}^{\prime},\phi_{+}^{\prime},T^{\prime})
are a distance
d
apart, then
G(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)
and
G(\phi_{-}^{\prime},\phi_{+}^{\prime},T^{\prime})
cannot be equal.)

First consider the map

H:(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)\mapsto(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}(\mathcal{J}_{j}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T),0).

Since its leading term is the identity map and the subsequent terms
\mathcal{J}_{j}
have derivatives bounded by a constant
C^{j}
with
0<C\ll 1
, it follows that
DH
is invertible at any
(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)
, provided
\widetilde{\varepsilon}>0
is sufficiently small and
T_{0}>0
is sufficiently large.

Next, instead of
G
we consider the map

\widetilde{G}:(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)\mapsto\beta_{-,T}(\phi_{-,T}+\psi_{-,T})+\beta_{+,T}(\phi_{+,T}+\psi_{+,T})

without the
\exp
. If
T_{0}\gg 0
, then from the considerations of the previous paragraph
D\widetilde{G}(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)|_{T_{\phi_{-}}\mathcal{S}_{-}\times T_{T}[T_{0},\infty)}
is very close to

(\zeta_{-}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-},{\mathfrak{t}})\mapsto\zeta_{-,T}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{-,T}-{\mathfrak{t}}\tfrac{\partial}{\partial s}

when the right-hand side is restricted to
s\leq-(T+T_{0})
. Since
T_{\phi_{-}}\mathcal{S}_{-}
and
\tfrac{\partial}{\partial s}
are independent, we see that
D\widetilde{G}
is injective, which in turn implies that
G
is a local
C^{1}
-embedding.
∎

7.7.
Surjectivity of the gluing map

Theorem 7.7.1
.

Given compact subsets
\mathcal{K}_{\pm}\subset\mathcal{S}_{\pm}
, there exist
T_{0}\gg 0
and
\widetilde{\delta}>0
such that, for all curves
u_{0}
that are
\widetilde{\delta}
-close to breaking into
(u^{\prime}_{-},u^{\prime}_{+},T^{\prime})\in\mathcal{K}_{-}\times\mathcal{K}_{+}\times[2T_{0},\infty)
, there exists a triple
(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)\in\mathcal{S}_{-}\times\mathcal{S}_{+}\times[T_{0},\infty)
such that
G(\phi_{-},\phi_{+},T)=u_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
T_{0}>0
be sufficiently large and
\widetilde{\delta}>0
be sufficiently small. Let
u_{0}
be
\widetilde{\delta}
-close to breaking into
(u^{\prime}_{-},u^{\prime}_{+},T^{\prime})
. After possibly translating
u_{0}
in the
\mathbb{R}
-direction and slightly modifying
T^{\prime}
, there exists a decomposition of the domain
(\dot{F}_{0},j_{0})
of
u_{0}
as:

\dot{F}_{0}=(F_{0})^{\circ}_{+}\cup(F_{0})^{\circ}_{-}\cup A_{[-T^{\prime},T^{\prime}]},

where

•

\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon}
is the
\varepsilon
-thin annular part of
\dot{F}_{0}
(with respect to the complete finite-volume hyperbolic metric compatible with
j_{0}
) corresponding to the neck that is being stretched;

•

A_{[-T^{\prime},T^{\prime}]}=u_{0}^{-1}(\{-T^{\prime}\leq s\leq T^{\prime}\})\cap\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon}
,
\operatorname{int}((F_{0})^{\circ}_{+})\cup\operatorname{int}((F_{0})^{\circ}_{-})=\dot{F}_{0}-A_{[-T^{\prime},T^{\prime}]}
, and
(F_{0})^{\circ}_{+}
,
(F_{0})^{\circ}_{-}
correspond to the top and bottom levels;

•

writing
\partial\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon}=C_{+}\sqcup C_{-}
corresponding to the top and bottom levels,
s_{C_{+}}(u_{0})
and
s_{C_{-}}(u_{0})
are as defined in Section
6.4
and we assume that
s_{C\pm}(u_{0})=\pm(T^{\prime}+T_{0})
.

Next consider

(\dot{F}^{\prime}_{+},j^{\prime}_{+}):=((F_{0})^{\circ}_{+},j_{0})\cup(A_{(-\infty,T^{\prime}]},j_{std}),

(\dot{F}^{\prime}_{-},j^{\prime}_{-}):=((F_{0})^{\circ}_{-},j_{0})\cup(A_{[-T^{\prime},\infty)},j_{std}),

such that
\dot{F}^{\prime}_{\pm}
extends
(F_{0})^{\circ}_{\pm}\cup A_{[-T^{\prime},T^{\prime}]}
.
For
\widetilde{\delta}>0
small and
T_{0}\gg 0
,
j^{\prime}_{\pm}
is close to the domain complex structure of
u^{\prime}_{\pm}
. We may replace
u^{\prime}_{\pm}
by
u^{\prime\prime}_{\pm}\in\mathcal{S}_{\pm}
with domain
(\dot{F}^{\prime}_{\pm},j^{\prime}_{\pm})
which is close to
u^{\prime}_{\pm}
and such that
u_{0}
is
\widetilde{\delta}
-close to breaking into
(u^{\prime\prime}_{-},u^{\prime\prime}_{+},T^{\prime})
, after possibly slightly enlarging
\widetilde{\delta}
.

Let
u^{(1)}_{*}
be the pregluing of
u^{(1)}_{-}=u^{\prime\prime}_{-}
and
u^{(1)}_{+}=u^{\prime\prime}_{+}
with gluing parameter
T^{(1)}=T^{\prime}
, as defined in Section
7.1
, and let

\dot{F}^{(1)}=(F^{(1)})^{\circ}_{+}\cup(F^{(1)})^{\circ}_{-}\cup A_{[-T^{(1)},T^{(1)}]}

be the domain of
u^{(1)}_{*}
, defined analogously.

We first solve for
\psi_{\pm}^{(1)}
in

(7.7.1)

\displaystyle\Phi\overline{\partial}\exp_{u^{(1)}_{*}}(\beta_{-,T^{(1)}}\psi_{-,T^{(1)}}^{(1)}+\beta_{+,T^{(1)}}\psi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)})\in\Phi E^{\prime}_{u_{0}},

as in Section
7.5
, where
\Phi
is the parallel transport to
\wedge^{0,1}(u^{(1)}_{*})^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M)
.
Let us write
u^{(1)}=\exp_{u^{(1)}_{*}}(\beta_{-,T^{(1)}}\psi^{(1)}_{-,T^{(1)}}+\beta_{+,T^{(1)}}\psi^{(1)}_{+,T^{(1)}})
. Here the superscript
(1)
indicates that we are in the first round of an iterative scheme. Note that
\|\psi_{\pm}^{(1)}\|_{*,\delta}\to 0
as
T_{0}\to\infty
by Lemma
7.5.9
.

Next suppose
\phi^{(1)}
satisfies

u_{0}=\exp_{u^{(1)}_{*}}(\beta_{-,T^{(1)}}\psi_{-,T^{(1)}}^{(1)}+\beta_{+,T^{(1)}}\psi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)}+\phi^{(1)}).

We decompose
\phi^{(1)}=\phi_{-,T^{(1)}}^{(1)}+\phi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)}
, such that

•

\phi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)}=\phi^{(1)}
on
(F^{\prime})^{\circ}_{+}
,
\phi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)}=0
on
(F^{\prime})^{\circ}_{-}
, and

\phi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)}(s,t)=\phi^{(1)}(s,t)\beta(\tfrac{s+hr}{2hr})

on
A_{[-T^{(1)},T^{(1)}]}
;

•

\phi_{-,T^{(1)}}^{(1)}=0
for
(F^{\prime})^{\circ}_{+}
,
\phi_{-,T^{(1)}}^{(1)}=\phi
on
(F^{\prime})^{\circ}_{-}
, and

\phi_{-,T^{(1)}}^{(1)}(s,t)=\phi^{(1)}(s,t)(1-\beta(\tfrac{s+hr}{2hr}))

on
A_{[-T^{(1)},T^{(1)}]}
.

Here
\phi_{\pm}^{(1)}\in\mathcal{H}_{\pm}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{2l^{\pm}}
and we are writing
\|\phi_{\pm}^{(1)}\|_{*,\delta}
for the sum of
\|\cdot\|_{*,\delta}
on
\mathcal{H}_{\pm}
and the standard norm on
\mathbb{R}^{2l^{\pm}}
as before.

We then solve for
(\phi_{\pm}^{\prime})^{(1)}
in

\overline{\partial}\exp_{u_{\pm}^{(1)}}(\phi_{\pm}^{(1)}+(\phi_{\pm}^{\prime})^{(1)})\in\Phi_{\pm}^{\phi_{\pm}^{(1)}+(\phi_{\pm}^{\prime})^{(1)}}E_{\phi_{\pm}^{(1)}+(\phi^{\prime}_{\pm})^{(1)}},

where
(\phi_{\pm}^{\prime})^{(1)}\in\mathcal{H}_{\pm}
,
\Phi_{\pm}^{\phi_{\pm}^{(1)}+(\phi_{\pm}^{\prime})^{(1)}}
is the parallel transport

\wedge^{0,1}(\exp_{u_{\pm}^{(1)}}(\phi_{\pm}^{(1)}+(\phi_{\pm}^{\prime})^{(1)}))^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M)\xrightarrow{\sim}\wedge^{0,1}(u_{\pm}^{(1)})^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times M)

and
E_{\phi_{\pm}^{(1)}+(\phi^{\prime}_{\pm})^{(1)}}
is the obstruction bundle for
\exp_{u_{\pm}^{(1)}}(\phi_{\pm}^{(1)}+(\phi_{\pm}^{\prime})^{(1)})
.

Claim 7.7.2
.

\|(\phi_{\pm}^{\prime})^{(1)}\|_{*,\delta}\leq C\|\phi_{\pm}^{(1)}\|_{*,\delta}
, where
0<C\ll 1
.

Proof of Claim
7.7.2
.

This is proved using the contraction mapping theorem as in Section
7.5
and relies on the fact that
\overline{\partial}\exp_{u_{\pm}^{(1)}}(\phi_{\pm}^{(1)})
is close to an element in
\Phi_{\pm}^{\phi_{\pm}^{(1)}}E_{\phi_{\pm}^{(1)}}
. We will indicate a proof of this fact under the simplified assumption that
E_{\phi_{\pm}^{(1)}}=0
; the general case is only more complicated in notation.

First observe that
\overline{\partial}\exp_{u_{+}^{(1)}}(\phi_{+}^{(1)})|_{s\geq-T_{0}}
is the
s=-(T^{(1)}+T_{0})
translate of
\overline{\partial}\exp_{u_{*}^{(1)}}(\phi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)})|_{s\geq T^{(1)}}
and that

(7.7.2)

\overline{\partial}\exp_{u_{*}^{(1)}}(\phi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)}+\psi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)})=0,\quad\overline{\partial}\exp_{u_{*}^{(1)}}(\psi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)})=0,\quad\overline{\partial}\exp_{u_{*}^{(1)}}(0)=0.

We claim that

(7.7.3)

\|\overline{\partial}\exp_{u_{*}^{(1)}}(\phi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)})|_{s\geq-T_{0}}\|_{\delta}^{+,T^{(1)}}\leq c\|\psi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)}\|_{*,\delta}^{+,T^{(1)}}\cdot\|\phi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)}\|_{*,\delta}^{+,T^{(1)}}

for some constant
c>0
. We write
u,\psi,\phi
for
u_{*}^{(1)},\psi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)},\phi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)}
and use local coordinates
(\sigma,\tau)
for the domain. Assume that

(
\dagger
)

\exp_{u}(\phi)=u+\phi
,
\exp_{u}(\psi)=u+\psi
,
\exp_{u}(\phi+\psi)=u+\phi+\psi
,

e.g., the target metric is flat.
Using Equation (
7.7.2
) and writing
v=u+\psi
we obtain:

(7.7.4)

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}(u+\phi)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\partial\phi}{\partial\sigma}+J(u)\tfrac{\partial\phi}{\partial\tau}+\nabla J(u)(\phi)\tfrac{\partial(u+\phi)}{\partial\tau}+Q(\phi)\tfrac{\partial(u+\phi)}{\partial\tau},

(7.7.5)

\displaystyle 0=\overline{\partial}(v+\phi)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\partial\phi}{\partial\sigma}+J(v)\tfrac{\partial\phi}{\partial\tau}+\nabla J(v)(\phi)\tfrac{\partial(v+\phi)}{\partial\tau}+Q(\phi)\tfrac{\partial(v+\phi)}{\partial\tau},

where we are writing
J(u+\phi)=J(u)+\nabla J(u)(\phi)+Q(\phi)
and
Q(\phi)
is a quadratic term. Taking the difference between the two, we can locally bound

\|\overline{\partial}(u+\phi)\|
by terms of the form
c\|\psi\|_{*}\|\phi\|_{*}
. In general, when (
\dagger
) does not hold, we may take
\exp_{u}(\phi+\psi)=u+\phi+\psi+Q(\phi,\psi)
, where
Q(\phi,\psi)
is a pointwise function of
\phi
and
\psi
times a pointwise bilinear function of
\phi
and
\psi
, and we obtain the local bound
\|\overline{\partial}(u+\phi)\|\leq c\|\psi\|_{*}\|\phi\|_{*}
. The standard exponential decay estimates then yield Equation (
7.7.3
).

Next we bound
\overline{\partial}\exp_{u_{+}^{(1)}}(\phi_{+}^{(1)})
on
-(T^{(1)}+T_{0})-hr\leq s\leq-T_{0}
, which is the
s=-(T^{(1)}+T_{0})
translate of
\overline{\partial}\exp_{u_{*}^{(1)}}(\phi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)})|_{-hr\leq s\leq T^{(1)}}
. Applying the same procedure as above
11
11

11

Note that we are dealing with a flat metric here, so (
\dagger
) holds.
with

u=u_{*}^{(1)},\quad v=u_{*}^{(1)}+\beta_{-,T^{(1)}}\psi_{-,T^{(1)}}^{(1)}+\beta_{+,T^{(1)}}\psi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)},

\psi=\beta_{-,T^{(1)}}\psi_{-,T^{(1)}}^{(1)}+\beta_{+,T^{(1)}}\psi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)},\quad\phi=\phi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)}

as well as exponential bounds on annuli from
[
HT2
, Lemma 2.3]
we obtain

(7.7.6)

\|\overline{\partial}\exp_{u_{+}^{(1)}}(\phi_{+}^{(1)})|_{-(T^{(1)}+T_{0})-hr\leq s\leq-T_{0}}\|_{\delta}^{+,T^{(1)}}\leq C(T^{(1)})\cdot\|\phi_{+,T^{(1)}}^{(1)}\|_{*,\delta}^{+,T^{(1)}},

where
C(T^{(1)})\to 0
as
T^{(1)}\to\infty
. Note that
\phi_{+}^{(1)}|_{s\leq-(T^{(1)}+T_{0})-hr}=0
by definition.

We then invert the error term
\overline{\partial}\exp_{u_{+}^{(1)}}(\phi_{+}^{(1)})
using Estimates (
7.7.3
) and (
7.7.6
) and the contraction mapping theorem. This proves the claim.
∎

Also observe that
\|\phi_{\pm}^{(1)}\|_{*,\delta}
is bounded above by a fixed constant times
\widetilde{\delta}
; this follows from elliptic bootstrapping as in Lemma
7.6.2
.

We iterate the above procedure with
(u^{(1)}_{-},u^{(1)}_{+},T^{(1)})
replaced by

(u^{(2)}_{-},u^{(2)}_{+},T^{(2)})=(\exp_{u^{(1)}_{-}}^{\circ}(\phi_{-}^{(1)}+(\phi_{-}^{\prime})^{(1)}),\exp_{u^{(1)}_{+}}^{\circ}(\phi_{+}^{(1)}+(\phi_{+}^{\prime})^{(1)}),T^{(1)}+T_{-}+T_{+}).

Here
\exp_{u^{(1)}_{\pm}}^{\circ}(\phi_{\pm}^{(1)}+(\phi_{\pm}^{\prime})^{(1)})
is
\exp_{u^{(1)}_{\pm}}(\phi_{\pm}^{(1)}+(\phi_{\pm}^{\prime})^{(1)})
normalized by shifting
T_{\pm}
units in the
\pm s
-direction; the amount that we shift is determined by the choice of slice (i.e., the choice of representative
\exp_{u^{(1)}_{\pm}}^{\circ}(\phi_{\pm}^{(1)}+(\phi_{\pm}^{\prime})^{(1)})
of
V_{\pm}/\mathbb{R}
). Let
u_{*}^{(2)}
be the resulting pregluing.

We can similarly verify that, if we replace
u_{*}^{(1)}
and
\psi_{\pm}^{(1)}
by
u_{*}^{(2)}
and
\psi_{\pm}^{(1)}+\psi_{\pm}^{(2)}
in Equation (
7.7.1
), then:

(7.7.7)

\|\psi_{\pm}^{(2)}\|_{*,\delta}\leq C\|\phi_{\pm}^{(1)}\|_{*,\delta},

where
0<C\ll 1
.
The bounds from Claim
7.7.2
and Estimate (
7.7.7
) imply that the error between
u_{0}
and
u^{(i)}
is of order
C^{i}
after the
i
th iteration. The proof then follows.
∎

This concludes the proof of Theorem
6.5.1
(A).

7.8.
Sketch of proof of Theorem
6.5.1
(B)

Theorem
6.5.1
(B) is a consequence of the following observations: By Section
7.1
, the pregluing only depends on the constants
h,r,T_{0}
, the cutoff function
\beta
, and the values of
s_{\pm,i}(\mathcal{F}_{j},u_{j})
, where
\{(\mathcal{F}_{j},u_{j})~|~j=1,\dots,m\}
, is the collection of curves we are trying to glue. In the gluing setup from Section
7.4
,
\Phi
only depends on the Riemannian metric on
\mathbb{R}\times M
;
e_{\pm,\tau}
is simply
\overline{\partial}u_{\pm T}
; and
\mathcal{L}_{\pm}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\pm}
are given by formulas Equation (
7.4
) and (
7.4.17
) and hence do not depend on the choice of compact subset
K_{j}^{\prime}
. Finally, in Section
7.5
, the definitions of
\mathcal{H}_{\pm}^{\perp,\psi_{\pm},T}
,
\Pi_{\pm}^{\psi_{\pm},T}
,
(\Pi_{\pm}^{\psi_{\pm},T}D_{\pm,T})^{-1}
, and hence of
\mathcal{I}_{\pm}
also do not depend on the choice of
K_{j}^{\prime}
.

7.9.
Sketch of proof of Theorem
6.5.3

In this subsection we sketch the proof of Theorem
6.5.3
. Without loss of generality assume that
m=3
. We are comparing
G_{(1,2,3)}
and
G_{((1,2),3)}\circ(G_{(1,2)},\operatorname{id})
. We are gluing
u_{1}\cup u_{2}\cup u_{3}
, where
(\mathcal{F}_{i},u_{i})
,
i=1,2,3
, is a representative of
V_{i}/\mathbb{R}
. For ease of notation we assume that
u_{2}
and
u_{3}
only have one negative end.

7.9.1.
Description of
G_{(1,2,3)}

Let
u_{*}^{(123)}
be the pregluing of
u_{1},u_{2},u_{3}
with gluing parameters
T_{12},T_{23}
(see Definition
7.1.2
). We solve for
\psi_{1}^{(123)},\psi_{2}^{(123)},\psi_{3}^{(123)}
which are analogs of
\psi_{-},\psi_{+}
in the equations

\Theta_{1}^{(123)}(\psi_{1}^{(123)},\psi_{2}^{(123)})=0,

\Theta_{2}^{(123)}(\psi_{1}^{(123)},\psi_{2}^{(123)},\psi_{3}^{(123)})=0,

\Theta_{3}^{(123)}(\psi_{2}^{(123)},\psi_{3}^{(123)})=0,

which are analogs of
\Theta_{\pm}(\psi_{-},\psi_{+})=0
. This yields

u_{(123)}=G_{(1,2,3)}(u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},T_{12},T_{23}).

We also write
\psi_{i}^{(123),\dagger}
,
i=1,2,3
, for the first approximation of
\psi_{i}^{(123)}
when applying the contraction mapping principle; this corresponds to
\mathcal{J}_{1}
in the proof of Theorem
7.6.1
and is obtained by using the linearized
\overline{\partial}
-operator to invert the errors that arise from the pregluing of
u_{1},u_{2},u_{3}
. Estimates similar to those of Sections
7.5
imply the existence of functions
\widetilde{C}(T)
and
\widetilde{D}(T)
,
T:=\min(T_{12},T_{23})
, such that

(7.9.1)

\displaystyle\|\psi_{i}^{(123)}-\psi_{i}^{(123),\dagger}\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{C}(T)\|\psi_{i}^{(123),\dagger}\|_{*,\delta},

\displaystyle\|\psi_{i}^{(123),\dagger}\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{D}(T),

and
\widetilde{C}(T),\widetilde{D}(T)\to 0
as
T\to\infty
.

7.9.2.
Description of
G_{((1,2),3)}\circ(G_{(1,2)},\operatorname{id})

Let
u_{*}^{(12)}
be the pregluing of
u_{1},u_{2}
with gluing parameter
T_{12}
. We solve for
\psi_{1}^{(12)},\psi_{2}^{(12)}
in

\Theta_{1}^{(12)}(\psi_{1}^{(12)},\psi_{2}^{(12)})=0,\quad\Theta_{2}^{(12)}(\psi_{1}^{(12)},\psi_{2}^{(12)})=0,

which yields
u_{(12)}=G_{(1,2)}(u_{1},u_{2},T_{12})
. Similarly we define
\psi_{i}^{(12),\dagger}
,
i=1,2
. We have

(7.9.2)

\displaystyle\|\psi_{i}^{(12)}-\psi_{i}^{(12),\dagger}\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{C}(T_{12})\|\psi_{i}^{(12),\dagger}\|_{*,\delta},

\displaystyle\|\psi_{i}^{(12),\dagger}\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{D}(T_{12}),

where
\widetilde{C}(T_{12}),\widetilde{D}(T_{12})\to 0
as
T_{12}\to\infty
.

Next let
u_{*}^{((12)3)}
be the pregluing of
u_{(12)},u_{3}
with gluing parameter
T_{23}
. We solve for
\psi_{(12)}^{((12)3)},\psi_{3}^{((12)3)}
in

\Theta_{(12)}^{((12)3)}(\psi_{(12)}^{((12)3)},\psi_{3}^{((12)3)})=0,\quad\Theta_{3}^{((12)3)}(\psi_{(12)}^{((12)3)},\psi_{3}^{((12)3)})=0,

which yields

u_{((12)3)}=G_{((1,2),3)}(u_{(12)},u_{3},T_{23}).

Similarly we define
\psi_{(12)}^{((12)3),\dagger},\psi_{3}^{((12)3),\dagger}
. For
i=(12)
and
i=3
we have

(7.9.3)

\displaystyle\|\psi_{i}^{((12)3)}-\psi_{i}^{((12)3),\dagger}\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{C}(T)\|\psi_{i}^{((12)3),\dagger}\|_{*,\delta},

\displaystyle\|\psi_{i}^{((12)3),\dagger}\|_{*,\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{D}(T),

where
\widetilde{C}(T),\widetilde{D}(T)\to 0
as
T\to\infty
.

7.9.3.
Conclusion

The
C^{0}
-closeness follows from Estimates (
7.9.1
), (
7.9.2
), and (
7.9.3
). Roughly speaking, the errors from the pregluing go to zero as
T_{12},T_{23}\to\infty
. The
C^{1}
-closeness is left to the reader and follows from Estimates (
7.6.2
), (
7.6.9
), and (
7.6.11
) from Section
7.6
. In words, the derivatives of the errors from the pregluing go to zero as
T_{12},T_{23}\to\infty
as well.

8.
Construction of semi-global Kuranishi structures

Starting from this section we specialize to contact homology.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2},\dots,\mathcal{M}_{\rho}
be a sequence of distinct moduli spaces

\mathcal{M}_{i}=\mathcal{M}_{J}^{\operatorname{ind}=k_{i}}(\dot{F}_{i},\mathbb{R}\times M;\gamma_{i,+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i,-}),

such that
\dot{F}_{i}
is a planar surface and each component of each level of a building in
\partial\mathcal{M}_{i}
is either a trivial cylinder or in
\mathcal{M}_{j}
with
j<i
. (A specific choice will be given in Section
8.6.1
.) The ends of the moduli spaces
\mathcal{M}_{i}
are
\vartheta
-sorted using the action. Also let
\mathcal{S}=\{\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2},\dots,\mathcal{M}_{\rho}\}
and
G_{i}=G(\dot{F}_{i},\mathbb{R}\times M;\gamma_{i,+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i,-})
.

Convention 8.0.1
.

In view of Convention
6.1.1
, we include moduli spaces
\mathcal{M}_{i}
in the list even when they are empty, provided
\partial\mathcal{M}_{i}\not=\varnothing
.

This section is organized as follows: In Section
8.1
we introduce combinatorial objects called
contact homology (or CH) trees of
\operatorname{(Symp)}
type
that encode the gluing data for the various strata of the SFT-compactified moduli spaces that appear in the definition of
\partial^{2}=0
and in Section
8.2
we describe automorphisms of the moduli spaces and their effect on the various strata. Section
8.3
is devoted to the definition of semi-global Kuranishi structures and multisections of semi-global Kuranishi structures, and culminates in Proposition
8.3.7
, which describes the weighted branched manifold extracted from a semi-global Kuranishi structure and a multisection. Sections
8.4
and
8.5
prove some preparatory lemmas and the semi-global Kuranishi structures and multisections for
\partial^{2}=0
are constructed in Sections
8.6
–
8.9
. In Sections
8.10
and
8.11
we explain the modifications needed for chain maps and chain homotopy.

8.1.
CH trees

8.1.1.
Definition of CH trees

Definition 8.1.1

(Pre-CH tree of
\operatorname{(Symp)}
type)
.

A
pre-CH tree of
\operatorname{(Symp)}
type
(or simply a
pre-CH tree
) is a directed tree
12
12

12

Trees are assumed to be connected. A collection of trees will be called a
forest
.
13
13

13

A directed tree
T
has a vertex, called the
root
, and all the edges of
T
point away from the root. The root corresponds to the topmost level of an SFT building, so the edges point down in the SFT sense.

T=(V(T),E(T)=G(T)\sqcup F(T)),

where the sets of vertices
V(T)
and edges
E(T)
are finite and the following hold:

(1)

V(T)
is labeled using the
vertex labeling function

l_{V(T)}:V(T)\to\{1,\dots,\rho\}.

The vertex labeling function assigns a moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{l_{V(T)}(v)}
to each vertex
v\in V(T)
and is not necessarily injective or surjective.

(2)

E(T)
consists of the
glued edges

G(T)
and the
free edges

F(T)
. The edges of
G(T)
have initial and terminal points but the edges of
F(T)
only have an initial point.

Given an edge
e\in E(T)
, we write
i(e)
for the initial point of
e
and
t(e)
for the terminal point of
e
(if it exists). Given a vertex
v\in V(T)
, we write
E(T)_{v}
for the set of all edges
e
with
i(e)=v
,
G(T)_{v}=E(T)_{v}\cap G(T)
, and
F(T)_{v}=E(T)_{v}\cap F(T)
.

(3)

There exists an
orbit assignment map

\operatorname{Orb}_{T}:E(T)\to\mathcal{P}_{\alpha}

such that:

(a)

for each
v\in V(T)
,
\operatorname{Orb}_{T}({E(T)_{v}})
, counted with multiplicity and modulo the ordering, agrees with
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}
where
\mathcal{M}_{l_{V(T)}(v)}=\mathcal{M}(\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-})
;

(b)

for each
e\in G(T)
,
\operatorname{Orb}_{T}(e)
agrees with the positive end
\gamma_{+}
of
\mathcal{M}_{l_{V(T)}(t(e))}=\mathcal{M}(\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-})
.

In other words, each edge
e\in G(T)
corresponds to a single gluing of an interior semi-global Kuranishi chart
V_{l_{V(T)}(i(e))}
on the upper level with
V_{l_{V(T)}(t(e))}
on the lower level.

We also define the
root orbit

\mathfrak{r}(T)
as the positive end
\gamma_{+}
of
\mathcal{M}_{l_{V(T)}(v)}=\mathcal{M}(\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-})
where
v
is the root vertex.

Definition 8.1.2

(CH tree of
\operatorname{(Symp)}
type)
.

A
CH tree of
\operatorname{(Symp)}
type
(or simply a
CH tree
) is a pre-CH tree
T
together with a
reordering function
, i.e., a bijective function

O(T):F(T)\to\{1,\dots,|F(T)|\}

that
respects the ordering
\vartheta
,
i.e., for any
e_{1},e_{2}\in F(T)
, if
\vartheta(\operatorname{Orb}_{T}(e_{1}))<\vartheta(\operatorname{Orb}_{T}(e_{2}))
, then
O(T)(e_{1})<O(T)(e_{2})
.

Remark 8.1.3
.

If
\operatorname{Orb}_{T}|_{F(T)}
is injective, then the reordering function
O(T)
is completely determined by
\vartheta
.

A CH tree
T
gives a prescription for gluing elements of
\mathcal{M}_{l_{V(T)}(v)}
to give an element of the moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{T/\!\!/T}
, where
T/\!\!/T
is the contraction given in Section
8.1.4
.

8.1.2.
Isomorphisms of CH trees

Definition 8.1.4
.

An
isomorphism
\theta:T\to T^{\prime}
of pre-CH trees
is an isomorphism of directed trees which

(1)

sends
V(T),G(T),F(T)
to
V(T^{\prime}),G(T^{\prime}),F(T^{\prime})
; and

(2)

preserves the labels of vertices, i.e.,
l_{V(T)}=l_{V(T^{\prime})}\circ\theta|_{V(T)}
.

An
isomorphism
\theta:T\to T^{\prime}
of CH trees
is an isomorphism of pre-CH trees which

(3)

maps
O(T)
to
O(T^{\prime})
, i.e.,
O(T)=O(T^{\prime})\circ\theta|_{F(T)}.

We denote the isomorphism class of
T
of CH trees by
[T]
.

Remark 8.1.5
.

(i)

Given an isomorphism
\theta:T\to T^{\prime}
of CH trees, we have

\operatorname{Orb}_{T}=\operatorname{Orb}_{T^{\prime}}\circ\theta|_{E(T)}
by Definition
8.1.4
; Definition
8.1.1
(3)(b) for glued edges; and the
\vartheta
-respecting property in Definition
8.1.2
and Definition
8.1.1
(3)(a) for free edges.

(ii)

There is a unique isomorphism class of
1
-vertex CH trees whose vertex is labeled
i
.

8.1.3.
Canonical representatives

In this subsection we explain how to obtain a canonical planar representative (modulo isotopies of the plane) of an isomorphism class
[T]
of CH trees.
14
14

14

Hopefully this puts to rest some doubts that may arise from using isomorphism classes
[T]
in the definition of semi-global Kuranishi structures (Definition
8.3.1
) below.

We first discuss pre-CH trees.
The representatives will be planar, with the root at the top, say along
y=N
. We say that a planar tree
T
is
\vartheta
-sorted
if the edges
e_{v,1},\dots,e_{v,k(v)}
leaving each vertex
v
, go from some
y=N^{\prime}
to
y=N^{\prime}-1
, where
N^{\prime}
depends only on
v
, and are ordered from left to right, and
(\operatorname{Orb}_{T}(e_{v,1}),\dots,\operatorname{Orb}_{T}(e_{v,k(v)}))
is
\vartheta
-sorted.

Let
T
be a
\vartheta
-sorted planar pre-CH tree and the edges leaving the root go to
y=N-1
with
\vartheta
-sorted orbit assignments of the form

(\underbrace{\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{1}}_{i_{1}\text{ copies }},\dots,\underbrace{\gamma_{k},\dots,\gamma_{k}}_{i_{k}\text{ copies }}).

We will inductively construct:

(1)

canonical
\vartheta
-sorted planar representatives for all pre-CH trees
T
; and

(2)

a total ordering
\prec
of
\mathcal{P}_{\alpha}\sqcup\{\mbox{isomorphism classes of pre-CH trees}\}
that extends the ordering on
\mathcal{P}_{\alpha}
from
\vartheta
.

If we restrict to
1
-vertex pre-CH trees
T
, then (1) is immediate and (2) follows from lexicographically comparing

(\mathfrak{r}(T),\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{k},\dots,\gamma_{k}).

If
\mathfrak{r}(T)=\gamma
, then
\gamma\prec[T]
; this includes the case where
T
has no edges.

Suppose we have inductively constructed (1) and (2) when restricted to all pre-CH trees
T^{\prime}
with
\mathfrak{r}(T^{\prime})\prec\mathfrak{r}(T)
. Then for each grouping
(\gamma_{j},\dots,\gamma_{j})
,
j=1,\dots,k
,

(a)

if the edge is not free, we replace the edge labeled
\gamma_{j}
