by the pre-CH tree below it in the planar representation to obtain
(\gamma_{j1},\dots,\gamma_{ji_{j}})
, where
\gamma_{j\ell}
is either
\gamma_{j}
or a pre-CH tree; and

(b)

we permute
(\gamma_{j1},\dots,\gamma_{ji_{j}})
(and the edges that go to
\gamma_{j\ell}
and the subtrees below if they exist) so that it is in lexicographic order; in particular, the free edges come first.

Hence (1) and (2) for
T
(more precisely, the extension of the ordering
\prec
to include
T
) can be constructed using

(\mathfrak{r}(T),\gamma_{11},\dots,\gamma_{1i_{1}},\dots,\gamma_{k1},\dots,\gamma_{ki_{k}}).

Next we discuss CH trees, i.e., add the reordering function
O(T)
to the data.
For a
1
-vertex CH tree
T
, we can normalize the reordering function
O(T)
by Remark
8.1.5
(ii) so the labels are
1,\dots,i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}
from left to right, since we can permute all the free edges that go to
\gamma_{j}
,
j=1,\dots,k
. In general, if
\gamma_{jj^{\prime}}=\gamma_{jj^{\prime}+1}
are orbits, then we permute the corresponding free edges
e^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime}
if necessary so that
O(T)(e^{\prime})<O(T)(e^{\prime\prime})
. If
\gamma_{jj^{\prime}}=\gamma_{jj^{\prime}+1}
are trees, then we switch their positions if
\operatorname{min}O(T)(F(\gamma_{jj^{\prime}}))>\operatorname{min}O(T)(F(\gamma_{jj^{\prime}+1}))
. This yields a canonical
\vartheta
-sorted planar representative of
T
together with canonical normalized
O(T)
; see Figure
2
for example.

\begin{overpic}[normalization.eps]
\put(2.0,34.0){\small{$\gamma_{1}$}} \put(21.0,34.0){\small{$\gamma_{1}$}}
\put(36.3,34.0){\small{$\gamma_{1}$}}
\put(59.0,24.0){\small$\gamma_{2}$}
\put(77.0,24.0){\small$\gamma_{2}$}
\put(96.0,24.0){\small$\gamma_{2}$}
\put(23.0,-5.0){\small$T_{1}$}
\put(32.0,-5.0){\small$\prec$}
\put(40.0,-5.0){\small$T_{2}$}
\end{overpic}

Figure 2.
Example of a canonical representative of a CH tree. The black dots represent vertices. The first
\gamma_{1}
edge and all the
\gamma_{2}
edges are free. The subtrees
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
lie below the second and third
\gamma_{1}
edges and are assumed to satisfy
T_{1}\prec T_{2}
. We may take the reordering function
O(T)
to label the free edges by
4,1,2,3,5,6,7
from left to right.

8.1.4.
Contraction of CH trees

Definition 8.1.6

(Good subtree/subforest)
.

A
good subtree

S
of a CH tree
T
is a subtree with no free edges and at least one glued edge. A possibly empty disjoint union
S=\sqcup_{i}S_{i}
of good subtrees of
T
is a
good subforest
of
T
.

Definition 8.1.7
.

If
S,S^{\prime}
are good subforests of CH trees
T,T^{\prime}
, respectively, then the pair
(T,S)
is
isomorphic to

(T^{\prime},S^{\prime})
if there exists a CH tree isomorphism
\theta:T\to T^{\prime}
that maps
S
to
S^{\prime}
. We denote the isomorphism class of
(T,S)
by
[(T,S)]
.

Definition 8.1.8

(Contraction)
.

Let
T
be a CH tree and
S=\sqcup_{i}S_{i}
be a good subforest of
T
.

Then the
contraction
T^{\prime}=T/\!\!/S
of
T
along
S
is the CH tree given as follows:

(1)

Each subtree
S_{i}
is replaced by a vertex labeled by
\tau(S_{i})\in\{1,\dots,\rho\}
, such that, if we glue elements of
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{j}/\mathbb{R}}
,
j\in V(S_{i})
, according to the gluing prescription given by
S_{i}
, we obtain elements of
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\tau(S_{i})}/\mathbb{R}}
. For the vertices not in
S_{i}
, the vertex labeling function
l_{V(T)}
remains the same.

(2)

The glued edges of
T
not in
S
remain glued edges of
T^{\prime}
and the free edges of
T
remain free edges of
T
. Hence there exists a natural bijection

c:E(T)-E(S)\xrightarrow{\sim}E(T^{\prime}).

(3)

\operatorname{Orb}_{T^{\prime}}
and
O(T^{\prime})
are defined by

\operatorname{Orb}_{T^{\prime}}\circ c=\operatorname{Orb}_{T}|_{E(T)-E(S)}\quad\mbox{and}\quad O(T^{\prime})\circ c|_{F(T)}=O(T)|_{F(T)}.

We write
T/\!\!/T
for
T/\!\!/S
when
S
is the largest good subforest of T.

Definition 8.1.9
.

We define a (strict) partial order on CH trees by
T^{\prime}<T
if
T^{\prime}=T/\!\!/S
for some non-empty good subforest
S
. The partial order also descends to isomorphism classes of CH trees:
[T^{\prime}]<[T]
if there exist
T^{\prime}\in[T^{\prime}]
and
T\in[T]
such that
T^{\prime}<T
.

8.1.5.
Degeneration of CH trees

Let
S
be a good subforest of a CH tree
T
. A
degeneration of
T
along
S
is roughly a forest
T_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{m}
of CH trees with some data indicating how to glue them. It is obtained from
T
as follows: First replace each edge
e\in S
by a free edge
e^{\prime}
such that
i(e^{\prime})=i(e)
and the orbit assignments of
e^{\prime}
and
e
remain the same. This yields
m=|E(S)|+1
connected components
T_{1},\dots,T_{m}
, where each
T_{i}
has an induced CH tree structure with the possible exception of the reordering function. We then choose a reordering function
O(T_{i})
for each
T_{i}
that is consistent with the reordering function induced from
O(T)
(i.e., we decide the ordering of the newly added free edges relative to the existing free edges).
Observe that, in general, the reordering function
O(T_{i})
is not uniquely determined by the above requirement.

The above degeneration procedure is formally given as follows:

Definition 8.1.10
.

A degeneration
\overrightarrow{T}(S)
(or simply
\overrightarrow{T}
if
S
is understood) of
T
along
S
is a tuple
(T_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{m},\iota^{E(T)},\iota^{V(T)},\theta)
such that:

(D1)

T_{i}
is a CH tree for
i\in\{1,\dots,m\}.

(D2)

\iota^{E(T)}:E(S)\to F(T_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{m})
is an injective map.

(D3)

\iota^{V(T)}:E(S)\to V(T_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{m})
is an injective map.

(D4)

T_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{m}
can be glued into a pre-CH tree
\widehat{T}
by replacing the free edge
\iota^{E(T)}(e)
with a glued edge that goes from
i(\iota^{E(T)}(e))
to
\iota^{V(T)}(e)
for all
e\in E(S)
.

(D5)

\theta:\widehat{T}\to T
an isomorphism of pre-CH trees that identifies the glued edge corresponding to
\iota^{E(T)}(e)
with
e
for all
e\in E(S)
and the bijection

\theta:F(T_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{m})-\iota^{E(T)}(E(S))\to F(T)

is compatible with the orderings
O(T_{i})
and
O(T)
for each
i\in\{1,\dots,m\}
, i.e., for
e_{1},e_{2}\in F(T_{i})-\iota^{E(T)}(E(S))
,
O(T_{i})(e_{1})>O(T_{i})(e_{2})
if and only if
O(T)(\theta(e_{1}))>O(T)(\theta(e_{2}))
.

If the pair
(T,S)
is isomorphic to the pair
(T^{\prime},S^{\prime})
and
\overrightarrow{T}
is a degeneration of
T
along
S
, then it is clear that
\overrightarrow{T}
is also a degeneration of
T^{\prime}
along
S^{\prime}
. Hence, it makes sense to refer to a degeneration of
[(T,S)].

Definition 8.1.11
.

Let
\overrightarrow{T}
and
(\overrightarrow{T})^{\prime}
be degenerations of
T
along
S
. We say that
\overrightarrow{T}
is isomorphic to
(\overrightarrow{T})^{\prime}
if there exist CH tree isomorphisms
\theta_{i}:T_{i}\to T_{i}^{\prime}
for
i\in\{1,\dots,m\}
such that they commute with
(D2)
–
(D5)
in the obvious sense.
We denote the isomorphism class by
[\overrightarrow{T}(S)]
or simply
[\overrightarrow{T}].

Example 8.1.12
.

Let
T
be a CH tree with two vertices
v_{1},v_{2}
and
S
be the subforest that contains the only glued edge
e
from
v_{2}
to
v_{1}
. Suppose that
\mathcal{M}_{l_{V(T)}(v_{2})}=\mathcal{M}(\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-})
and
\operatorname{Orb}_{T}(e)=\gamma_{-,1}
, where

\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}=(\underbrace{\gamma_{-,1},\dots,\gamma_{-,1}}_{i_{1}\text{ copies }},\dots,\underbrace{\gamma_{-,k},\dots,\gamma_{-,k}}_{i_{k}\text{ copies }})

is
\vartheta
-sorted. Let
\overrightarrow{T}=(T_{1}\sqcup T_{2},\iota^{E(T)},\iota^{V(T)},\theta)
be a degeneration of
T
along
S
. Then
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
are one-vertex trees; we take
V(T_{i})=\{v_{i}\}
,
i=1,2
. Let us denote
E(T_{2})=\{e_{2}^{1},\dots,e_{2}^{n}\}
with
n=i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}
, where for convenience we are taking the superscript of
e_{2}^{j}
to agree with the reordering function
O(T_{2})
. Then
\iota^{E(T)}(e)=e_{2}^{q}
for some
q\in\{1,\dots,i_{1}\}
. We denote this degeneration by

\overrightarrow{T}_{q}=(T_{1,q}\sqcup T_{2,q},\iota_{q}^{E(T)},\iota_{q}^{V(T)},\theta_{q}).

We claim that the
\overrightarrow{T}_{q}
are non-isomorphic for different
q
. Indeed, suppose
\overrightarrow{T}_{q}
is isomorphic to
\overrightarrow{T}_{q^{\prime}}
and
\theta_{i}:T_{i,q}\to T_{i,q^{\prime}}
is the CH tree isomorphism for
i\in\{1,2\}.

Since
\iota^{V(T)}_{q^{\prime}}(e)=\theta_{1}(\iota^{V(T)}_{q}(e))
and
\theta_{1}
maps
O(T_{1,q})
to
O(T_{1,q^{\prime}})
, it follows that
q=q^{\prime}
. On the other hand, as long as
q=q^{\prime}
one can always find
\theta_{1}
and
\theta_{2}.
Hence there are
i_{1}
non-isomorphic degenerations of
T
along
S
.

Remark 8.1.13
.

When
\operatorname{Orb}_{T}|_{E(S)}
is injective, we can define a special degeneration
\overrightarrow{T}^{s}
by choosing a special reordering function
O(T_{i})
as follows:

(1)

if
F(T_{i})\cap\operatorname{Im}\iota^{E(T)}=\varnothing
, then
O(T_{i})
is uniquely determined by
O(T)
;

(2)

if there exists
e\in F(T_{i})\cap\operatorname{Im}\iota^{E(T)}
, then we choose
O(T_{i})
such that
O(T_{i})(e)\geq O(T_{i})(e^{\prime})
for all
e^{\prime}\in F(T_{i})
and
\operatorname{Orb}_{T_{i}}(e^{\prime})=\operatorname{Orb}_{T_{i}}(e)
.

In Example
8.1.12
, this means choosing
q=i_{1}
.

8.2.
Symmetries of moduli spaces

The group
G_{i}=G(\dot{F}_{i},\mathbb{R}\times M;\gamma_{i,+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i,-})
generated by marker rotations and puncture reorderings can be viewed as a group of automorphisms acting on
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}}
. The boundary strata of
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}}
are described by CH trees
T
such that
T/\!\!/T=i
.

Given a CH tree
T
such that
T/\!\!/T=i
and
\sigma\in G_{i}
, we can define a CH tree
T_{\sigma}
such that:

(
\sigma
1)

V(T_{\sigma})=V(T)
,
15
15

15

Note that we have an equality of sets, not just a bijection.

(
\sigma
2)

E(T_{\sigma})=E(T)
,

(
\sigma
3)

l_{V(T_{\sigma})}=l_{V(T)}
,

(
\sigma
4)

\operatorname{Orb}_{T_{\sigma}}=\operatorname{Orb}_{T}
, and

(
\sigma
5)

O(T_{\sigma})=\sigma\circ O(T)
,

where
\sigma
is sometimes viewed as a permutation of the negative ends (e.g., in
\sigma\circ O(T)
).
There is an isomorphism
\sigma_{*}:T\to T_{\sigma}
of pre-CH trees (but not necessarily an isomorphism of CH trees). Moreover
G_{i}
permutes the set of CH trees with underlying pre-CH tree
T
.

Definition 8.2.1

(Subgroups of
G_{i}
)
.

(1)

Let
G_{i}(T)\subset G_{i}
be the stabilizer of the CH tree
T
such that
T/\!\!/T=i
; by this we mean
G_{i}(T)
contains all the marker rotations and the puncture reorderings
\sigma
such that
T_{\sigma}=T
.

(2)

The superscript
-
(resp.
+
) as in
G_{i}^{-}
indicates the subgroup generated by marker rotations and puncture reorderings of the negative ends (resp. marker rotations of the positive ends) and the subscript
P
(resp.
M
) as in
G_{i,P}
indicates the subgroup generated by puncture reorderings (resp. marker rotations).

(3)

We write
G_{i,M,k}^{-}
to indicate the marker rotations of the
k
th negative end.

By the constructions in the later subsections, elements of
G_{i}
will give isomorphisms of semi-global Kuranishi charts, which are labelled by isomorphism classes of CH trees. A marker rotation does not change the isomorphism class of the tree and hence maps a chart to itself, whilst a puncture reordering can map a chart to another one.

Let
(T,S)
be a pair consisting of a CH tree
T
and a good subtree
S
,
\overrightarrow{T}=(T_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{m},\iota^{E(T)},\iota^{V(T)},\theta)
a degeneration of
T
along
S
, and
\sigma\in G_{i}
. Then
S_{\sigma}:=\sigma_{*}(S)
is a good subforest of
T_{\sigma}
and we choose a specific degeneration

\overrightarrow{T_{\sigma}}=(T_{1,\sigma}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{m,\sigma},\iota^{E(T_{\sigma})},\iota^{V(T_{\sigma})},\theta_{\sigma})

of
T_{\sigma}
along
S_{\sigma}
as follows: Each
T_{i,\sigma}
is given by the tree
T_{i}
with a (possibly) different reordering function
O(T_{i,\sigma})
.
The reordering function
O(T_{\sigma})
orders
F(T_{\sigma})
, which contains all the free edges of
T_{i}
except those that come from
S
, which are denoted by
F_{S,i}
.
The reordering function
O(T_{i,\sigma})
is defined by

(1)

for
e\in F_{S,i}
,
O(T_{i,\sigma})(e)=O(T_{i})(e)
,

(2)

for
e,e^{\prime}\in F(T_{\sigma})
,
O(T_{i,\sigma})(e)<O(T_{i,\sigma})(e^{\prime})
if
\sigma(O(T_{i})(e))<\sigma(O(T_{i})(e^{\prime})).

It is easy to see that maps
\iota^{E(T_{\sigma})},\iota^{V(T_{\sigma})}
and
\theta_{\sigma}
are also determined canonically.
One can verify that
\overrightarrow{T_{\sigma}}
is a degeneration of
T_{\sigma}
along
S_{\sigma}
and that
[\overrightarrow{T_{\sigma}}]
is independent of the choice of representative of
[(T,S)]
.

Since the free edges of
T_{i}
and
T_{i,\sigma}
are canonically identified, comparing the reordering functions
O(T_{i})
and
O(T_{i,\sigma})
gives us a permutation
\sigma_{i}\in G^{-}_{T_{i}/\!\!/T_{i},P}
such that
(\sigma_{i})_{*}:T_{i}\to T_{i,\sigma}
. The map
\sigma\mapsto(\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{m})
induces an injective group homomorphism denoted by
\delta_{\overrightarrow{T}}:G^{-}_{T/\!\!/T,P}\to\prod_{i=1}^{m}G^{-}_{T_{i}/\!\!/T_{i},P}
.

8.3.
Definition of semi-global Kuranishi structures

To simplify notation, we write
T
instead of
[T]
for the isomorphism class unless otherwise stated.

We will work in the
\operatorname{(Symp)}
case.

Definition 8.3.1

(Semi-global Kuranishi structures
\operatorname{(Symp)}
)
.

A
semi-global Kuranishi structure
\mathscr{K}
for
(\overline{\mathcal{M}_{1}/\mathbb{R}},\dots,\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\rho}/\mathbb{R}})
is the disjoint union of the categories
\mathscr{K}(\mathcal{M}_{i})
over all
i=1,\dots,\rho
, together with the
strata compatibility conditions
, given by Definition
8.3.3
.

Definition 8.3.2

(Definition of
\mathscr{K}(\mathcal{M}_{i})
)
.

\mathscr{K}(\mathcal{M}_{i})
is a category with data (K1)–(K5) and the action of
G_{i}
on it given by (K6)–(K8):

(K1)

The objects are semi-global Kuranishi charts

\mathcal{C}_{T}=(\pi_{T}:E_{T}\to V_{T},\overline{\partial}_{T},\psi_{T},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G_{i}(T)})

indexed by isomorphism classes
T
of CH trees
T
such that
T/\!\!/T=i
, together with their
slight enlargements

\mathcal{C}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}=(\pi_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}:E_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}},\overline{\partial}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}},\psi_{T}^{\operatorname{en}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G_{i}(T)}),

such that:

(a)

(\pi_{T}:E_{T}\to V_{T},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G_{i}(T)})
and
(\pi_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}:E_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}\to V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G_{i}(T)})
are global orbibundle charts,
\overline{\partial}_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}:V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}\to E_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
is a section of the global quotient orbibundle, and
\psi_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}:(\overline{\partial}_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}})^{-1}(0)\to\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}
is a homeomorphism onto an open subset such that

\dim V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}-\operatorname{rank}E_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}=\operatorname{vdim}(\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}).

(b)

V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
is a manifold with a compatible metric
d_{T}
,
V_{T}
is an open subset of
V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
whose closure
\overline{V}_{T}
in
V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
is complete with respect to
d_{T}
,
\pi_{T}=\pi_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}|_{V_{T}}
(by this we mean the restriction of
\pi_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
to
(\pi_{T}^{\operatorname{en}})^{-1}(V_{T})
),
\overline{\partial}_{T}=\overline{\partial}_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}|_{V_{T}}
, and
\psi_{T}=\psi_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}|_{\overline{\partial}^{-1}_{T}(0)}
.

The slight enlargements
V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
of
V_{T}
exist for technical reasons which can be summarized in Example
8.3.4
below; they can be viewed as replacements for manifolds with corners. We denote
\partial V_{T}=\overline{V}_{T}-V_{T}
. When
T=(i)
, we also write
T=i
.

(c)

\mathcal{C}_{i}=(\pi_{i}:E_{i}\to V_{i},\overline{\partial}_{i},\psi_{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G_{i}})
and its slight enlargement
\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\operatorname{en}}=(\pi_{i}^{\operatorname{en}}:E_{i}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{i}^{\operatorname{en}},\overline{\partial}_{i}^{\operatorname{en}},\psi_{i}^{\operatorname{en}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G_{i}})
are interior semi-global Kuranishi charts for a compact subset
K_{i}
of
\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}
and
V_{i}
is bounded with respect to the metric
d_{i}
.

(K2)

For each
T^{\prime}<T
, there is a unique morphism, called the “restriction-inclusion” morphism, consisting of

(i)

\phi_{T^{\prime},T}:\mathcal{C}_{T^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{C}_{T}
encoded by
(V_{T^{\prime},T},\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\sharp},\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\flat})
and

(ii)

enlargement
\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}:\mathcal{C}_{T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}}\to\mathcal{C}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
encoded by
(V_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}},(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\sharp})^{\operatorname{en}},(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}})

such that:

(a)

V_{T^{\prime},T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
is an open subset of
V_{T^{\prime}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
,
E_{T^{\prime},T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}:=E_{T^{\prime}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}|_{V_{T^{\prime},T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}}
, and there is a
G_{i}(T)\cap G_{i}(T^{\prime})
-equivariant bundle embedding given by the commutative diagram

(8.3.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{T^{\prime},T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{T^{\prime},T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\sharp})^{{\operatorname{en}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\flat})^{{\operatorname{en}}}}

(b)

V_{T^{\prime},T}
is an open subset of
V_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}\cap V_{T^{\prime}}
whose closure
\overline{V}_{T^{\prime},T}
is complete with respect to
d_{T^{\prime}}
,
E_{T^{\prime},T}=E_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}|_{V_{T^{\prime},T}}
,
\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\sharp}:=(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\sharp})^{\operatorname{en}}|_{E_{T^{\prime},T}}
has image in
E_{T}
,
\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\flat}:=(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}}|_{V_{T^{\prime},T}}
has image in
V_{T}
, and the analog of Diagram (
8.3.1
) with
{}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
removed is also
G_{i}(T)\cap G_{i}(T^{\prime})
-equivariant.

(c)

Writing
V^{\operatorname{en}}_{T^{\prime},T}-V_{T^{\prime},T}=V^{1}_{T^{\prime},T}\sqcup V^{2}_{T^{\prime},T}
, where
V^{1}_{T^{\prime},T}\subset V^{\operatorname{en}}_{T^{\prime}}-V_{T^{\prime}}
and
V^{2}_{T^{\prime},T}\subset V_{T^{\prime}}
, we have:

(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\flat})^{{\operatorname{en}}}(V^{2}_{T^{\prime},T})\subset V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}-V_{T}\quad\mbox{and}\quad(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\flat})^{{\operatorname{en}}}(V^{1}_{T^{\prime},T})\subset V_{T}.

(d)

(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\sharp})^{{\operatorname{en}}}\circ\overline{\partial}_{T^{\prime},T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}=\overline{\partial}_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}\circ(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\flat})^{{\operatorname{en}}}
,
where
\overline{\partial}_{T^{\prime},T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}:=\overline{\partial}_{T^{\prime}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}|_{V_{T^{\prime},T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}}
.

(e)

\psi_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\circ(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}}=\psi_{T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}}
on
(\overline{\partial}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T^{\prime}})^{-1}(0)\cap V_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}
.

(f)

Over
(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\flat})^{{\operatorname{en}}}(V_{T^{\prime},T}^{{\operatorname{en}}})
,
(\overline{\partial}_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}})_{*}:TV_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}\to E_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
descends to an isomorphism

TV_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}/(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\flat})^{{\operatorname{en}}}_{*}(TV_{T^{\prime},T}^{{\operatorname{en}}})\xrightarrow{\sim}E_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}/(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\sharp})^{{\operatorname{en}}}_{*}E_{T^{\prime},T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}.

(f) is the abstract version of the requirement on
\overline{\partial}^{\prime}_{J}
and
\overline{\partial}_{J}
from Definition
5.4.2
(2).

(K3)

The identity morphism consisting of
\phi_{T,T}:\mathcal{C}_{T}\to\mathcal{C}_{T}
and
\phi_{T,T}^{\operatorname{en}}:\mathcal{C}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to\mathcal{C}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
is given by the data

\displaystyle(V_{T,T},\phi_{T,T}^{\sharp}=\operatorname{id}|_{E_{T}},\phi_{T,T}^{\flat}=\operatorname{id}|_{V_{T}})

\displaystyle(V_{T,T}^{\operatorname{en}},(\phi_{T,T}^{\operatorname{en}})^{\sharp}=\operatorname{id}|_{E_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}},(\phi_{T,T}^{\operatorname{en}})^{\flat}=\operatorname{id}|_{V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}}).

(K4)

The composition of

(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}:\mathcal{C}_{T^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{C}_{T},\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}:\mathcal{C}_{T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}}\to\mathcal{C}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}})

and

(\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T^{\prime}}:\mathcal{C}_{T^{\prime\prime}}\to\mathcal{C}_{T^{\prime}},\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}}:\mathcal{C}_{T^{\prime\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}}\to\mathcal{C}_{T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}})

given by

((V_{T^{\prime},T},\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\sharp},\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\flat}),(V_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}},(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\sharp})^{\operatorname{en}},(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}}))

and

((V_{T^{\prime\prime},T^{\prime}},\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T^{\prime}}^{\sharp},\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T^{\prime}}^{\flat}),(V_{T^{\prime\prime},T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}},(\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T^{\prime}}^{\sharp})^{\operatorname{en}},(\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T^{\prime}}^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}}))

is

(\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T}:\mathcal{C}_{T^{\prime\prime}}\to\mathcal{C}_{T},\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}:\mathcal{C}_{T^{\prime\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}}\to\mathcal{C}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}})

given by

((V_{T^{\prime\prime},T},\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T}^{\sharp},\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T}^{\flat}),(V_{T^{\prime\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}},(\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T}^{\sharp})^{\operatorname{en}},(\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T}^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}}))

such that

\displaystyle V_{T^{\prime\prime},T}=(\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T^{\prime}}^{\flat})^{-1}(V_{T^{\prime},T}),\quad V_{T^{\prime\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}=((\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T^{\prime}}^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}})^{-1}(V_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}),

\displaystyle\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T}^{\flat}=\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\flat}\circ\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T^{\prime}}^{\flat},\quad\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T}^{\sharp}=\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\sharp}\circ\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T^{\prime}}^{\sharp},

\displaystyle(\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T}^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}}=(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}}\circ(\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T^{\prime}}^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}},\quad(\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T}^{\sharp})^{\operatorname{en}}=(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\sharp})^{\operatorname{en}}\circ(\phi_{T^{\prime\prime},T^{\prime}}^{\sharp})^{\operatorname{en}}.

Composition is clearly associative.

(K5)

\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}=\cup_{T/\!\!/T=i}\psi_{T}(\overline{\partial}_{T}^{-1}(0))
.

Given CH trees
T,T^{\prime}
with
T/\!\!/T=T^{\prime}/\!\!/T=i
, an
isomorphism of charts
is a pair

\phi=(\phi^{\sharp},\phi^{\flat}):\mathcal{C}_{T^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{C}_{T},\quad\phi^{\operatorname{en}}=((\phi^{\sharp})^{\operatorname{en}},(\phi^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}}):\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T},

where
(E_{T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}})\xrightarrow{((\phi^{\sharp})^{\operatorname{en}},(\phi^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}})}(E_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}})
is a bundle isomorphism such that
(\phi^{\sharp})^{\operatorname{en}}\circ\overline{\partial}_{T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}}=\overline{\partial}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\circ(\phi^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}}
and
\psi_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\circ(\phi^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}}=\psi_{T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}}
, and which restricts to a bundle isomorphism
(E_{T^{\prime}}\to V_{T^{\prime}})\xrightarrow{(\phi^{\sharp},\phi^{\flat})}(E_{T}\to V_{T})
. We will usually denote an isomorphism of charts by
\phi^{\operatorname{en}}=((\phi^{\sharp})^{\operatorname{en}},(\phi^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}}):\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T}
.

(K6)

For any
T
and
r\in G^{+}_{i,M}
or
G^{-}_{i,M}
,
16
16

16

Recall the notation from Definition
8.2.1
.
there exists an automorphism

r^{\operatorname{en}}=((r^{\sharp})^{\operatorname{en}},(r^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}}):\mathcal{C}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to\mathcal{C}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}

such that
\psi_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\circ(r^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}}\circ(\psi_{T}^{\operatorname{en}})^{-1}
is the same map as rotating the asymptotic markers of the domain Riemann surfaces by
r
.

(K7)

For any
T
and
\sigma\in G^{-}_{i,P}
, there exists an an isomorphism

\sigma^{\operatorname{en}}=((\sigma^{\sharp})^{\operatorname{en}},(\sigma^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}}):\mathcal{C}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to\mathcal{C}_{T_{\sigma}}^{\operatorname{en}}

such that
\psi_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\circ(\sigma^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}}\circ(\psi_{T}^{\operatorname{en}})^{-1}
is given by
permuting the labels of the negative ends of the domain Riemann surfaces by
\sigma
.

(K8)

The collection of restrictions of
E_{T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}}
to
V_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}
(resp.
E_{T^{\prime}}\to V_{T^{\prime}}
to
V_{T^{\prime},T}
) and inclusions
E_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}
to
E_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
(resp.
E_{T^{\prime},T}\to V_{T^{\prime},T}
to
E_{T}\to V_{T}
) given by the commutative diagram from
(K2)
commute with
G_{i}
.

In other words,
G_{i}
acts on
\{\mathcal{C}_{T}\}_{T/\!\!/T=i}
with stabilizer
G_{i}(T)
for
T
and on
\{\mathcal{C}_{T^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{C}_{T}\}_{T^{\prime}<T}
with stabilizer
G_{i}(T)\cap G_{i}(T^{\prime})
for the pair
T^{\prime}<T
.

Note that
\mathscr{K}(\mathcal{M}_{i})
admits a fully faithful functor from the category
\mathcal{T}_{i}
whose objects are CH trees
T
with
T/\!\!/T=i
and morphisms which are given by
T^{\prime}<T
.

Let
S
be a good subforest of
T
and
\overrightarrow{T}=\overrightarrow{T}(S)=(T_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{m},\iota^{E(T)},\iota^{V(T)},\theta)
be a degeneration with
m=m(S)
. We write
V_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}=V_{T_{1}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}\times\dots\times V_{T_{m}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
and
E_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}=\operatorname{pr}_{T_{1}}^{*}E_{T_{1}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}\oplus\dots\oplus\operatorname{pr}_{T_{m}}^{*}E_{T_{m}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
, where
\operatorname{pr}_{T_{k}}:V_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}\times(R-\epsilon_{0},\infty)^{m-1}\to V_{T_{k}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
is the
k
th projection map.

For each
e\in E(S)
, suppose
t(e)
is the root of
T_{a(e)}
and
e
becomes the
k(e)
th free edge of
T_{b(e)}
.
There is a canonical isomorphism

\theta_{e}:G^{+}_{T_{a(e)}/\!\!/T_{a(e)},M}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}G^{-}_{T_{b(e)}/\!\!/T_{b(e)},M,k(e)}.

We then set
G_{\overrightarrow{T},M}=\textstyle(\prod_{j=1}^{m}G_{T_{j}/\!\!/T_{j},M})/G^{\operatorname{diag}}_{\overrightarrow{T},M},
where

\displaystyle G^{\operatorname{diag}}_{\overrightarrow{T},M}=\textstyle\prod_{e\in E(S)}\{(r,\theta_{e}(r))~|~r\in G^{+}_{T_{a(e)}/\!\!/T_{a(e)},M}\}.

There is a natural inclusion
\tau_{\overrightarrow{T}}:G_{T/\!\!/T,M}\to G_{\overrightarrow{T},M}
; also recall
\delta_{\overrightarrow{T}}:G^{-}_{T/\!\!/T,P}\to\prod_{i=1}^{m}G^{-}_{T_{i}/\!\!/T_{i},P}
from Section
8.2
:

Definition 8.3.3

(Stata compatibility conditions)
.

The
strata compatibility conditions for
\mathscr{K}
are given as follows:

(SC1)

There exist global constants
R>0
and
\epsilon_{0}>0
small, “intermediate”
G_{T/\!\!/T}(T)
-invariant open subsets
V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
for all

and analogously defined bundles
E_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\to V_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
such that, for any CH tree
T
with

and a good subforest
S
of

, there exists a
