-bundle map
(\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}},\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}})
between bundles of the same rank

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\times(R-\epsilon_{0},\infty)^{m-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}}

called the
gluing map
and which satisfies the following:

(a)

\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
is a
C^{1}
-map which is a homeomorphism onto its image.

(b)

\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}\circ(\overline{\partial}_{T_{1}}^{{\operatorname{en}}},\dots,\overline{\partial}_{T_{m}}^{{\operatorname{en}}})|_{V_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\times(R-\epsilon_{0},\infty)^{m-1}}
and
\overline{\partial}_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}\circ\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}

are
C^{1}
-close and their difference goes to zero as
\operatorname{min}_{j=1}^{m-1}I_{j}\to\infty
, where
I_{j}
is the coordinate for the
j
th
(R-\epsilon_{0},\infty)
factor corresponding to an edge
e_{j}\in E(S)
.

(c)

Let
S_{j}
be a good subforest of
T_{j}
which we view as a subforest of
T
.
The simultaneous and iterated gluing maps

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S\cup S_{j})}^{{\operatorname{en}}}(v_{1},\dots,v_{j-1},\mathbf{v}_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})},v_{j+1},\dots,v_{m},\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})},\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S)}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S)}^{{\operatorname{en}}}(v_{1},\dots,v_{j-1},\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})}^{{\operatorname{en}}}(\mathbf{v}_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})},\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})}),v_{j+1},\dots,v_{m},\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S)})

where
v_{i}\in V_{T_{i}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
,
\mathbf{v}_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})}\in V_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
,
\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})}\in(R-\epsilon_{0},\infty)^{m(S_{j})-1}
, and
\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S)}\in(R-\epsilon_{0},\infty)^{m(S)-1}
, are
C^{1}
-close with error
\to 0
as the minimum of all the components of
\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S\cup S_{j})}\to\infty
.

Let us denote

\mathfrak{G}^{R_{0},T}:=\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}(T_{\operatorname{max}})}^{\operatorname{en}}(V_{\overrightarrow{T}(T_{\operatorname{max}})}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\times((R-\epsilon_{0},\infty)^{|G(T)|}-(R-\epsilon_{0},R_{0})^{|G(T)|})),

where
T_{\operatorname{max}}
is the maximal good subforest of
T
consisting of all of
V(T)
and
G(T)
.

(d)

\mathfrak{G}^{R-\epsilon_{0},T}\supset\overline{V}_{T}
and
\overline{V}_{T}-\mathfrak{G}^{R_{0},T}
is compact for any
R_{0}\geq R-\epsilon_{0}
.

In view of (c), the sets
\mathfrak{G}^{R_{0},T}
and

(\mathfrak{G}^{R_{0},T})^{\prime}:=\bigcup_{S^{\prime}\subset T}\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S^{\prime})}^{\operatorname{en}}(V_{\overrightarrow{T}(S^{\prime})}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\times(R_{0},\infty)^{m(S^{\prime})-1}),

where the union is taken over all good subforests
S^{\prime}
, can be used interchangeably, i.e., (d) holds with
(\mathfrak{G}^{R_{0},T})^{\prime}
instead of
\mathfrak{G}^{R_{0},T}
.

(SC2)

The maps
\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}
are
G^{\operatorname{diag}}_{\overrightarrow{T},M}
-invariant and for any
\sigma\in G_{T/\!\!/T}
the following diagrams commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\times(R-\epsilon_{0},\infty)^{m-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\overrightarrow{T_{\sigma}}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\times(R-\epsilon_{0},\infty)^{m-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{T_{\sigma}}^{\operatorname{en}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{G}^{\operatorname{en}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\delta_{\overrightarrow{T}}(\sigma)^{\sharp})^{\operatorname{en}}\times\operatorname{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\sigma^{\sharp})^{\operatorname{en}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{G}^{\operatorname{en}}_{\overrightarrow{T_{\sigma}}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}_{\overrightarrow{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}_{\overrightarrow{T_{\sigma}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{T_{\sigma}}^{\operatorname{en}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}^{\operatorname{en}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\delta_{\overrightarrow{T}}(\sigma)^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\sigma^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}^{\operatorname{en}}_{\overrightarrow{T_{\sigma}}}}

For the rest of the paper we will usually omit
{{\operatorname{en}}}
from our notation, namely when we refer to bundle maps and multisections, we assume they admit slight enlargements with superscripts
{}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
.

Let
\sim_{\mathscr{K}}
be the equivalence relation on
\coprod_{T/\!\!/T=i}V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
that is induced by (K2)(a), i.e.,

(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}}(x)\sim_{\mathscr{K}}x,\quad\forall T^{\prime}<T~\mbox{ and }~x\in V_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}.

We define the topology on

\mathcal{V}_{i}:=\left(\textstyle\coprod_{T/\!\!/T=i}V_{T}\right)/\sim_{\mathscr{K}}

as follows: First take the quotient topology on
(\coprod_{T/\!\!/T=i}\overline{V}_{T})/\sim_{\mathscr{K}}
and then take the subset topology on
\mathcal{V}_{i}
. This slightly roundabout procedure is justified by the following example which describes the pathology of taking the quotient topology with respect to an open relation.

Example 8.3.4

(Issues with taking the quotient topology)
.

Consider the subsets
V_{1}=\{x<0\}
and
V_{2}=\{y=0\}
of standard
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{(x,y)}
with the subset topologies. Then the quotient topology
\mathcal{T}_{1}
on
(V_{1}\sqcup V_{2})/\sim
, where
\sim
identifies
(x,0)\in V_{1}
with
(x,0)\in V_{2}
, is not the same as the subset topology
\mathcal{T}_{2}
on
V=\{x<0\mbox{ or }y=0\}
induced from
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. This is because for example

\{(x,y)~|~(x+1)^{2}+y^{2}<1\}\cup\{(x,y)~|~x\in(-1,1),y=0\}

is in
\mathcal{T}_{1}
but not in
\mathcal{T}_{2}
. To circumvent the issue, as explained in
[
FO32
, Section 2]
, we first take the quotient topology on
(\overline{V}_{1}\sqcup\overline{V}_{2})/\sim
, where
\overline{V}_{1}=\{x\leq 0\}
and
\overline{V}_{2}=V_{2}
, and then take the subset topology on
(V_{1}\sqcup V_{2})/\sim
.

Lemma 8.3.5
.

\mathcal{V}_{i}
is Hausdorff.

Proof.

Each
\overline{V}_{T}
is Hausdorff. We then use the fact that if, for
i=1,2
,
X_{i}
is a Hausdorff topological space and
A_{i}
a closed subset of
X_{i}
and
\phi:A_{1}\xrightarrow{\sim}A_{2}
is a homeomorphism, then
(X_{1}\coprod X_{2})/(x\sim\phi(x))
,
\forall x\in A_{1}
, with the quotient topology is also Hausdorff. Hence
(\coprod_{T/\!\!/T=i}\overline{V}_{T})/\sim_{\mathscr{K}}
is Hausdorff and the restriction to
\mathcal{V}_{i}
is also Hausdorff.
∎

Since we will use
\overline{\partial}^{-1}(\mathfrak{s})
as a replacement of
\overline{\partial}^{-1}(0)
, we define:

Definition 8.3.6

(Multisection of a semi-global Kuranishi structure)
.

A
multisection of
\mathscr{K}
is a collection
\mathfrak{S}=\{{\mathfrak{s}}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}:V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to E_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\}_{T}
of slight enlargements of multisections such that:

(1)

for any
T^{\prime}<T
,
(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\sharp})^{\operatorname{en}}\circ\mathfrak{s}_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}=\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\circ(\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\flat})^{\operatorname{en}}
, where
\mathfrak{s}_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}:=\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}|_{V_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}}
;

(2)

there exists
R_{0}\gg 0
such that for each good subforest
S
of
T
with
S\not=\varnothing
, if we write
\overrightarrow{T}=\overrightarrow{T}(S)=(T_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{m(S)},\iota^{E(T)},\iota^{V(T)},\theta)
, then on
\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}(V_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\times(R_{0},\infty)^{m(S)-1})

\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}\circ(\mathfrak{s}_{T_{1}}^{\operatorname{en}},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{T_{m}}^{\operatorname{en}})\circ(\mathfrak{G}^{\operatorname{en}}_{\overrightarrow{T}})^{-1}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}

are
C^{1}
-close and their difference goes to zero as
\min_{j=1}^{m(S)-1}I_{j}\to\infty
, where
(I_{1},\dots,I_{m(S)-1})
are the
(R_{0},\infty)^{m(S)-1}
-coordinates; and

(3)

\mathfrak{S}_{i}:=\{{\mathfrak{s}}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}~|~T/\!\!/T=i\}\subset\mathfrak{S}
is preserved under
G_{i}
.

A multisection
\mathfrak{S}=\{\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\}_{T}
is
C^{1}
-close to the
0
-section
if
\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
is
C^{1}
-close to the
0
-section for each
T
. We often write
\mathfrak{S}=\{\mathfrak{s}_{T}\}_{T}
when the slight enlargements are understood.

Definition
8.3.6
(1) implies that
(\overline{\partial}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}})^{-1}({\mathfrak{s}}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}})
and
(\overline{\partial}_{T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}})^{-1}(\mathfrak{s}_{T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}})
agree on the “overlap” of semi-global Kuranishi charts
V_{T}
and
V_{T^{\prime}}
. Hence
\overline{\partial}^{-1}(\mathfrak{s})
can be obtained by patching
\{\overline{\partial}^{-1}_{T}(\mathfrak{s}_{T})\}_{T}
together. In view of
(SC1)
(d) and Definition
8.3.6
(2), we say that
\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T}
is
product-like
outside a compact subset of
\overline{V}_{T}
.

Given a multisection
\mathfrak{S}_{i}=\{\mathfrak{s}_{T}\}_{T/\!\!/T=i}
of
\mathscr{K}(\mathcal{M}_{i})
, we define

(8.3.2)

\mathcal{Z}_{i}=\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}(\mathcal{M}_{i}),\mathfrak{S}_{i})=\left(\coprod_{T/\!\!/T=i}\overline{\partial}^{-1}_{T}({\mathfrak{s}}_{T})\right)/\sim_{\mathscr{K}},

where
\sim_{\mathscr{K}}
is as above and the topology is the subset topology of
\mathcal{V}_{i}
.

When we want to specify the orbit
\gamma_{+}
at the positive end of the topmost level, the orbits
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}
at the negative end of the bottommost level, and possibly the homology class
A\in H_{2}(M;\mathbb{Z})
(see Section
9.1.2
for an explanation of how to assign a homology class
A\in H_{2}(M;\mathbb{Z})
to
[\mathcal{F},u]
), and that fact that
\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}(\gamma_{+})<L
, we write
\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}^{L}(\alpha,J;\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}),\mathfrak{S})
or
\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}^{L}(\alpha,J;\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-};A),\mathfrak{S})
.

Proposition 8.3.7
.

If the multisection
\mathfrak{S}=\{{\mathfrak{s}}_{T}\}_{T}
of
\mathscr{K}
is
C^{1}
-close to the
0
-section and
\overline{\partial}_{T}
is transverse to
\mathfrak{s}_{T}
for all
T
, then:

(1)

The space
\mathcal{Z}_{i}
is a weighted branched manifold of dimension
\operatorname{vdim}\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}
.

(2)

If
\operatorname{vdim}\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}=0
, then
\mathcal{Z}_{i}
consists of finitely many points with weights.

(3)

If
\operatorname{vdim}\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}=1
, then there exists
\mathcal{L}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that for any generic
\mathcal{L}>\mathcal{L}_{0}
, the
\mathcal{L}
-boundary of
\mathcal{Z}_{i}
defined by

\partial^{\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{Z}_{i}:=\partial\coprod_{T/\!\!/T=i}\left[\overline{\partial}^{-1}_{T}(\mathfrak{s}_{T})-\mathfrak{G}^{\mathcal{L},T}\right]/\sim_{\mathscr{K}}

is in bijection with the union

\coprod_{T/\!\!/T=i,~|V(T)|=2}\left(\mathcal{Z}_{j(T)}\times\mathcal{Z}_{k(T)}\right)/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G^{\operatorname{diag}}_{\overrightarrow{T}^{s},M}}

of product weighted branched submanifolds, where

\overrightarrow{T}^{s}=(j(T)\sqcup k(T),\iota^{E(T)},\iota^{V(T)},\theta)

is the special degeneration of
T
as in Remark
8.1.13
.

Proof.

For convenience, for any
T^{\prime}<T
we often omit the morphism
\phi_{T^{\prime},T}
from the notation and view
V_{T^{\prime},T}
as a subspace of
V_{T}
; we also often omit the map
\psi_{T}
and view
\operatorname{Im}(\psi_{T})\subset\mathcal{M}_{T/\!\!/T}
as a subspace of
V_{T}
. We write
\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{Z}_{i}
.

(1) Lemma
8.3.5
implies that
\mathcal{Z}
is Hausdorff. Then (1) follows from the transversality of
\mathfrak{S}
and
\overline{\partial}_{J}
and Condition
(K2)
(f).

(2) This is an immediate consequence of the compactness of
\mathcal{Z}
. To show the compactness we start with the following:

Claim 8.3.8
.

There exists
R_{0}
such that
\mathcal{Z}\cap\mathfrak{G}^{R^{\prime},T}=\varnothing
for all
T
such that
T/\!\!/T=i
and
R^{\prime}>R_{0}.

Proof of Claim
8.3.8
.

Arguing by contradiction, suppose there exist:

(i)

a CH tree
T
such that
T/\!\!/T=i
and
k=|V(T)|\geq 2
,

(ii)

a good subtree
S
and a degeneration
\overrightarrow{T}=\overrightarrow{T}(S)=T_{j_{1}}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{j_{k}}
along
S
, and

(iii)

a sequence of curves
\{z_{j}\}_{j=j^{\prime}}^{\infty}
in
\mathcal{Z}\cap\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}(V_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\times(j,\infty)^{m(S)-1})
,
with Fredholm index
\operatorname{ind}z_{j}=1
and
j^{\prime}
a large integer.

By (iii), the curve
z_{j}
satisfies the equation
\overline{\partial}_{T}z_{j}=\mathfrak{s}_{T}(z_{j})
.
By Condition
(SC1)
(b) and Definition
8.3.6
(2), for
j\gg 0
there exists
z_{j}^{\prime}\in V_{T}
satisfying
\overline{\partial}_{T}^{\prime}z_{j}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}_{T}(z_{j}^{\prime})
, where

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}_{T}^{\prime}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}\circ(\overline{\partial}_{T_{j_{1}}}^{\operatorname{en}},\dots,\overline{\partial}_{T_{j_{k}}}^{\operatorname{en}})\circ({\mathfrak{G}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}})^{-1},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{\prime}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}\circ(\mathfrak{s}_{T_{j_{1}}}^{\operatorname{en}},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{T_{j_{k}}}^{\operatorname{en}})\circ({\mathfrak{G}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}})^{-1}.

Writing
(\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}})^{-1}(z_{j}^{\prime})=(z_{j1},\dots,z_{jk},I_{1},\dots,I_{k-1})
, we obtain
\overline{\partial}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T_{j_{i}}}z_{ji}=\mathfrak{s}_{T_{j_{i}}}^{\operatorname{en}}(z_{ji})
for each
j,i
with
j\gg 0
. Hence
\operatorname{ind}z_{ji}\geq 1
, contradicting the fact that
\sum_{i=1}^{k}\operatorname{ind}z_{ji}=\operatorname{ind}z_{j}^{\prime}=1
.
∎

For
R^{\prime}\gg 0
,
((\overline{\partial}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T})^{-1}(0)\cap\overline{V}_{T})-\mathfrak{G}^{R^{\prime},T}
is compact by Condition
(SC1)
(d) and hence is bounded since
\overline{V}_{T}
is complete. By Claim
8.3.8
,
\overline{\partial}^{-1}_{T}(\mathfrak{s}_{T})\cap\mathfrak{G}^{R^{\prime},T}=\varnothing
. Let
U
be a relatively compact open neighborhood of
((\overline{\partial}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T})^{-1}(0)\cap\overline{V}_{T})-\mathfrak{G}^{R^{\prime},T}
in
\overline{V}_{T}
. Since
\mathfrak{S}=\{\mathfrak{s}_{T}\}_{T}
is
C^{1}
-close to the
0
-section,
(\overline{\partial}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T})^{-1}(\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}})\cap\overline{V}_{T}\subset U
. Hence
(\overline{\partial}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T})^{-1}(\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}})\cap\overline{V}_{T}
is compact and so is
\mathcal{Z}
.

(3) The key point is the following claim which roughly states that
\mathcal{Z}
has no “interior boundary”:

Claim 8.3.9
.

If
\mathfrak{s}_{T}
is
C^{1}
-close to the
0
-section for all
T
such that
T/\!\!/T=i
, then the closure
\overline{\mathcal{Z}}
, obtained by gluing the closures of
\overline{\partial}_{T}^{-1}(\mathfrak{s}_{T})\subset\overline{V}_{T}
over all
T/\!\!/T=i
, is covered by
\{V_{T}~|~T/\!\!/T=i\}
and the ends of
\mathcal{Z}
are close to a half-open interval times
\prod_{i=1}^{2}\overline{\partial}_{T_{j_{i}}}^{-1}(\mathfrak{s}_{T_{j_{i}}})
, where we are ranging over degenerations
T_{j_{1}}\sqcup T_{j_{2}}
of
T
along a single edge.

Proof of Claim
8.3.9
.

Let
u\in(\overline{\partial}_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}})^{-1}(0)\cap\partial V_{T}
such that
T/\!\!/T=i
. We will show that there exists a neighborhood
U
of
u
in
V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
such that if
\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
is
C^{1}
-close to the
0
-section, then
(\overline{\partial}_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}})^{-1}(\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}})\cap U\subset V_{T^{\prime\prime}}
for some
T^{\prime\prime}
with
T^{\prime\prime}/\!\!/T^{\prime\prime}=i
. By (K1)(a),
\psi_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}(u)\in\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}
. Then by
(K5)
there exists
T^{\prime}
such that
u\in V_{T^{\prime}}
. We consider two cases: (A)
T^{\prime}<T
or (B)
T<T^{\prime}
.

(A) By Conditions
(K2)
(a) (the embedding property and in particular the equivalence of the topology of
V^{\operatorname{en}}_{T^{\prime},T}
and the induced topology), (c), (d), and (f) there exist a neighborhood
U
of
u
in
V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
such that
U\cap V_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}\subset V_{T^{\prime}}
and coordinates
(v_{T^{\prime},T},x)
of
U
, where
v_{T^{\prime},T}\in V_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}
and
x
is the coordinate in the normal direction, such that

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}(v_{T^{\prime},T},x)=(\overline{\partial}_{T^{\prime}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}(v_{T^{\prime},T}),x+\operatorname{h.o.}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}(v_{T^{\prime},T},x)=(\mathfrak{s}_{T^{\prime},T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}(v_{T^{\prime},T}),h(v_{T^{\prime},T},x)),

where
h(v_{T^{\prime},T},0)=0
and has small
x
-derivative. If
U
is small and hence
|x|
is small, the equation

\overline{\partial}_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}(v_{T^{\prime},T},x)=\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}(v_{T^{\prime},T},x)

implies
x=0
. Hence

(\overline{\partial}_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}})^{-1}(\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}})\cap U=(\overline{\partial}_{T^{\prime},T}^{{\operatorname{en}}})^{-1}(\mathfrak{s}_{T^{\prime},T}^{{\operatorname{en}}})\cap U\subset V_{T^{\prime}}.

(B) This is straightforward: By Condition
(K2)
(c) there exists a neighborhood
U
of
u
in
V_{T,T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}}
such that
U\subset V_{T^{\prime}}
, so
(\overline{\partial}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T})^{-1}(\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}})\cap U\subset V_{T^{\prime}}
.

This proves the existence of the neighborhood
U
of
u
and tree
T^{\prime\prime}
.

We now observe that
\mathfrak{s}_{T}
is product-like outside of a compact subset
K_{T}
of
\overline{V}_{T}
. Let
\mathscr{U}_{T}
be a small neighborhood of the closure of
\overline{\partial}^{-1}_{T}(0)\cap K_{T}
in
\overline{V}_{T}
; by compactness we may assume that the closure of
\overline{\partial}^{-1}_{T}(\mathfrak{s}_{T})\cap K_{T}
in
\overline{V}_{T}
(=
(\overline{\partial}^{\operatorname{en}})^{-1}(\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}})\cap K_{T}
) is a subset of
\mathscr{U}_{T}
by requiring
\mathfrak{s}_{T}
to be sufficiently
C^{1}
-close to the
0
-section.
By the existence of
U
and
T^{\prime\prime}
for each
u\in(\overline{\partial}_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}})^{-1}(0)\cap\partial V_{T}
as above,

(\overline{\partial}^{\operatorname{en}})^{-1}(\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}})\cap K_{T}\subset\cup_{T^{\prime}/\!\!/T^{\prime}=i}V_{T^{\prime}}
.

The elements of the ends of
\mathcal{Z}
are close to elements
(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})
of the product
\prod_{i=1}^{k}\overline{\partial}_{T_{j_{i}}}^{-1}(\mathfrak{s}_{T_{j_{i}}})
, together with some gluing parameters. By the index count as in the proof of Claim
8.3.8
,
\sum_{i=1}^{k}\operatorname{ind}z_{i}=2
and
\operatorname{ind}z_{i}\geq 1
. Hence
k
must be exactly two and the claim follows.
∎

In view of Claim
8.3.9
, the bijection in (3) is given by the gluing map
\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}
in
(SC1)
, modulo the symmetry given by
(SC2)
, and the special degeneration is chosen to avoid “over-counting”.
∎

8.4.
Trimming

In Sections
8.4
–
8.7
we assume the following:

(H)

\chi(\dot{F}_{i})<0
for all the domains
\dot{F}_{i}
of
\mathcal{M}_{i}
, where
\mathcal{M}_{i}\in\mathcal{S}
.

Here (H) stands for “hyperbolic”. In particular, whenever there is a reference to
{\bf q}
, we assume
{\bf q}=\varnothing
. In Section
8.8
we make the necessary modifications when
(H)
does not hold.

Choose
\delta>0
small such that
(\alpha,J)
is
L
-simple (see Definitions
3.1.1
and
3.1.4
) with parameter
\delta
.

We make the following definition:

Definition 8.4.1

(Neck length)
.

Given

(\mathcal{F},u)=((F,j,{\bf p},{\bf q},{\bf r}),u)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta}(V_{1}/\mathbb{R},\dots,V_{m}/\mathbb{R}),

recall that
g_{\ddot{F}}
is the complete finite volume hyperbolic metric on
\ddot{F}=F-{\bf p}-{\bf q}
that is compatible with
j
. If there is an annular component
A
of
\operatorname{Thin}_{\varepsilon}(\ddot{F},g_{\ddot{F}})
such that
u|_{A}
is close to a trivial cylinder over
\gamma
, then its
neck length

\mathfrak{nl}(A)
with respect to
\gamma
is the value
C
such that
A
is conformally equivalent to
(\mathbb{R}/\mathcal{A}(\gamma)\mathbb{Z})\times[0,C]
with the standard complex structure.

Choose
\mathcal{L}\gg 0
and
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\gg\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}>0
. Roughly speaking,
\mathcal{L}
will determine the size in terms of neck lengths of the interior semi-global Kuranishi charts
V_{i}
,
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
will measure the widths in terms of neck lengths of the overlapping regions when including
V_{T/\!\!/S}
into
V_{T}
, and
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}
will measure the widths of the slight enlargements
V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
of
V_{T}
.

Let
K
be a sufficiently large
G
-invariant compact subset of
\mathcal{M}/\mathbb{R}
in the sense that (NL
{}_{\mathcal{L}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}
) holds, where for
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}>0
we define:

(NL
{}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
)

[(\mathcal{F},u)]\in K
whenever there is no annular component of
\operatorname{Thin}_{\varepsilon}(\ddot{F},g_{\ddot{F}})
or all the annular components of
\operatorname{Thin}_{\varepsilon}(\ddot{F},g_{\ddot{F}})
have neck length
\leq\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
.

Consider an interior semi-global Kuranishi chart
(K,\pi_{V}:E\to V,\overline{\partial}_{J},\psi,G)
as constructed in Section
5.3
.

We will sometimes suppress
\overline{\partial}_{J}
,
\psi
, and
G
from the notation.

By slightly shrinking
V
and
K
if necessary subject to (NL
{}_{\mathcal{L}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}
), and without changing notation, we may assume that
V
and
K
are
G
-invariant.

Next we explain how to further trim
\pi_{V}:E\to V
and
K
. Given
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
such that
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\leq\mathcal{L}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}
, let
V_{\geq\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
(resp.
V_{>\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
,
V_{=\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
) be the subset of
V
consisting of
[(\mathcal{F},u)]
for which there exists an annular component
A
in
\operatorname{Thin}_{\varepsilon}(\ddot{F},g_{(\ddot{F},j)})
with
\mathfrak{nl}(A)\geq\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
(resp.
\mathfrak{nl}(A)>\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
,
\mathfrak{nl}(A)=\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
) and let
V_{<\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}=V-V_{\geq\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
and
V_{\leq\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}=V-V_{>\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
.
Note that
V_{<\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
and
K\cap V_{\leq\mathcal{L}^{\prime}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}
are still
G
-invariant.

Definition 8.4.2

(Interior charts)
.

The interior semi-global Kuranishi chart for
\mathcal{M}/\mathbb{R}
that we use in
(K1)
(c) is as follows:

(
\mathcal{I}
1)

the bundle is
\pi_{V}|_{V_{<\mathcal{L}}}:E|_{V_{<\mathcal{L}}}\to V_{<\mathcal{L}}
, the compact set is
K\cap V_{\leq\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}
, and the slight enlargement is
\pi_{V}|_{V_{<\mathcal{L}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}}:E|_{V_{<\mathcal{L}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}}\to V_{<\mathcal{L}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}
;

(
\mathcal{I}
2)

the compact set
K\cap V_{\leq\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}
satisfies (NL
{}_{\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}
); and

(
\mathcal{I}
3)

V_{<\mathcal{L}}
,
V_{<\mathcal{L}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}
, and
K\cap V_{\leq\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}
are all
G
-invariant; and

(
\mathcal{I}
4)

\overline{\partial}
(resp.
\overline{\partial}^{\operatorname{en}}
) and
\psi
(resp.
\psi^{\operatorname{en}}
) are restrictions of
\overline{\partial}_{J}
and
\psi
to
V_{<\mathcal{L}}
(resp.
V_{<\mathcal{L}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}
) and
\overline{\partial}^{-1}(0)
(resp.
(\overline{\partial}^{\operatorname{en}})^{-1}(0)
).

Resetting notation, we write
\pi:E\to V
,
K
, and
\pi^{\operatorname{en}}:E^{\operatorname{en}}\to V^{\operatorname{en}}
for the bundle, compact set, and slight enlargement satisfying (
\mathcal{I}
1)–(
\mathcal{I}
3); the constant
\mathcal{L}
is chosen in Section
8.6.2
.

The boundary
\partial V
can be decomposed into two parts, the
vertical boundary

\partial_{v}V=V_{=\mathcal{L}}
and the
horizontal boundary

\partial_{h}V
which is the closure in
\partial V
of
\partial V-\partial_{h}V
.

8.5.
Universal choices of
R,\varepsilon,\ell

Recall
\mathcal{S}=\{\mathcal{M}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{M}_{\rho}\}
. We explain how to choose the universal constants
R,\varepsilon
, and
\ell
.

Remark 8.5.1
.

Observe that, in view of the description of the pregluing from Section
7.1
, the gluing parameter
T_{e}\in[R,\infty)
corresponding to an edge
e
of a CH tree is approximately equal to
C\mathfrak{nl}(A_{e})+D
, where
C>0
and
D\in\mathbb{R}
are fixed constants and
A_{e}
is the
\varepsilon
-thin annulus of the glued domain corresponding to the edge
e
. Hence the neck length bound and the gluing parameter bound can be used interchangeably in Theorems
6.5.1
and
6.5.3
.
In this subsection we take
R
to be the neck length bound.

Lemma 8.5.2
.

There exist a “large”
G_{i}
-invariant compact subset
K_{i}
of
\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}
for each
\mathcal{M}_{i}\in\mathcal{S}
, a gluing parameter bound
R
, and constants
\varepsilon
and
\ell
, such that (i
S
) and (ii
S
) hold for
R,\varepsilon,\ell
, where for any subset
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{S}
such that each component of each level of a building in
\partial\mathcal{M}_{i}
with
\mathcal{M}_{i}\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
is either a trivial cylinder or is in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
we define:

(i
{}_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}
)

Theorems
6.5.1
(A) and
6.5.3
hold for all possible gluings and iterated gluings of all orders of all the
G_{i}
-invariant neighborhoods
V_{i}
of
K_{i}
, possibly with multiplicities, such that
\mathcal{M}_{i}\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
with the same gluing parameter bound
R
.
Here all the
V_{i}
depend on the same parameters
\varepsilon,\ell
.

(ii
{}_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}
)

For each
\mathcal{M}_{i}\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
, the compact subset
K_{i}
contains all the elements of
\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}
that are not in
\cup_{T}\mathcal{V}_{T}
, where
T
ranges over all CH trees representing boundary strata of
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}}
. Here if
T
corresponds to the stratum
\times_{v\in V(T)}(\mathcal{M}_{l_{V(T)}(v)}/\mathbb{R})
, then
\mathcal{V}_{T}
is the intersection of all the images of the gluing maps and iterated gluing maps of all orders of Theorem
6.5.1
(A) and
6.5.3
, obtained by gluing the
V_{l_{V(T)}(v)}
with gluing parameter bound
R
(and parameters
\varepsilon,\ell
).

Proof.

If we want to indicate the dependence of
V_{i}
on
\ell^{i}
and
\varepsilon^{i}
, then we write
V_{i}=V_{i}^{\ell^{i},\varepsilon^{i}}
.
The following is immediate:

Claim 8.5.3
.

If
(\ell^{i})^{\prime}\geq\ell^{i}
and Theorems
6.5.1
and
6.5.3
hold for
V_{i}=V_{i}^{\ell^{i},\varepsilon^{i}}\supset K_{i}
,
i=1,\dots,m
, and gluing parameter bound
R
, then Theorems
6.5.1
and
6.5.3
also hold for
V_{i}^{(\ell^{i})^{\prime},\varepsilon^{i}}
and the same gluing parameter bound
R
, provided
V_{i}^{(\ell^{i})^{\prime},\varepsilon^{i}}
is a sufficiently small neighborhood of
K_{i}
.

The claim states that
\ell
can be increased simply by shrinking the neighborhoods of
K_{i}
. Hence
\ell
can be adjusted at the very end without affecting
R
,
\varepsilon
, and the
K_{i}
.

Step
0
.
Let
\mathcal{S}_{0}
be the set of all
\mathcal{M}_{i}
such that
\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}=\overline{\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}}
. If
\mathcal{M}_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}
, then take
K_{i}=\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}
. Choose
\varepsilon_{0},\ell_{0}
that works for all the
K_{i}
with
\mathcal{M}_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}
and let
R_{0}
be a constant larger than the gluing parameter bounds in Theorems
6.5.1
(A) and
6.5.3
for all possible gluings of the
V_{i}
, possibly with multiplicities, such that
\mathcal{M}_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}
. Note that action considerations imply that there are only finitely many gluing types. Hence (i
{}_{\mathcal{S}_{0}}
) and (ii
{}_{\mathcal{S}_{0}}
) hold with
R=R_{0}
,
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{0}
,
\ell=\ell_{0}
.

Step
j-1
.
Suppose we have inductively chosen:

(1)

\mathcal{S}_{0}\subsetneq\dots\subsetneq\mathcal{S}_{j-1}\subset\{\mathcal{M}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{M}_{\rho}\}
;

(2)

a compact subset
K_{i}
of
\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}
whenever
\mathcal{M}_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{j-1}
; and

(3)

a gluing parameter bound
R_{j-1}
and constants
\varepsilon_{j-1},\ell_{j-1}
;

satisfying (i
{}_{\mathcal{S}_{j-1}}
) and (ii
{}_{\mathcal{S}_{j-1}}
) with
R=R_{j-1}
,
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{j-1}
, and
\ell=\ell_{j-1}
.

We now come to the key point: If we increase
R_{j-1}
to
R_{j-1}^{\prime}>R_{j-1}
with
\varepsilon_{j-1}
and
\ell_{j-1}
fixed, then we must enlarge
K_{i}
to some
K_{i}^{\prime}
so that (ii
{}_{\mathcal{S}_{j-1}}
) still holds. At first glance we then need to increase
R_{j-1}^{\prime}
since it depends on
K_{i}^{\prime}
, leading to a circular argument. Upon closer inspection, however, we can enlarge
K_{i}
to
K_{i}^{\prime}
without increasing
R_{j-1}
so that (i
{}_{\mathcal{S}_{j-1}}
) and (ii
{}_{\mathcal{S}_{j-1}}
) hold for
K_{i}^{\prime}
, as we now explain: First (ii
{}_{\mathcal{S}_{j-1}}
) still holds with
K_{i}
replaced by
K_{i}^{\prime}\supset K_{i}
, as making the compact set larger just makes (ii
{}_{\mathcal{S}_{j-1}}
) easier to attain. Let
V_{i}^{\prime}
be a small neighborhood of
K_{i}^{\prime}
. Next, by (ii
{}_{\mathcal{S}_{j-1}}
) for
K_{i}
, all curves in
V_{i}^{\prime}-V_{i}
are obtained from a simultaneous boundary stratum gluing of
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}}
with gluing parameter bound
R_{j-1}
. Hence the gluing of curves
(u_{i_{1}},\dots,u_{i_{m}})\in V_{i_{1}}^{\prime}\times\dots\times V_{i_{m}}^{\prime}
from a possibly repeated collection of the
K_{i}^{\prime}
corresponding to
\mathcal{M}_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{j-1}
can be performed with gluing parameter bound
R_{j-1}
, since the gluing can be viewed as the result of an iterated gluing where we first obtain the elements
u_{i_{a}}\in V_{i_{a}}^{\prime}-V_{i_{a}}
by a simultaneous boundary stratum gluing of
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{i_{a}}/\mathbb{R}}
with gluing parameter bound
R_{j-1}
and we then simultaneously glue the resulting curves
u_{i_{a}}\in V_{i_{a}}^{\prime}-V_{i_{a}}
and those
u_{i_{a}}
that were in
V_{i_{a}}
. This is possible since Condition (i
{}_{\mathcal{S}_{j-1}}
) allows all possible gluings and iterated gluings. The consistency of the gluings defined above follows from Theorem
6.5.1
(B). Hence for any
R_{j-1}^{\prime}\geq R_{j-1}
we can enlarge
K_{i}
to
K_{i}^{\prime}
such that (i
{}_{\mathcal{S}_{j-1}}
) and (ii
{}_{\mathcal{S}_{j-1}}
) hold with
K_{i}^{\prime}
and
R=R_{j-1}^{\prime}
instead.

On the other hand, in order to shrink
\varepsilon_{j-1}
to
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{j-1}
subject to
0<\varepsilon^{\prime}_{j-1}<\varepsilon_{j-1}
, we must first increase
R_{j-1}
. This also means we enlarge
K_{i}
for all
\mathcal{M}_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{j-1}
as in the previous paragraph. Then for
R_{j-1}
and
K_{i}
sufficiently large, we may substitute
V_{i}^{\ell_{j-1},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{j-1}}
for
V_{i}^{\ell_{j-1},\varepsilon_{j-1}}
since the gluing maps of Theorems
6.5.1
(A) and
6.5.3
can be made to hold simultaneously with the same
R_{j-1}
.

Step
j
.
Let
\mathcal{M}_{i_{j}}
be the moduli space with smallest subscript which is not in
\mathcal{S}_{j-1}
. Choose a compact subset
K_{i_{j}}\subset\mathcal{M}_{i_{j}}/\mathbb{R}
such that

(
\dagger_{j}
)

K_{i_{j}}
contains all the curves that are not in the images of the gluing maps of Theorem
6.5.1
(A) with gluing parameter bound
R_{j-1}
for all the boundary strata of
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{i_{j}}/\mathbb{R}}
.

Let
\mathcal{S}_{j}=\mathcal{S}_{j-1}\cup\{\mathcal{M}_{i_{j}}\}
. We will use
\varepsilon_{j},\ell_{j}
for
K_{i_{j}}
such that
0<\varepsilon_{j}\leq\varepsilon_{j-1}
and
\varepsilon_{j-1},\ell_{j-1}
for all
K_{i}
with
\mathcal{M}_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{j-1}
.

Step
j1
.
Let
R_{j1}\geq R_{j-1}
be a constant larger than the gluing parameter bounds in Theorems
6.5.1
(A) and
6.5.3
for all possible gluings of the
V_{i}
(possibly with multiplicities) such that
\mathcal{M}_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{j}
, subject to the condition that
V_{i_{j}}
be used at most once.
If we increase
R_{j-1}
so that it equals
R_{j1}
, then we must enlarge
K_{i_{j}}
to
K_{i_{j}}^{\prime}
so that (
\dagger_{j}
) holds with gluing parameter bound
R_{j1}
. Using Step
j-1
, since gluing an element of
K_{i_{j}}^{\prime}-K_{i_{j}}
with elements of
\mathcal{S}_{j-1}
can be done with gluing parameter bound
R_{j-1}
, we can enlarge
K_{i_{j}}
to
K^{\prime}_{i_{j}}
without increasing
R_{j1}
, take
R_{j-1}=R_{j1}
, and then enlarge
K_{i}
to
K_{i}^{\prime}
whenever
\mathcal{M}_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{j-1}
.

If we take
R_{j1}
to be sufficiently large, then we may substitute
V_{i}^{\ell_{j-1},\varepsilon_{j}}
for
V_{i}^{\ell_{j-1},\varepsilon_{j-1}}
for all
\mathcal{M}_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{j-1}
as in Step
j-1
. We then reset notation and refer to the enlarged
K^{\prime}_{i}
as
K_{i}
.

Step
j2
.

Next let
R_{j2}
be a constant larger than the gluing parameter bounds in Theorems
6.5.1
(A) and
6.5.3
for all possible gluings of the
V_{i}
(possibly with multiplicities) such that
\mathcal{M}_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{j}
, subject to the condition that
V_{i_{j}}
be used at most twice
; we also require
R_{j2}\geq R_{j1}
. If we increase
R_{j-1}=R_{j1}
so that it equals
R_{j2}
, then we must enlarge
K_{i_{j}}
to
K_{i_{j}}^{\prime}
so that (
\dagger_{j}
) holds with gluing parameter bound
R_{j2}
. As before, since gluing an element of
K_{i_{j}}^{\prime}-K_{i_{j}}
with elements of
\mathcal{S}_{j}
, subject to the condition that
V_{i_{j}}
be used only one additional time, can be done with gluing parameter bound
R_{j1}
, we can enlarge
K_{i_{j}}
to
K^{\prime}_{i_{j}}
without increasing
R_{j2}
, take
R_{j1}=R_{j2}
, and then enlarge
K_{i}
to
K_{i}^{\prime}
whenever
\mathcal{M}_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{j-1}
. We then reset notation and refer to the enlarged
K^{\prime}_{i}
as
K_{i}
.

Step
jk
.

We can repeat this process, constructing
R_{j3},R_{j4},\dots
(the process terminates) and enlarging
K_{i_{j}}
and increasing the gluing parameter bounds such that
R_{j-1}=R_{j1}=R_{j2}=\dots=R_{j}
.

The
G_{i}
-invariance of
V_{i}
and
K_{i}
can be additionally imposed during the above construction.
∎

8.6.
Construction of the semi-global Kuranishi charts

8.6.1.
Complexity

The semi-global Kuranishi structure
\mathscr{K}^{L}(\alpha,J)
is constructed by induction on a triple which we call the
complexity

(8.6.1)

c(\mathcal{F},u)=(c_{1}(\mathcal{F},u),c_{2}(\mathcal{F},u),c_{3}(\mathcal{F},u))=(\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}(\gamma_{+}),E_{\alpha}(u),-\chi(\dot{F})),

where we are using lexicographic ordering. Here
u
is a map from
\gamma_{+}
to
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}
. The complexity
c(T)
of a CH tree
T
is the complexity of
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\tau(T)}/\mathbb{R}}
.

We choose
L\gg 0
and only consider
\gamma_{+}
satisfying
\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}(\gamma_{+})<L
. (Eventually we will take direct limits as
L\to\infty
.) We then choose a finite sequence
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2},\dots,\mathcal{M}_{\rho}
of distinct moduli spaces

\mathcal{M}_{i}=\mathcal{M}_{J}^{\operatorname{ind}=k_{i}}(\dot{F}_{i},\mathbb{R}\times M;\gamma_{i,+},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i,-}),

where the moduli spaces are ordered according to nondecreasing complexity.

We make some observations regarding the complexity: For a fixed
c_{1}
, the moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{i}
with the smallest
c_{2}
consist of branched covers of trivial cylinders, i.e., satisfy
E_{\alpha}(u)=0
.
We will not construct Kuranishi charts about trivial cylinders and hence trivial cylinders will not be included in the list.
There are finitely many
\gamma_{+}
and when
\gamma_{+}
is fixed and the genus is zero, there is an upper bound on the number of punctures, which in turn gives an upper bound on the number of branch points. We list the
\mathcal{M}_{i}
with the fewest number of branch points first. Similarly, for
E_{\alpha}(u)>0
, we list the
\mathcal{M}_{i}
with the smallest
E_{\alpha}(u)
first and use the fact that there is an upper bound on the number of punctures.

The following is immediate:

Lemma 8.6.1
.

Each component of each level of a building of
\partial\mathcal{M}_{i}
is either a trivial cylinder or in
\mathcal{M}_{j}
with
j<i
.

8.6.2.
Interior charts and multisections

By Lemma
8.5.2
there exist a “large” compact subset
K_{i}
of
\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,\rho\}
, a “universal” gluing parameter bound
R>0
, and constants
\varepsilon
and
\ell
, such that (i
S
) and (ii
S
) hold. In view of Remark
8.5.1
, we may take the large neck length bound
\mathcal{L}\gg 0
from Section
8.4
to be the universal gluing parameter bound
R\gg 0
.

With this choice of
\mathcal{L}
, we take the interior semi-global Kuranishi charts
\mathcal{C}_{i}=(\pi_{i}:E_{i}\to V_{i},\overline{\partial}_{i}=\overline{\partial}_{J},\psi_{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G_{i})}
for
K_{i}\subset\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}
and their slight enlargements
\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\operatorname{en}}=(\pi_{i}^{\operatorname{en}}:E_{i}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{i}^{\operatorname{en}},\overline{\partial}_{i}^{\operatorname{en}}=\overline{\partial}_{J},\psi_{i}^{\operatorname{en}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G_{i})}
as in Definition
8.4.2
.

Let
d_{i}
be a metric on
V_{i}^{\operatorname{en}}
which is compatible with the topology on
V_{i}^{\operatorname{en}}
; such a metric exists since manifolds are metrizable. By construction,
\overline{V}_{i}
is compact and hence bounded with respect to
d_{i}
.

We also choose an obstruction multisection
{\mathfrak{s}}_{i}
and its enlargement
\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{i}
, such that the collection
\{\mathfrak{s}_{i}\}_{i}
is invariant under marker rotations and puncture reorderings.

8.6.3.
Boundary strata

We now explain how to construct charts corresponding to the boundary strata.

Recall Conventions
6.1.1
and
8.0.1
. Given
\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}/\mathbb{R}
, its boundary
\partial(\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}/\mathbb{R})
is described as follows:

(8.6.2)

\partial(\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}/\mathbb{R})=\left(\coprod_{T}\mathcal{M}_{T}^{\prime}\right)/\sim,\quad\mathcal{M}_{T}^{\prime}:=\times_{v\in V(T)}(\mathcal{M}_{l_{V(T)}(v)}/\mathbb{R}),

where the disjoint union is over all CH trees
T
not equal to the single vertex tree
T_{0}=i_{0}
and such that
T/\!\!/T=i_{0}
and the equivalence relation
\sim
is induced by isomorphisms
\theta:T\xrightarrow{\sim}T^{\prime}
of CH trees. (To simplify notation, we will often write “
i\in V(T)
” with
i\in\{1,\dots,\rho\}
to mean that there is
v\in V(T)
with
l_{V(T)}(v)=i
.)

Let
T
be a CH tree such that
T/\!\!/T=i_{0}
. We have already constructed

\mathcal{C}_{i}=(\pi_{i}:E_{i}\to V_{i},\overline{\partial}_{i}=\overline{\partial}_{J},\psi_{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G_{i})}\quad\mbox{and}\quad K_{i},\quad\forall i\in V(T),

and the slight enlargement
\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\operatorname{en}}
.
In general, a slight enlargement will be denoted by adding a superscript
{}^{\operatorname{en}}
.
In this subsection we construct

\mathcal{C}_{T}=(\pi_{T}:E_{T}\to V_{T},\overline{\partial}_{T},\psi_{T},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G_{T/\!\!/T}(T)}),\quad K_{T}\subset\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}/\mathbb{R},

and the slight enlargement, such that
V_{T}\supset K_{T}
and
\{K_{T}~|~T/\!\!/T=i_{0}\}
covers all of
\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}/\mathbb{R}
.

Remark 8.6.2
.

Note that
K_{T}
is not necessarily compact,
although it admits a compactification inside
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}/\mathbb{R}}
.

Warning 8.6.3
.

Even if
\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}/\mathbb{R}=\varnothing
and the semi-global chart
\mathcal{C}_{i_{0}}
is empty, we need to construct
\mathcal{C}_{T}
if there exists a CH tree
T
such that

•

T/\!\!/T=i_{0}
and
\times_{i\in V(T)}(\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R})
is nonempty.

There are examples that the authors learned from Michael Hutchings, where omitting
\mathcal{C}_{T}
leads to some inconsistencies.

Remark 8.6.4
.

For our purposes it is not necessary to cover the actual boundary of the SFT compactification
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}/\mathbb{R}}
of
\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}/\mathbb{R}
with Kuranishi charts.

Let
\mathcal{G}_{\delta,T}=\mathcal{G}_{\delta}(\{V_{i}^{\text{en}}\}_{i\in V(T)})
be the set of equivalence classes of maps that are
\delta
-close to breaking into representatives of
V_{i}^{\text{en}}
, where the “gluing” is prescribed by the set of glued edges
G(T)
. Let
E^{\prime}
be the bundle over
\mathcal{G}_{\delta,T}
, whose fiber
E^{\prime}_{(\mathcal{F},u)}
over
(\mathcal{F},u)
is given by Equation (
6.4.2
) (note that the definition descends to the fiber
E^{\prime}_{[\mathcal{F},u]}
over the equivalence class
[\mathcal{F},u]
) and let

\mathcal{G}_{\delta,T}^{E^{\prime}}=\mathcal{G}_{\delta}^{E^{\prime}}(\{V_{i}^{\text{en}}\}_{i\in V(T)})=\{[\mathcal{F},u]\in\mathcal{G}_{\delta,T}~|~\overline{\partial}_{J}u\in E^{\prime}\}.

Definition 8.6.5

(Neck length functions)
.

We define the
neck length functions

\mathfrak{nl}_{e}([\mathcal{F},u]):{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta,T}^{E^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{R}^{+},\quad e\in G(T),

where
\mathfrak{nl}_{e}([\mathcal{F},u])
is the neck length of the annular component
A_{e}

of the
\varepsilon
-thin part of
g_{(\ddot{F},j)}
corresponding to
e
; if there is no such annular component
A_{e}
, we set
\mathfrak{nl}_{e}([\mathcal{F},u])=0
. We also write
\mathfrak{nl}_{e}(u)
when
\mathcal{F}
is implicit.

Definition 8.6.6

(
V_{T}
,
V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
,
V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
,
K_{T}
,
\pi_{T}:E_{T}\to V_{T}
,
\pi_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}:E_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
,
d_{T}
)
.

(1)

V_{T}
(resp.
V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
,
V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
) is the subset of
{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta,T}^{E^{\prime}}
consisting of
[\mathcal{F},u]
such that
\mathfrak{nl}_{e}(u)>\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
(resp.
>\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}-\tfrac{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2}
,
>\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}
) for all
e\in G(T)
and
\mathfrak{nl}<\mathcal{L}
(resp.
<\mathcal{L}+\tfrac{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2}
,
<\mathcal{L}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}
) for all other
\varepsilon
-thin annuli of
g_{(\ddot{F},j)}
.

(2)

K_{T}
is the subset of
(\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}/\mathbb{R})\cap{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta,T}^{E^{\prime}}
consisting of
[\mathcal{F},u]
such that
\mathfrak{nl}_{e}(u)\geq\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}+\tfrac{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2}
for all
e\in G(T)
and
\mathfrak{nl}\leq\mathcal{L}-\tfrac{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2}
for all other
\varepsilon
-thin annuli of
g_{(\ddot{F},j)}
.

(3)

\pi_{T}:E_{T}\to V_{T}
and
\pi_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}:E_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
are restrictions of
E^{\prime}\to\mathcal{G}_{\delta,T}^{E^{\prime}}
to
V_{T}
and
V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
.

(4)

d_{T}
is a metric on
V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
satisfying

d_{T}([\mathcal{F},u],[\mathcal{F}^{\prime},u^{\prime}])\geq\max_{e\in V(T)}|\mathfrak{nl}_{e}(u)-\mathfrak{nl}_{e}(u^{\prime})|.

We take
V_{T}
,
V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
,
V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
,
K_{T}
,
\pi_{T}:E_{T}\to V_{T}
, and
\pi_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}:E_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
to all be
G_{T/\!\!/T}(T)
-invariant.
If there is an isomorphism
\theta:T\xrightarrow{\sim}T^{\prime}
of CH trees, then we identify
\pi_{T}:E_{T}\to V_{T}
and
\pi_{T^{\prime}}:E_{T^{\prime}}\to V_{T^{\prime}}
via
\theta
.

We briefly discuss the construction of the metric
d_{T}
on
V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
. Since manifolds are metrizable, there exists a metric
d_{T}^{\prime}
on
V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
. We then take
d_{T}=d_{T}^{\prime}+\phi
, where
\phi:V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\times V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to\mathbb{R}^{\geq 0}
is the continuous function

\phi([\mathcal{F},u],[\mathcal{F}^{\prime},u^{\prime}])=\max_{e\in V(T)}|\mathfrak{nl}_{e}(u)-\mathfrak{nl}_{e}(u^{\prime})|.

Lemma 8.6.7
.

\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}/\mathbb{R}=\cup_{T/\!\!/T=i_{0}}K_{T}
.

Proof.

Let
[\mathcal{F},u]\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}/\mathbb{R}
.
By Definition
8.4.2
(
\mathcal{I}
2), if there is no annular component of
\operatorname{Thin}_{\epsilon}(\ddot{F},g_{\ddot{F}})
or all the annular components of
\operatorname{Thin}_{\epsilon}(\ddot{F},g_{\ddot{F}})
have neck length
\leq\mathcal{L}-\tfrac{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2}
, then
[\mathcal{F},u]\in K_{i_{0}}
. On the other hand, if there are annular components with neck length
>\mathcal{L}-\tfrac{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2}
, then the set of all such determines a CH tree
T
such that
T/\!\!/T=i_{0}
and
[\mathcal{F},u]\in K_{T}
by Definition
8.6.6
(2).
∎

8.6.4.
Morphisms

Let
T
be a CH tree,
S=\sqcup_{j=1}^{b}S_{j}
be a good subforest of
T
, and
T^{\prime}=T/\!\!/S
.
We define the morphism

\phi_{T^{\prime},T}:\mathcal{C}_{T^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{C}_{T},\quad\phi_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}:\mathcal{C}_{T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}}\to\mathcal{C}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}

as follows (we may take the morphism to be
G_{T/\!\!/T}(T)\cap G_{T^{\prime}/\!\!/T^{\prime}}(T^{\prime})
-invariant by construction): first restrict
\pi_{T^{\prime}}:E_{T^{\prime}}\to V_{T^{\prime}}
to

V_{T^{\prime},T}:=V_{T^{\prime}}\cap\{\mathcal{L}>\mathfrak{nl}_{e}>\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}~|~e\in G(S)\},

then apply stabilization (see Section
5.4.1
), and finally include into
\pi_{T}:E_{T}\to V_{T}
. The composition of the second and third steps is clearly an embedding of vector bundles and is denoted by
(\phi^{\sharp}_{T^{\prime},T},\phi^{\flat}_{T^{\prime},T})
.

The slight enlargement
V_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}
is given by

V_{T^{\prime},T}^{\operatorname{en}}:=V_{T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}}\cap\{\mathcal{L}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}>\mathfrak{nl}_{e}>\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}~|~e\in G(S)\},

and the embedding
((\phi^{\sharp}_{T^{\prime},T})^{\operatorname{en}},(\phi^{\flat}_{T^{\prime},T})^{\operatorname{en}})
of the slightly enlarged bundles is defined in the same way as
(\phi^{\sharp}_{T^{\prime},T},\phi^{\flat}_{T^{\prime},T})
.

Given good subforests
S^{\prime},S^{\prime\prime}\subset S
, one can easily verify the commutativity of the diagram:

(8.6.3)

\begin{diagram}

as well as the analogs for the slight enlargements.

8.6.5.
Examples

(A) Suppose that
V(T)=\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
and
G(T)=\{e=(i_{2},i_{1})\}
. Then there are two charts
\mathcal{C}_{T/\!\!/T}
and
\mathcal{C}_{T}
and a morphism
\mathcal{C}_{T/\!\!/T}\to\mathcal{C}_{T}
.

(B) Suppose that
V(T)=\{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}\}
and
G(T)=\{e_{1}=(i_{2},i_{1}),e_{2}=(i_{3},i_{2})\}
. Let

(8.6.4)

S^{\prime}=(\{i_{1},i_{2}\},\{e_{1}\}),\quad S^{\prime\prime}=(\{i_{2},i_{3}\},\{e_{2}\})

be good subtrees of
T
. There are four charts

\mathcal{C}_{T/\!\!/T},\quad\mathcal{C}_{T/\!\!/S^{\prime}},\quad\mathcal{C}_{T/\!\!/S^{\prime\prime}},\quad\mathcal{C}_{T}

for
\mathscr{K}(\mathcal{M}_{T/\!\!/T})
, given schematically in Figure
3
. The morphisms satisfy (
8.6.3
) with
S=T
.

Figure 3.
Schematic diagram of
V_{T/\!\!/T}
,
V_{T/\!\!/S^{\prime}}
,
V_{T/\!\!/S^{\prime\prime}}
, and
V_{T}
. The horizontal and vertical lines represent the
\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{1}}
- and
\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{2}}
-coordinate axes and we are considering the projections of the charts to the
(\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{1}},\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{2}})
-coordinate plane.

(C) Suppose that
V(T)=\{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}\}
and
G(T)=\{e_{1}=(i_{3},i_{1}),e_{2}=(i_{3},i_{2})\}
.
Let

(8.6.5)

S^{\prime}=(\{i_{1},i_{3}\},\{e_{1}\}),\quad S^{\prime\prime}=(\{i_{2},i_{3}\},\{e_{2}\})

be good subtrees of
T
. Case (C) is similar to Case (B) except that
\mathcal{M}_{i_{1}}
may equal
\mathcal{M}_{i_{2}}
, in which case
\operatorname{Aut}(T)
is nontrivial.

8.6.6.
Gluing maps

Let
S
be a good subforest of
T
and
\overrightarrow{T}=\overrightarrow{T}(S)=T_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{m}
. We construct
E_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\to V_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\times(\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}-\tfrac{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2},\infty)^{m-1}
from
\pi_{T_{i}}^{\operatorname{en}}:E_{T_{i}}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{T_{i}}^{\operatorname{en}}
,
i=1,\dots,m
, as in Section
8.3
(two paragraphs above Definition
8.3.3
).

We define the gluing map
(\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}},\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}})
as follows: The map

\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}:V_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\times(\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}-\tfrac{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2},\infty)^{m-1}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta,T}^{E^{\prime}},

is obtained from Theorem
6.5.1
. The map
\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}:E_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\to E^{\operatorname{en}}_{T}
is given by
\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}(x,\xi)=(\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}(x),\phi_{x}(\xi))
, where
\phi_{x}
canonically identifies the fibers over
x
and
\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}(x)
.

By Theorem
6.5.1
and our choice of gluing parameter bound
\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}-\tfrac{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2}
(see Definition
8.6.6
):

Theorem 8.6.8
.

(SC1)
(a) holds for
\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}
and

\operatorname{Im}\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}\supset V_{T}
.

Theorem
6.5.3
becomes:

Theorem 8.6.9
.

(SC1)
(c) holds for the collection of gluing maps
\{\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}\}_{\overrightarrow{T}}
.

8.7.
Multisections on products

Given a CH tree
T
, the goal of this subsection is to inductively define a multisection
{\mathfrak{s}}_{T}
of
E_{T}\to V_{T}
; the construction of its slight enlargement
\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
will be omitted.

8.7.1.
Melding

Let
X
be a smooth manifold,

C
be a submanifold of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
of dimension
m
with corners (e.g.,
[0,1]^{m}
,
[0,1)^{m}
,
[c,\infty)^{m}
) and
\pi:X\to C
be a smooth map. Let
R_{1},\dots,R_{a}
be submanifolds of
C
of dimension
m
with corners such that
\cup R_{i}=C
. For
\varepsilon_{0}>0
small, let
R_{i}^{\prime}\subset C
be an open set that contains the
\varepsilon_{0}
-neighborhood of
R_{i}
.

A
melding of a collection of functions
\{g_{i}:\pi^{-1}(R_{i}^{\prime})\to\mathbb{R}^{b}\}_{i=1}^{a}
with respect to
\pi:X\to C
,
\{R_{1},\dots,R_{a}\}
, and
\{R_{1}^{\prime},\dots,R_{a}^{\prime}\}
is a function
g:X\to\mathbb{R}^{b}
defined as follows: For each
i
, choose a smooth function
\phi_{i}:C\to[0,1]
which is equal to
1
on
R_{i}
and
0
outside of
R_{i}^{\prime}
and such that
\sum_{i}\phi_{i}>0
. (In other words, we are taking a specific partition of unity.) We then set

(8.7.1)

g=\frac{\sum_{i}\pi^{*}(\phi_{i})g_{i}}{\sum_{i}\pi^{*}(\phi_{i})}.

A melding of a collection of sections can be defined similarly.

Remark 8.7.1
.

When melding of a collection
\{\mathfrak{s}_{i}=(\mathfrak{s}_{i1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{in_{i}})\}_{i=1}^{a}
of lifted multisections, we do not meld all possible
a
-tuples
(\mathfrak{s}_{1j_{1}},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{aj_{a}})
of branches; instead we specify which
a
-tuples of branches are to be melded, based upon which branches are close to each other.

8.7.2.
Some more gluing maps

In this subsection we slightly reformulate the gluing maps from Section
8.6.6
.

Given a good subtree
S
of a CH tree
T
(in particular
S\not=\varnothing
by definition), we write
S^{\star}
for the CH tree

obtained from
S
by adding all
e\in E(T)
such that
i(e)\in V(S)
and viewing them as free edges. We similarly define
S^{\star}
for a good subforest
S
of
T
.

Let
S=\sqcup_{j=1}^{b}S_{j}
be a good subforest of
T
. Consider the bundle

E_{T,S}^{\operatorname{en}}:=(\times_{j}E_{S_{j}^{\star}}^{\operatorname{en}})\times(\times_{i\in V(T)-V(S)}E_{i}^{\operatorname{en}})\to V_{T,S}^{\operatorname{en}}:=(\times_{j}V_{S_{j}^{\star}}^{\operatorname{en}})\times(\times_{i\in V(T)-V(S)}V_{i}^{\text{en}}).

As in Section
8.6.3
, let
\mathcal{G}_{\delta,T,S}=\mathcal{G}_{\delta}(\{V_{S_{j}^{\star}}^{\operatorname{en}}\}_{j},\{V_{i}^{\operatorname{en}}\}_{i\in V(T)-V(S)})
be the set of equivalence classes of maps that are
\delta
-close to breaking into representatives of
V_{S_{j}^{\star}}^{\operatorname{en}}
and
V_{i}^{\operatorname{en}}
, where the “gluing” is prescribed by the set of glued edges
G(T)
. Let
E^{\prime}
be the bundle over
\mathcal{G}_{\delta,T,S}
, whose fiber
E^{\prime}_{(\mathcal{F},u)}
over
(\mathcal{F},u)
is given by Equation (
6.4.2
) (again the definition descends to the fiber
E^{\prime}_{[\mathcal{F},u]}
over the equivalence class
[\mathcal{F},u]
), and let

(8.7.2)

\mathcal{G}_{\delta,T,S}^{E^{\prime}}=\mathcal{G}_{\delta}^{E^{\prime}}(\{V_{S_{j}^{\star}}^{\operatorname{en}}\}_{j},\{V_{i}^{\operatorname{en}}\}_{i\in V(T)-V(S)})=\{[\mathcal{F},u]\in\mathcal{G}_{\delta,T,S}~|~\overline{\partial}_{J}u\in E^{\prime}\}.

There exists a gluing map

\mathfrak{G}_{T,S}^{\operatorname{en}}:V^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}_{T,S}\times(\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}-\tfrac{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2},\infty)^{|G(T)|-|G(S)|}\to\mathcal{G}_{\delta,T,S}^{E^{\prime}},

obtained from Theorem
6.5.1
, as well as
\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T,S}:E_{T,S}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\to E_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
as in Section
8.6.6
.

Remark 8.7.2
.

The gluing map
\mathfrak{G}^{\operatorname{en}}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S)}
from Section
8.6.6
agrees with
\mathfrak{G}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T,S^{c}}
, where
S^{c}
is a good subforest of
T
and
G(S^{c})=G(T)-G(S)
.

Theorem
6.5.3
can be rephrased as follows:

Theorem 8.7.3
.

Let
S=\sqcup_{j=1}^{a}S_{j}
and
S^{\prime}=\sqcup_{j=1}^{a}S_{j}^{\prime}
be good subforests of
T
such that
S_{j}^{\prime}
is a good subforest of
S_{j}^{\star}
. Then the gluing maps
\mathfrak{G}_{T,S^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}}
and
\mathfrak{G}_{T,S}^{\operatorname{en}}\circ(\times_{j=1}^{a}\mathfrak{G}_{S_{j}^{\star},S^{\prime}_{j}}^{\operatorname{en}},\operatorname{id})

are
C^{1}
-close with error
\to 0
as all the components of
(\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}-\tfrac{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2},\infty)^{|G(T)-G(S^{\prime})|}
go to
\infty
. (Here if
S_{j}=S_{j}^{\prime}
, then we take
\mathfrak{G}_{S_{j}^{\star},S^{\prime}_{j}}^{\operatorname{en}}=\operatorname{id}
.)

8.7.3.
Definition of the multisection
{\mathfrak{s}}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}

We have already constructed lifted multisections
\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{\operatorname{en}}
of
E_{i}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{i}^{\operatorname{en}}
that are
C^{1}
-close to the
0
-section and invariant under
G_{i}
. Assuming that we have already constructed all the
\mathfrak{s}_{S^{\star}}^{\operatorname{en}}
, where
S
ranges over all good subtrees of
T
such that
S^{\star}\not=T
, and all the
\mathfrak{s}_{T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}}
, where
T^{\prime}<T
, and we have invariance under marker rotations and puncture reorderings, we inductively construct the multisection
\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
by defining multisections on subsets of
V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
and applying the melding procedure.
By construction it will be clear that, starting with
\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{\operatorname{en}}
that are
C^{1}
-close to the
0
-section,
\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
can also be made
C^{1}
-close to the
0
-section.

We are still using
0<\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}\ll\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
from Section
8.4
.
We first define “rectangular” regions of
[\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\infty)^{|G(T)|}
of type
R_{i}
(appearing in the definition of melding in Section
8.7.1
): Let

R_{T,S,1}:=\{\mathcal{L}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}\geq\mathfrak{nl}_{e}\geq\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}~|~e\in G(S)\}\cap\{\mathfrak{nl}_{e}\geq\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}~|~e\in G(T)-G(S)\},

where
S\not=\varnothing
is a good subforest of
T
,
17
17

17

Note that we are using
\mathcal{L}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}
instead of the more natural
\mathcal{L}
. This is to give us some space so that Definition
8.3.6
holds.
and

R_{T,S,2}:=\{\mathcal{L}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\geq\mathfrak{nl}_{e}\geq\mathcal{L}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}~|~e\in G(S)\}\cap\{\mathfrak{nl}_{e}\geq\mathcal{L}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}~|~e\in G(T)-G(S)\},

where
S
is a good subforest of
T
. Also let
R^{\prime}_{T,S,j}
,
j=1,2
, be rectangular regions of type
R_{i}^{\prime}
obtained from

by extending each side by

Next we extend
\mathfrak{s}_{T/\!\!/S}^{\operatorname{en}}
to
\mathfrak{nl}_{T}^{-1}(R^{\prime}_{T,S,1})
such that:

(1)

if
S^{\prime}\subset S

;

\{\mathfrak{s}_{T/\!\!/S}^{\operatorname{en}}\}_{S}

\mathfrak{s}_{T/\!\!/S}^{\operatorname{en}}

, followed by a stabilization given in Section

and inclusion of

into
V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
.
(1) is made possible by the commutativity of Diagram (
8.6.3
).

Definition 8.7.4

(Multisection
\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
)
.

The multisection
{\mathfrak{s}}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
on
E_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
is the melding of the following multisections with respect to

\mathfrak{nl}_{T}=(\mathfrak{nl}_{e})_{\{e\in G(T)\}}:V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to(\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime},\infty)^{|G(T)|}

and the collections
\{R_{T,S,j}\}
and
\{R^{\prime}_{T,S,j}\}
:

(R1)

on
\mathfrak{nl}_{T}^{-1}(R^{\prime}_{T,S,1})
, take the stabilization
\operatorname{stab}_{T/\!\!/S,T}({\mathfrak{s}}_{T/\!\!/S}^{\operatorname{en}})
of
{\mathfrak{s}}_{T/\!\!/S}^{\operatorname{en}}
;

(R2)

on
\mathfrak{nl}_{T}^{-1}(R^{\prime}_{T,S,2})
such that
T/\!\!/S
is a not a one-vertex tree, take the restriction of
(\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{T,S}^{\operatorname{en}})_{*}\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T,S}:=\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{T,S}^{\operatorname{en}}\circ\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T,S}\circ(\mathfrak{G}_{T,S}^{\operatorname{en}})^{-1}
, where

\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T,S}=(\mathfrak{s}_{S_{j}^{\star}}^{\operatorname{en}})_{j}\times(\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{\operatorname{en}})_{i\in V(T)-V(S)};

(R3)

on
\mathfrak{nl}_{T}^{-1}(R^{\prime}_{T,S,2})
such that
T/\!\!/S
is a one-vertex tree, take an arbitrary multisection subject to invariance under marker rotations and puncture reorderings;

where the collections of branches to be melded must satisfy the following
consistency condition
:

(CC)

the branches must agree on the overlaps as we approach the “boundary”.

Melding smoothes out the multisections defined on overlapping rectangular regions. We assume that the smooth functions
\phi_{T,S,j}:R^{\prime}_{T,S,j}\to[0,1]
that appear in the definition of melding satisfy the following:

(1)

\phi_{T,S,1}=0
on
R^{\prime}_{T,S,1}\cap\{\mathfrak{nl}_{e}\geq\mathcal{L}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}+\varepsilon^{(4)}\mbox{ for some }e\in G(S)\}
,
where
0<\varepsilon^{(4)}\ll\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}
;

(2)

the collection
\{\phi_{T,S,j}\}
is invariant under marker rotations and puncture reorderings.

We say that two multisections
\mathfrak{s}
and
\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}
on a bundle
E\to V

are
C^{1}
-close
if there exists a large integer
m
such that both can be locally viewed as sections of
E^{m}\to V
which are
C^{1}
-close modulo reordering.
The following lemma roughly states that whenever the regions of type (R1) and/or (R2) overlap, the relevant multisections are sufficiently close for “long neck lengths” and that melding does not modify things much for “long neck lengths”.

Lemma 8.7.5
.

(1)

If
S^{\prime}\subset S
is a good subforest, then
\operatorname{stab}_{T/\!\!/S,T}({\mathfrak{s}}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T/\!\!/S})
and
\operatorname{stab}_{T/\!\!/S^{\prime},T}({\mathfrak{s}}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T/\!\!/S^{\prime}})
agree on the overlap of
\mathfrak{nl}_{T}^{-1}(R^{\prime}_{T,S,1})
and
\mathfrak{nl}_{T}^{-1}(R^{\prime}_{T,S^{\prime},1})
.

(2)

If
S^{\prime}\subsetneq S
is a good subforest, then
(\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{T,S}^{\operatorname{en}})_{*}\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T,S}
and
(\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{T,S^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}})_{*}\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T,S^{\prime}}
are
C^{1}
-close on the overlap of
\mathfrak{nl}_{T}^{-1}(R^{\prime}_{T,S,2})
and
\mathfrak{nl}_{T}^{-1}(R^{\prime}_{T,S^{\prime},2})
when all
\mathfrak{nl}_{e}
,
e\in G(T)-G(S)
, are sufficiently large.

(3)

(\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{T,S}^{\operatorname{en}})_{*}\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T,S}
and
\operatorname{stab}_{T/\!\!/S,T}({\mathfrak{s}}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T/\!\!/S})
are
C^{1}
-close on the overlap of
\mathfrak{nl}_{T}^{-1}(R^{\prime}_{T,S,1})
and
\mathfrak{nl}_{T}^{-1}(R^{\prime}_{T,S,2})
when all
\mathfrak{nl}_{e}
,
e\in G(T)-G(S)
, are sufficiently large.

Proof.

(1) has already been arranged.

(2) is a consequence of Theorem
8.7.3
and the inductive step: Let
S=\sqcup_{j=1}^{a}S_{j}
be a good subforest of
T
, let
S_{j}^{\prime}
be a good subforest of
S_{j}^{\star}
, and let
S^{\prime}=\sqcup_{j}S_{j}^{\prime}
.
By definition and Theorem
8.7.3
,

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{T,S}^{\operatorname{en}})_{*}\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T,S}

\displaystyle=(\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{T,S}^{\operatorname{en}})_{*}((\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{S^{\star}_{j}})_{j=1}^{a}\times(\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{i})_{i\in V(T)-V(S)}),

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{T,S^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}})_{*}\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T,S^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\approx(\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{T,S}^{\operatorname{en}})_{*}(((\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{S_{j}^{\star},S_{j}^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}})_{*}\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{S_{j}^{\star},S_{j}^{\prime}})_{j=1}^{a}\times(\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{i})_{i\in V(T)-V(S)}),

where
\approx
means
C^{1}
-close on their overlap. Since by induction we are assuming that
\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{S_{j}^{\star}}
and
(\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{S_{j}^{\star},S_{j}^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}})_{*}\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{S_{j}^{\star},S_{j}^{\prime}}
are
C^{1}
-close on their relevant overlaps, with improved accuracy as
\mathfrak{nl}_{e}\to\infty
for all
e\in G(S_{j}^{\star})-G(S_{j}^{\prime})
, (2) follows.

(3) By induction we are assuming that

(\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{S_{j}^{\star},S_{j}}^{\operatorname{en}})_{*}\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{S_{j}^{\star},S_{j}}=\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{S_{j}^{\star}}=\operatorname{stab}_{S_{j}^{\star}/\!\!/S_{j}^{\star},S_{j}^{\star}}({\mathfrak{s}}^{\operatorname{en}}_{S_{j}^{\star}/\!\!/S_{j}^{\star}})

on the overlap
\mathfrak{nl}_{S_{j}^{\star}}^{-1}(R^{\prime}_{S_{j}^{\star},S_{j},2})\cap\mathfrak{nl}_{S_{j}^{\star}}^{-1}(R_{S_{j}^{\star},S_{j},1})
.
Then (3) is a consequence of the commutativity of

(8.7.3)

\begin{diagram}

where the horizontal arrows are inclusions and the vertical arrows are gluing maps.
∎

Lemma 8.7.6
.

If
S=\sqcup_{j=1}^{a}S_{j}
is a good subforest of
T
, then on all of

R_{T,S}:=\{\mathfrak{nl}_{e}>\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}~|~e\in G(S)\}\cap\{\mathfrak{nl}_{e}\gg 0~|~e\in G(T)-G(S)\}

we have

(8.7.4)

\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\approx(\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{T,S}^{\operatorname{en}})_{*}((\mathfrak{s}_{S_{j}^{\star}}^{\operatorname{en}})_{j}\times(\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{\operatorname{en}})_{i\in V(T)-V(S)}).

Proof.

The lemma is proved by induction.
(
8.7.4
) holds on
R_{T,S,2}\cap R_{T,S}
by (R2) of Definition
8.7.4
and on
R_{T,S,1}\cap R_{T,S}
by (R1) and

\operatorname{stab}_{T/\!\!/S,T}({\mathfrak{s}}_{T/\!\!/S}^{\operatorname{en}})\approx(\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{T,S}^{\operatorname{en}})_{*}((\operatorname{stab}_{S_{j}^{\star}/\!\!/S_{j},S_{j}^{\star}}({\mathfrak{s}}_{S_{j}^{\star}/\!\!/S_{j}}^{\operatorname{en}}))_{j},(\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{{\operatorname{en}}})_{i\in V(T)-V(S)}).

For any
S^{\prime}=\sqcup_{j}S_{j}^{\prime}
as in the proof of Lemma
8.7.5
(2), (
8.7.4
) holds on
R_{T,S^{\prime},2}\cap R_{T,S}
since

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{T,S^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}})_{*}

\displaystyle((\mathfrak{s}_{(S^{\prime}_{j})^{\star}}^{\operatorname{en}})_{j},(\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{\operatorname{en}})_{i\in V(T)-V(S^{\prime})})

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{T,S}^{\operatorname{en}})_{*}(((\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}^{\operatorname{en}}_{S_{j}^{\star},S_{j}^{\prime}})_{*}(\mathfrak{s}_{(S^{\prime}_{j})^{\star}}^{\operatorname{en}},(\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{\operatorname{en}})_{i\in V(S_{j})-V(S^{\prime}_{j})}))_{j},(\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{\operatorname{en}})_{i\in V(T)-V(S)}).

by Theorem
8.7.3
and
\mathfrak{s}_{S_{j}^{\star}}\approx(\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}^{\operatorname{en}}_{S_{j}^{\star},S_{j}^{\prime}})_{*}(\mathfrak{s}_{(S^{\prime}_{j})^{\star}}^{\operatorname{en}},(\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{\operatorname{en}})_{i\in V(S_{j})-V(S^{\prime}_{j})})
by induction on
R_{S_{j}^{\star},S_{j}^{\prime}}.
The lemma follows by observing that the regions of the above three types cover
R_{T,S}
.
∎

Lemma 8.7.7
.

The collection
\{\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\}_{T}
satisfies the conditions of Definition
8.3.6
.

Proof.

(1) is immediate from (R1).
(2) follows from Remark
8.7.2
and Lemma
8.7.6
. (3) follows from the symmetry built into the construction.
∎

8.8.
Modifications when (H) does not hold

When
(H)
is not satisfied, we generally need to add or remove additional punctures to the domain Riemann surface when we define morphisms
\phi_{T^{\prime},T}:\mathcal{C}_{T^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{C}_{T}
.
In this subsection we describe how to modify the definition of the interior semi-global Kuranishi charts to accommodate the additional punctures.

Let
\delta>0
be a small constant as before, used when referring to curves that are close to breaking. Recall that we are taking the moduli spaces
\mathcal{M}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{M}_{\rho}
to have domains of the form
\dot{F}_{i}=F_{i}-{\bf p}_{i}
, i.e.,
we have not removed the punctures
{\bf q}_{i}
yet.

Initial step.
Suppose
\partial(\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}/\mathbb{R})=\varnothing
. Then there exists a small positive constant
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\varepsilon^{\prime}(\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}})>0
such that for each
[\mathcal{F},u]=[(F,j,{\bf p},{\bf r}),u]\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}
there exists a set of removable punctures
{\bf q}
as in Section
4.2.5
when
\chi(\dot{F})=0
or
1
. Let
K_{i_{0}}=\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}/\mathbb{R}
.
The only difference is that in the definition of
V_{i_{0}}

we use
s_{\pm,i}
that depends on
((F,j,{\bf p},{\bf q},{\bf r}),u)
instead of
(\mathcal{F},u)
.

Puncturing map.
Let
[\mathcal{F},u]=[(F,j,{\bf p},{\bf r}),u]\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}
be such that
(\mathcal{F},u)
is
\delta^{\prime}
-close to breaking into the building
\times_{i\in V(T)}(\mathcal{F}_{i},u_{i})
, as prescribed by a CH tree
T
such that
T/\!\!/T=i_{0}
, and that each
\mathcal{F}_{i}=(F_{i},j_{i},{\bf p}_{i},{\bf r}_{i})
has been assigned a set
{\bf q}_{i}
of removable punctures; we write
\mathcal{F}^{i}_{i}=(F_{i},j_{i},{\bf p}_{i},{\bf q}_{i},{\bf r}_{i})
by analogy with
\mathcal{F}^{T}
below. If
\delta^{\prime}>0
is sufficiently small, then there exists a set
{\bf q}^{T}=\sqcup_{i\in V(T)}{\bf q}_{i}^{\prime}
of removable punctures of
\mathcal{F}
that is defined using the analogs of Equations (
6.3.1
) and (
6.3.2
) and is close to
\sqcup_{i\in V(T)}{\bf q}_{i}
and we can define the following
puncturing map
:

\mathfrak{Punc}^{T}:(\mathcal{F},u)\mapsto(\mathcal{F}^{T}=(F,j,{\bf p},{\bf q}^{T},{\bf r}),u).

We further shrink
\delta^{\prime}>0
if necessary so that
(\mathcal{F}^{T},u)
is
\delta
-close to
\times_{i\in V(T)}(\mathcal{F}_{i}^{i},u)
.

Choose
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}
such that
0\ll\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\ll\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\ll\mathcal{L}
.

Warm-up.
We consider the simplest nontrivial case where

V(T)=\{i_{1},i_{2}\},\quad G(T)=\{e=(i_{2},i_{1})\},

\partial(\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}/\mathbb{R})=(\mathcal{M}_{i_{1}}/\mathbb{R})\times(\mathcal{M}_{i_{2}}/\mathbb{R}).

Let
\mathcal{B}_{i_{0}}
be the set of
[\mathcal{F},u]=[(F,j,{\bf p},{\bf r}),u]\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}
that are
\delta^{\prime}
-close to breaking into

[\mathcal{F}_{i_{1}},u_{i_{1}}]\times[\mathcal{F}_{i_{2}},u_{i_{2}}]\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{1}}\times\mathcal{M}_{i_{2}},~~\mathcal{F}_{i_{j}}=(F_{i_{j}},j_{i_{j}},{\bf p}_{i_{j}},{\bf r}_{i_{j}}),~~j=1,2,

where any of
\chi(\dot{F}),\chi(\dot{F}_{i_{1}}),\chi(\dot{F}_{i_{2}})
may be nonnegative. Given
[\mathcal{F},u]\in\mathcal{B}_{i_{0}}
, let
(\mathcal{F}^{T},u)=\mathfrak{Punc}^{T}(\mathcal{F},u)
so that
(\mathcal{F}^{T},u)
is
\delta
-close to breaking into
(\mathcal{F}_{i_{1}}^{i_{1}},u_{1})\cup(\mathcal{F}_{i_{2}}^{i_{2}},u_{2})
. We also choose a sufficiently large compact set
K_{i_{0}}\subset\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}
such that
K_{i_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{i_{0}}=\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}
and a small constant
\varepsilon^{\prime}(K_{i_{0}})>0
to define the removable punctures
{\bf q}
for all
[\mathcal{F},u]\in K_{i_{0}}
.

Consider the two hyperbolic metrics

g^{T/\!\!/T}:=g_{(\dot{F}-{\bf q},j)},\quad g^{T}:=g_{(\dot{F}-{\bf q}^{T},j)}

where they are defined.
Let
\mathfrak{nl}_{e}^{T}(\mathcal{F},u)
be the neck length function defined using
g^{T}
and let
s_{\pm,j}^{T/\!\!/T}(\mathcal{F},u)
be
s_{\pm,j}(\mathcal{F},u)
defined using
g^{T/\!\!/T}
.

For each
a<b
, choose a smooth nondecreasing function
\lambda_{a,b}:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
such that
\lambda_{a,b}(a)=0
and
\lambda_{a,b}(b)=1
and then set
\lambda=\lambda_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
.

We now state the modifications (Mod
1
) and (Mod
2
) in the processes of trimming and constructing the obstruction bundle:

(Mod
1
)

In the definitions of
K_{i_{0}}
and
V_{i_{0}}
(see Definition
8.4.2
), the neck length
\mathfrak{nl}_{e}(\mathcal{F},u)
is taken to be
\mathfrak{nl}_{e}^{T}(\mathcal{F},u)
if
(\mathcal{F}^{T},u)
is
\delta
-close to breaking and is taken to be zero otherwise. (In particular,
K_{i_{0}}
is chosen sufficiently large so that all the curves
(\mathcal{F},u)
with
\mathfrak{nl}_{e}^{T}(\mathcal{F},u)\leq\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}
are contained.)

(Mod
2
)

E_{(\mathcal{F},u)}
is defined as in Step 2 of Theorem
5.1.2
but using
\widetilde{s}_{\pm,j}(\mathcal{F},u)
, which in turn is defined as:

(a)

s_{\pm,j}^{T/\!\!/T}(\mathcal{F},u)
on
\{\mathfrak{nl}_{e}^{T}\leq\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\}
and
s_{\pm,j}^{T}(\mathcal{F},u)
on
\{\mathfrak{nl}_{e}^{T}\geq\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\}
; and

(b)

the interpolation
(1-\lambda(\mathfrak{nl}_{e}^{T}))s_{\pm,j}^{T/\!\!/T}(\mathcal{F},u)+\lambda(\mathfrak{nl}_{e}^{T})s_{\pm,j}^{T}(\mathcal{F},u),
on
\{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\leq\mathfrak{nl}_{e}^{T}\leq\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\}
.

On
V_{i_{0}}
the bundle
E_{i_{0}}
is obtained from the bundle with fibers
E_{(\mathcal{F},u)}
; on
V_{i_{0},T}
, still defined using the parameter
\mathcal{L}
, the bundle
E_{T}
agrees with the stabilization
E^{\prime}_{(\mathcal{F},u)}
of
E_{(\mathcal{F},u)}
, constructed as in Equation (
6.4.2
) using
g^{T}
.

General case.
We will describe a neighborhood of the corner of
V_{i_{0}}
given by the CH tree
T
such that
i_{0}=T/\!\!/T
. Let
(\mathcal{F},u)
be as in the “Puncturing map” paragraph and
and let
(\mathcal{F}^{T},u)=\mathfrak{Punc}^{T}(\mathcal{F},u).
As before, if
\mathcal{B}_{i_{0}}
is the set of curves that are
\delta^{\prime}
-close to breaking, then we choose a sufficiently large compact set
K_{i_{0}}\subset\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}
such that
K_{i_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{i_{0}}=\mathcal{M}_{i_{0}}
and a small constant
\varepsilon^{\prime}(K_{i_{0}})>0
to define the removable punctures
{\bf q}
for all
[\mathcal{F},u]\in K_{i_{0}}
.

Let
g^{T/\!\!/T}
be the hyperbolic metric
g_{(\dot{F}-{\bf q},j)}
and, for a good subforest
S
of
T
such that
T/\!\!/S\not=T/\!\!/T
, let
g^{T/\!\!/S}
be the hyperbolic metric
g_{(\dot{F}-{\bf q}^{T/\!\!/S},j)}
, where the set of removable punctures
{\bf q}^{T/\!\!/S}
is as in the definition of
\mathfrak{Punc}^{T/\!\!/S}
. Let
\mathfrak{nl}^{T/\!\!/S}_{e}(\mathcal{F},u)
be the neck length function defined using
g^{T/\!\!/S}
for
e\not\in E(S)
and let
s_{\pm,j}^{T/\!\!/S}(\mathcal{F},u)
be
s_{\pm,j}(\mathcal{F},u)
defined using
g^{T/\!\!/S}
. Also let
e^{C}
(“the complement of
e
”) be the good subforest of
T
consisting of glued edges
\not=e
.

(Mod
1
)

In the definitions of
K_{i_{0}}
and
V_{i_{0}}
(see Definition
8.4.2
), the neck length
\mathfrak{nl}_{e}(\mathcal{F},u)
is taken to be
\mathfrak{nl}_{e}^{T/\!\!/e^{C}}(\mathcal{F},u)
if
(\mathcal{F}^{T/\!\!/e^{C}},u)
is
\delta
-close to breaking into a building with a long neck corresponding to
e
and is taken to be zero otherwise.

(Mod
2
)

E_{(\mathcal{F},u)}
is defined as in Step 2 of Theorem
5.1.2
but using
\widetilde{s}_{\pm,j}(\mathcal{F},u)
, which is given as follows:
for good subforests
S^{\prime}\subset S
of
T
, the interpolation

\sum_{S^{\prime}\subset S^{\prime\prime}\subset S}\left(\prod_{e\in E(S^{\prime\prime})-E(S^{\prime})}(1-\lambda(\mathfrak{nl}^{T/\!\!/e^{C}}_{e}))\prod_{e\in E(S)-E(S^{\prime\prime})}\lambda(\mathfrak{nl}^{T/\!\!/e^{C}}_{e})\right)s_{\pm,j}^{T/\!\!/S^{\prime\prime}}(\mathcal{F},u),

where the summation is over all good subforests
S^{\prime\prime}
such that
S^{\prime}\subset S^{\prime\prime}\subset S
,
on

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\leq\mathfrak{nl}_{e}^{T/\!\!/e^{C}}\leq\mathcal{L}^{\prime}~|~e\in E(S)-E(S^{\prime})\}\cap\{\mathfrak{nl}^{T/\!\!/e^{C}}_{e}>\mathcal{L}^{\prime}~|~e\not\in E(S)\}

\displaystyle\cap\{\mathfrak{nl}^{T/\!\!/e^{C}}_{e}\leq\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}~|~e\in E(S^{\prime})\}.

Near each corner/edge corresponding to
T
with
T/\!\!/T=i_{0}
on
V_{i_{0}}
the bundle
E_{i_{0}}
is obtained from the bundle with fibers
E_{(\mathcal{F},u)}
as above after quotienting by domain automorphisms. The definitions of the edges and corners of
E_{i_{0}}
and
V_{i_{0}}
corresponding to the various
T
with
T/\!\!/T=i_{0}
agree by construction. This gives the interior obstruction bundles
\pi_{i_{0}}:E_{i_{0}}\to V_{i_{0}}
and hence the interior semi-global Kuranishi charts. The slight enlargements
\pi_{i_{0}}^{\operatorname{en}}:E_{i_{0}}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{i_{0}}^{\operatorname{en}}
can be defined in the same manner.

Example (B).
Figure
5
corresponds to Example (B) in Section
8.6.5
.
The numbers indicate the regions corresponding to the following: (1)
s^{T/\!\!/T}_{\pm,j}(\mathcal{F},u)
, (2)

, (3)
s^{T/\!\!/S^{\prime\prime}}_{\pm,j}(\mathcal{F},u)
, (4)

\displaystyle(1-\lambda(\mathfrak{nl}^{T/\!\!/e_{1}^{C}}_{e_{1}}))(1-\lambda(\mathfrak{nl}^{T/\!\!/e_{2}^{C}}_{e_{2}}))s^{T/\!\!/T}+\lambda(\mathfrak{nl}^{T/\!\!/e_{1}^{C}}_{e_{1}})(1-\lambda(\mathfrak{nl}^{T/\!\!/e_{2}^{C}}_{e_{2}}))s^{T/\!\!/S^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle+(1-\lambda(\mathfrak{nl}^{T/\!\!/e_{1}^{C}}_{e_{1}}))\lambda(\mathfrak{nl}^{T/\!\!/e_{2}^{C}}_{e_{2}})s^{T/\!\!/S^{\prime}}+\lambda(\mathfrak{nl}^{T/\!\!/e_{1}^{C}}_{e_{1}})\lambda(\mathfrak{nl}^{T/\!\!/e_{2}^{C}}_{e_{2}})s^{T}.

The horizontal and vertical lines represent the

- and

-coordinate axes and we are considering the projections to the

The bundles
\pi_{T}:E_{T}\to V_{T}
and their enlargements are defined in the same way as in Section

, the morphisms

8.9.
Verification of
(K1)
–
(K8)
and
(SC1)
,
(SC2)

We now verify that
(K1)
–
(K8)
and
(SC1)
,
(SC2)
hold for our semi-global Kuranishi structure that we constructed in Sections

–
8.8
.

(K1)
The Kuranishi charts were constructed in Sections
8.6.2
(defined in Definition
8.4.2
) and
8.6.3
(defined in Definition
8.6.6
).
(K1)
(a) and (c) are immediate from the construction and the first line of

8.6.6
(4).

(K2)
The morphisms were defined in Section
8.6.4
.
(K2)
(a)–(f) are easy consequences of the definition. The unique morphism property is a consequence of the commutativity of (
8.6.3
).

(K3)
is immediate.

(K4)
is immediate from the definition of the morphisms.

(K5)
follows from Lemma
8.6.7
.

(K6)
–
(K8)
The symmetries are built into the construction. The interior charts satisfy Definition
8.4.2
(
\mathcal{I}
3). The boundary charts are defined using the interior charts and the neck length function, and the latter is geometrically defined (i.e., defined using hyperbolic geometry) and commutes with marker rotations and puncture reorderings.

(SC1)
The gluing maps
\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}
were defined in Section
8.6.6
.
(SC1)

8.6.8
and
(SC1)

. The first statement of

, and the second statement of
(SC1)

and in particular the fact that the error (given by the left-hand side of (

) in the case of a two-level gluing) goes to zero as the gluing parameters go to
\infty
.

(SC2)
The symmetries are built into the construction.

8.10.
Cobordisms

In this subsection we describe the modifications needed for the cobordism maps in Section
9.2
.

Let
(\widehat{W}=W\cup([1,\infty)\times M_{+})\cup((-\infty,-1]\times M_{-}),\widehat{\alpha})
be the completion of the compact Liouville cobordism
(W,\alpha)
from
(M_{+},\alpha_{+})
to
(M_{-},\alpha_{-})
as in Section
3.1
. Given
L_{+}\leq L_{-}
, let
J
be an almost complex structure on
\widehat{W}
which restricts to
J_{+}
at the positive end and to
J_{-}
at the negative end. Suppose
(\widehat{\alpha},J)
is an
(L_{+},L_{-})
-simple pair and
J
is
(L_{+},L_{-})
-end-generic.

8.10.1.
Mixed curves

We first define a slightly unusual, infinite-dimensional, space
\mathcal{M}_{J}^{\operatorname{ind}=k}(\dot{F}^{\prime},\widehat{W};\gamma;\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+}\sqcup\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-})
of
J
-holomorphic maps
u
. Strictly speaking
u
does not need to be
J
-holomorphic, provided (iii) and (iv) below hold; in other words, it suffices to keep track of the data of the ends of
u
and the Fredholm index of the completion
\tilde{u}
, defined below.

Let
\gamma
be a Reeb orbit for
\alpha_{+}
and let
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+}=(\gamma_{+,1},\dots,\gamma_{+,l_{+}})
and
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}=(\gamma_{-,1},\dots,\gamma_{-,l_{-}})
be ordered tuples of Reeb orbits for
\alpha_{+}
and
\alpha_{-}
.
Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{J}^{\operatorname{ind}=k}(\dot{F}^{\prime},\widehat{W};\gamma;\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+}\sqcup\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-})

\displaystyle=\{\mbox{$J$-holomorphic maps $u:(\dot{F}^{\prime},j)\to(\widehat{W},J)$}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mbox{of ``Fredholm index'' $k$ satisfying (i)--(iv)}\},

where:

(i)

\dot{F}=F-\{p\}-\bf p_{+}-\bf p_{-}
, where
(F,j)
is a closed Riemann surface and
\{p\}
,
{\bf p}_{+}=\{p_{+,1},\dots,p_{+,l_{+}}\}
, and
{\bf p}_{-}=\{p_{-,1},\dots,p_{-,l_{-}}\}
are disjoint sets of punctures;
