(ii)

\dot{F}^{\prime}
is
\dot{F}
minus the union of closed disk neighborhoods
N(p_{+,i})
of
p_{+,i}
that are mutually disjoint and disjoint from the other punctures;

(iii)

u
is asymptotic to
\gamma
at the positive end near
p
and to
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}
at the negative end near
\bf p_{-}
;

(iv)

there exists an oriented parametrization
\mathbb{R}/\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{+}}(\gamma_{+,i})\mathbb{Z}
of
\partial N(p_{+,i})
(here we are using the orientation as the boundary of
N(p_{+,i})
and simple coordinates for
\gamma_{+,i}
) such that

u|_{\partial N(p_{+,i})}:\mathbb{R}/\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{+}}(\gamma_{+,i})\mathbb{Z}\to[1,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}/\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{+}}(\gamma_{+,i})\mathbb{Z}\times D_{\delta}

is
\delta
-close to the map
t\mapsto(s_{i},t,0)
for some
s_{i}\in[2,\infty)
,

and the “Fredholm index” of
u
is the Fredholm index of the completion
\tilde{u}
, obtained from
u
by attaching cylindrical ends that are close to
(-\infty,s_{i}]\times\gamma_{+,i}\subset(-\infty,s_{i}]\times M_{+}
and asymptotic to
\gamma_{+,i}
as
s\to-\infty
.
The curve
\tilde{u}
does not exist in
\widehat{W}
.

As before, we choose an ordering
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2},\dots,\mathcal{M}_{\rho}
of moduli spaces where each
\mathcal{M}_{i}
is one of

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{i}^{J}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}_{J}^{\operatorname{ind}=k_{i}}(\dot{F}_{i},\widehat{W};\gamma_{i,+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i,-}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{i}^{J_{\pm}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}_{J_{\pm}}^{\operatorname{ind}=k_{i}}(\dot{F}_{i},\mathbb{R}\times M_{\pm};\gamma_{i,+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i,-}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{i}^{J,\operatorname{Mixed}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}_{J}^{\operatorname{ind}=k_{i}}(\dot{F}_{i}^{\prime},\widehat{W};\gamma_{i};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i,+}\sqcup\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i,-}).

Here
\dot{F}_{i}
is a connected planar surface,
\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{\pm}}(\gamma_{i,+})\leq L_{\pm}
, and each component of each level of a building of
\partial\mathcal{M}_{i}
is either a trivial cylinder or in
\mathcal{M}_{j}
with
j<i
(for the moment we take the definition of
\partial\mathcal{M}_{i}
to be the set of curves that are close to breaking into curves in
\mathcal{M}_{i}^{J},\mathcal{M}_{i}^{J_{\pm}},\mathcal{M}_{i}^{J,\operatorname{Mixed}}
). The moduli spaces
\mathcal{M}_{i}^{J},\mathcal{M}_{i}^{J_{\pm}},\mathcal{M}_{i}^{J,\operatorname{Mixed}}
are said to be
of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type,
\operatorname{(Symp)}
type, and
\operatorname{(Mixed)}
type,
respectively.

Let us assume that Condition (H) holds.

8.10.2.
CH trees of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type

Definition 8.10.1

(CH tree of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type)
.

A
CH tree
T
of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type
is defined in the same way as a CH tree of
\operatorname{(Symp)}
type, with the following extra data:

(1)

There is a splitting
V(T)=V^{\operatorname{Cob}}(T)\sqcup V^{\operatorname{Symp}}(T)\sqcup V^{\operatorname{Mixed}}(T)
and the elements of
V^{\operatorname{Cob}}(T),V^{\operatorname{Symp}}(T),V^{\operatorname{Mixed}}(T)
are called
\operatorname{(Cob)}
vertices,
\operatorname{(Symp)}
vertices, and
\operatorname{(Mixed)}
vertices
, respectively.

(2)

Each maximal directed path of the direct tree passes through precisely one
\operatorname{(Cob)}
vertex or at least one
\operatorname{(Mixed)}
vertex. Every
\operatorname{(Mixed)}
vertex has a directed path to some
\operatorname{(Cob)}
vertex.

(3)

A
\operatorname{(Cob)}
vertex (resp. a
\operatorname{(Symp)}
vertex sitting above some
\operatorname{(Cob)}
or
\operatorname{(Mixed)}
vertex, a
\operatorname{(Symp)}
which does not sit above any
\operatorname{(Cob)}
or
\operatorname{(Mixed)}
vertex, a
\operatorname{(Mixed)}
vertex) is labeled by an index corresponding to a moduli space of type
\mathcal{M}_{i}^{J}
(resp
\mathcal{M}_{i}^{J_{+}}
,
\mathcal{M}_{i}^{J_{-}}
,
\mathcal{M}_{i}^{J,\operatorname{Mixed}}
).

Definition 8.10.2
.

Let
T
be a CH tree of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type
and let
S
be a subtree of
T
.

(1)

S
is a
good subtree of
T
if it has no free edges and is either a
1
-vertex tree with a
\operatorname{(Mixed)}
vertex or has at least one glued edge.

(2)

S
is
of
\operatorname{(Symp)}
type
if
S
has only
\operatorname{(Symp)}
vertices.

(3)

S
is
of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type
if
S
has at least one
\operatorname{(Cob)}
vertex and every
\operatorname{(Cob)}
vertex of
T
that is reached by a directed path from a vertex in
S
is also a vertex of
S
. (Note that it is possible for the root of
S
to be a
\operatorname{(Mixed)}
vertex.)

(4)

S
is
incomplete
if it is neither of
\operatorname{(Symp)}
type nor of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type.

A disjoint union
S=\sqcup_{i}S_{i}
of good subtrees of
T
is a
good subforest of
T
. It is of
\operatorname{(Symp})
(resp.
\operatorname{(Cob})
) type if all the
S_{i}
are of
\operatorname{(Symp)}
(resp.
\operatorname{(Cob)}
) type. The
closure
Cl(S)
of a good subforest
S
is the smallest good subforest in
T
containing
S
such that all of its trees are subtrees of
\operatorname{(Symp)}
or
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type.

Definition 8.10.3
.

An
isomorphism
of CH trees of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type is defined in the same way as an isomorphism of CH trees of
\operatorname{(Symp)}
type, except that we additionally require that
\operatorname{(Symp)}
vertices be taken to
\operatorname{(Symp)}
vertices,
\operatorname{(Cob)}
vertices to
\operatorname{(Cob)}
vertices, and
\operatorname{(Mixed)}
vertices to
\operatorname{(Mixed)}
vertices.

Main difference between the
\operatorname{(Symp)}
and
\operatorname{(Cob)}
cases.
In the
\operatorname{(Symp)}
case,
\mathfrak{nl}_{e}
for all the glued edges
e\in G(T)
are independent. The main difference with the
\operatorname{(Cob)}
case is that for any two directed paths
\gamma_{1}
,
\gamma_{2}
from the top vertex to a
\operatorname{(Cob)}
vertex,
\mathfrak{nl}_{\gamma_{1}}
and
\mathfrak{nl}_{\gamma_{2}}
are dependent, where

(8.10.1)

\mathfrak{nl}_{\gamma}:=\sum_{e\in G(\gamma)}\mathfrak{nl}_{e}.

This motivates the definitions in the following subsections.

8.10.3.
Decomposition of
\mathcal{M}_{i}
of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type

For
\mathcal{M}_{i}
of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type, we define the subset
\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\dagger}
consisting of
[\mathcal{F},u]
for which

(
\dagger
)

there exists a path from the positive puncture of
\ddot{F}
to a negative puncture which does not pass through an annular component
A
of
\operatorname{Thin}_{\varepsilon}(\ddot{F},g_{(\ddot{F},j)})
with
\mathfrak{nl}\geq\mathcal{L}
. (By Assumption (H),
\ddot{F}=\dot{F}
.)

Note that this does not preclude all annular components
A
with
\mathfrak{nl}\geq\mathcal{L}
. In fact, for
[\mathcal{F},u]\in\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\dagger}
, let
\{A_{i}\}_{i}
be the set of annular components of
\operatorname{Thin}_{\varepsilon}(\ddot{F},g_{(\ddot{F},j)})
with
\mathfrak{nl}\geq\mathcal{L}
. Then the restriction of
u
to the component of
\ddot{F}-\sqcup_{i}A_{i}
containing the positive puncture can be viewed as an element of some
\mathcal{M}_{i}^{J,\operatorname{Mixed}}
by (
\dagger
).

We then view
\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\dagger}
as the union
\cup_{T^{\prime}}\mathcal{M}_{T^{\prime}}^{\dagger}
, where the tree
T^{\prime}
encodes some topological data of
[\mathcal{F},u]
and is constructed as follows: For each component
C
of
\ddot{F}-\sqcup_{i}A_{i}
, view the restriction
u|_{C}
as an element of type
\mathcal{M}_{j(C)}^{J}
,
\mathcal{M}_{j(C)}^{J_{\pm}}
, or
\mathcal{M}_{j(C)}^{J,\operatorname{Mixed}}
and convert
C
into a vertex with label
j(C)
. Also convert each
A_{i}
into a glued edge. The negative ends of
u
become the free edges. By the previous paragraph, the root of
T^{\prime}
must be a
\operatorname{(Mixed)}
vertex.

We now consider the contraction
T/\!\!/S
of a CH tree
T
of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type along a good subforest
S=\sqcup_{i}S_{i}
of
T
. For each
S_{i}
we replace
S_{i}
by a vertex which is labeled
\tau(S_{i})
. There are two cases to consider:

(1)

If
S_{i}
is of
\operatorname{(Symp)}
(resp.
\operatorname{(Cob)}
) type, then
\tau(S_{i})
is a label for a
\operatorname{(Symp)}
(resp.
\operatorname{(Cob)}
) vertex as in Definition
8.1.8
(1).

(2)

If
S_{i}
is incomplete, then
\tau(S_{i})
is a label for a
\operatorname{(Mixed)}
vertex corresponding to
\mathcal{M}_{T^{\prime}}^{\dagger}
whose topological type is that of a curve obtained from pregluing the curves
[\mathcal{F}_{v},u_{v}]
,
v\in V(S_{i})
, according to the prescription given by
G(S_{i})
.

The rest of Definition
8.1.8
carries over.

8.10.4.
Kuranishi charts

Definition
8.3.1
carries over to the definition of a semi-global Kuranishi structure of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type after modifying
(SC1)
and
(SC2)
in Section
8.10.7
. In this subsection we describe the Kuranishi charts and morphisms of the semi-global Kuranishi structure
\mathscr{K}^{(L_{+},L_{-})}(\widehat{\alpha},J)
of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type, leaving the analogs of
(SC1)
and
(SC2)
to Sections
8.10.6
and
8.10.7
.

When
\mathcal{M}_{i}
is of
\operatorname{(Symp)}
type, the interior semi-global Kuranishi chart
\mathcal{C}_{i}=(\pi_{i}:E_{i}\to V_{i},\overline{\partial}_{i},\psi_{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G_{i}})
for
K_{i}\subset\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}
satisfies Definition
8.4.2
from Section
8.4
; in particular
K_{i}
(resp.
V_{i}
) consists of
[\mathcal{F},u]
in
\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}
(resp. in a neighborhood of
\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}
) for which there are no annular components in
\operatorname{Thin}_{\varepsilon}(\ddot{F},g_{(\ddot{F},j)})
or all annular components have
\mathfrak{nl}\leq\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}
(resp.
\mathfrak{nl}<\mathcal{L}
).

When
\mathcal{M}_{i}
is of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type, then we simply replace
\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}
by
\mathcal{M}_{i}
in Definition
8.4.2
to obtain a Kuranishi chart
\mathcal{C}_{i}=(\pi_{i}:E_{i}\to V_{i},\overline{\partial}_{i},\psi_{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}G_{i}})
for
K_{i}\subset\mathcal{M}_{i}
.

If
T
is a CH tree of
\operatorname{(Symp)}
type, then the corresponding charts were given in Definition
8.6.6
. Now let
T
be a CH tree of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type. Then the following definition is the analog of Definition
8.6.6
:

Definition 8.10.4

(
V_{T}
,
V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
,
V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
,
K_{T}
,
\pi_{T}:E_{T}\to V_{T}
,
\pi_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}:E_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
)
.

First suppose that the root vertex
v
of
T
is a
\operatorname{(Mixed)}
vertex and
Cl(\{v\})^{\star}=T
. Then:

(1)

K_{T}
is the compact subset of
\mathcal{M}_{T/\!\!/T}
consisting of
[\mathcal{F},u]
such that
\mathfrak{nl}_{e}(u)\geq\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}+\tfrac{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2}
for all
e\in G(T)
and
\mathfrak{nl}\leq\mathcal{L}-\tfrac{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2}
for all other
\varepsilon
-thin annuli of
g_{(\ddot{F},j)}
.

(2)

V_{T}
(resp.
V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
,
V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
) is the set of equivalence classes of maps
[\mathcal{F},u]
in a neighborhood of
\mathcal{M}_{T/\!\!/T}
such that
\mathfrak{nl}_{e}(u)>\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
(resp.
>\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}-\tfrac{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2}
,
>\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}
) for all
e\in G(T)
,
\mathfrak{nl}(u)<\mathcal{L}
(resp.
<\mathcal{L}+\tfrac{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2}
,
<\mathcal{L}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}
) for all other
\varepsilon
-thin annuli of
g_{(\ddot{F},j)}
, and
\overline{\partial}_{J}u\in E^{\prime}_{(\mathcal{F},u)}
.

(3)

For
[\mathcal{F},u]\in V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
, as in Equation (
6.4.2
) we are setting

E^{\prime}_{(\mathcal{F},u)}:=\oplus_{C}E^{\ell,\varepsilon}_{(\mathcal{F},u),C},

where
C
ranges over the boundary components of the
\varepsilon
-thin annuli corresponding to the edges of
G(T)
and the
\varepsilon
-thin cusps of
g_{(\ddot{F},j)}
, and
E^{\ell,\varepsilon}_{(\mathcal{F},u),C}
is as in Section
6.4
. The bundles
\pi_{T}:E_{T}\to V_{T}
and
\pi_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}:E_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
have fibers
E^{\prime}_{(\mathcal{F},u)}
.

In general view
V^{\operatorname{Mixed}}(T)
as a subforest
S
of
T
and write
Cl(S)=\sqcup_{j}\widetilde{S}_{j}
. Recalling
\widetilde{S}_{j}^{\star}
from Section
8.7.2
, let

\mathcal{G}_{\delta,T}:=\mathcal{G}_{\delta}(\{V_{\widetilde{S}_{j}^{\star}}^{\text{en}}\}_{j},\{V_{i}^{\text{en}}\}_{i\in V(T)-V(Cl(S))})

be the set of equivalence classes of maps that are
\delta
-close to breaking into representatives of
V_{\widetilde{S}_{j}^{\star}}^{\text{en}}
and
V_{i}^{\text{en}}
, where the gluing is prescribed by
G(T)
, let
E^{\prime}
be the bundle over
\mathcal{G}_{\delta,T}
whose fiber
E^{\prime}_{(\mathcal{F},u)}
over
(\mathcal{F},u)
is given by (3) above, and let

\mathcal{G}^{E^{\prime}}_{\delta,T}:=\mathcal{G}_{\delta}^{E^{\prime}}(\{V_{\widetilde{S}_{j}^{\star}}^{\text{en}}\}_{j},\{V_{i}^{\text{en}}\}_{i\in V(T)-V(Cl(S))})=\{[\mathcal{F},u]\in\mathcal{G}_{\delta,T}~|~\overline{\partial}_{J}u\in E^{\prime}\}.

(4)

V_{T}
is the set of
[\mathcal{F},u]\in\mathcal{G}^{E^{\prime}}_{\delta,T}
such that
\mathfrak{nl}_{e}(u)>\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
for all
e\in G(T)
and
\mathfrak{nl}(u)<\mathcal{L}
for all other
\varepsilon
-thin annuli of
g_{(\ddot{F},j)}
.
V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
and
V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
are defined analogously.

(5)

\pi_{T}:E_{T}\to V_{T}
and
\pi_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}:E_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
are restrictions of
E^{\prime}\to\mathcal{G}^{E^{\prime}}_{\delta,T}
to
V_{T}
and
V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
.

We take
V_{T}
,
V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
,
V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
,
K_{T}
,
\pi_{T}:E_{T}\to V_{T}
, and
\pi_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}:E_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
to all be
G_{T/\!\!/T}(T)
-invariant.

The metric
d_{T}
is as in Definition
8.6.6
and the morphisms are defined in the same way as in the
\operatorname{(Symp)}
case.

8.10.5.
Examples

We give some examples which illustrate the corners (see Figure
6
). Let
T
be a CH tree of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type.
Given a collection of glued edges
e_{1},\dots,e_{k}
of
T
, let

(8.10.2)

\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{1}\dots e_{k}}:=\min(\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{1}},\dots,\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{k}}).

Figure 6.
The
\operatorname{(Symp)}
vertices are given by solid dots and the
\operatorname{(Cob)}
vertices by open squares.

(A) Let
T
be CH tree given in Figure
6
(A), i.e.,

V^{\operatorname{Symp}}(T)=\{i_{3}\},~~V^{\operatorname{Cob}}(T)=\{i_{1},i_{2}\},

G(T)=\{e_{1}=(i_{3},i_{1}),e_{2}=(i_{3},i_{2})\}.

Let
S^{\prime}=(\{i_{1},i_{2}\},\{e_{1}\})
and
S^{\prime\prime}=(\{i_{2},i_{3}\},\{e_{2}\})
be incomplete subtrees. See Figure
7
for the image of
\mathcal{M}_{T/\!\!/T}
in the
(\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{1}},\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{2}})
-coordinate plane.

Figure 7.
The shaded region is the image of
\mathcal{M}_{T/\!\!/T}
, which is at a bounded distance from
\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{1}}=\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{2}}
. The labels for the regions indicate that we have stabilized using the asymptotic eigenfunctions corresponding to the given edges. The regions labeled
e_{1}
and
e_{2}
correspond to
T/\!\!/S^{\prime\prime}
and
T/\!\!/S^{\prime}
, respectively.

Note that
\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{1}}=\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{2}}
“in the large”.

(B) Let
T
be the CH tree given in Figure
6
(B), i.e.,

V^{\operatorname{Symp}}(T)=\{i_{4},i_{5}\},~~V^{\operatorname{Cob}}(T)=\{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}\},

G(T)=\{e_{1}=(i_{4},i_{1}),e_{2}=(i_{4},i_{2}),e_{3}=(i_{5},i_{4}),e_{4}=(i_{5},i_{3})\}.

We first map
\mathcal{M}_{T/\!\!/T}
to the
(\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{1}},\dots,\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{4}})
-coordinate plane. Note that, “in the large” we have relations
\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{1}}=\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{2}}
and
\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{1}}+\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{3}}=\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{4}}
. Hence
\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{3}}
and any one of
\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{1}},\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{2}},\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{4}}
determine the other neck length functions. Note that
\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{4}}>\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{1}e_{2}}
when we are sufficiently close to the corner and hence
\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{1}e_{2}e_{4}}=\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{1}e_{2}}
“in the large”.

(C) Let
T
be the CH tree given in Figure
6
(C), i.e.,

V^{\operatorname{Symp}}(T)=\{i_{3},i_{4},i_{5}\},~~V^{\operatorname{Cob}}(T)=\{i_{1},i_{2}\},

G(T)=\{e_{1}=(i_{3},i_{1}),e_{2}=(i_{4},i_{2}),e_{3}=(i_{5},i_{3}),e_{4}=(i_{5},i_{4})\}.

When we map
\mathcal{M}_{T/\!\!/T}
to the
(\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{1}},\dots,\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{4}})
-coordinate plane, then the image of
\mathcal{M}_{T/\!\!/T}
is a bounded distance away from
\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{1}}+\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{3}}=\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{2}}+\mathfrak{nl}_{e_{4}}
.

8.10.6.
Gluing maps

We describe the gluing maps in the
\operatorname{(Cob)}
case.

Let
T
be a CH tree of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type with
|V(T)|\geq 2
. Suppose
S
is a good subforest which is the nonempty union of glued edges such that

\overrightarrow{T}=\overrightarrow{T}(S)=(T_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{m(S)},\iota^{E(T)},\iota^{V(T)},\theta),\quad m(S)=|G(S)|+1,

is a degeneration such that, for each
T_{i}
,
Cl(T_{i}^{\operatorname{trim}})=T_{i}^{\operatorname{trim}}
, where
T_{i}^{\operatorname{trim}}
is the subtree of
T
consisting of the vertices and glued edges of
T_{i}
, and
\iota^{E(T)},\iota^{V(T)},
and
\theta
are the same maps as defined in Definition
8.1.10
.
Let

E_{T}:=\{e\in G(S)~|~\mbox{$t(e)$ is the root of $T_{i}$ of $(\operatorname{Cob})$ type}\}

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}:=V_{T_{1}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\times\dots\times V_{T_{m(S)}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\times(R-\epsilon_{0},\infty)^{m(S)-1},

where
R>0
is large and
\epsilon_{0}>0
is small.
We will set
R=\mathcal{L}
and
\epsilon_{0}=\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}+\tfrac{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2}
as before. Also let

E^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}_{\overrightarrow{T}}=\operatorname{pr}^{*}_{T_{1}}E^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}_{T_{1}}\oplus\dots\oplus\operatorname{pr}^{*}_{T_{m(S)}}E^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}_{T_{m(S)}},

where
\operatorname{pr}_{T_{k}}:\widetilde{W}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}_{\overrightarrow{T}}\to V^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}_{T_{k}}
is the
k
th projection map.

We then define
W_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\subset\widetilde{W}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
as follows:
Let
{\bf v}\in V_{T_{1}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\times\dots\times V_{T_{m(S)}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
and

\displaystyle({\bf I}^{\prime},{\bf I}):=(I_{1}^{\prime},\dots,I^{\prime}_{|E_{T}|},I_{1},\dots,I_{|G(S)-E_{T}|})\in(R-\epsilon_{0},\infty)^{m(S)-1}.

Here the
I_{i}^{\prime}
are the gluing parameters corresponding to the edges in
E_{T}
and the
I_{i}
are the gluing parameters corresponding to the edges in
G(S)-E_{T}
. Then
W_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
is the set of “preglueable”
({\bf v},{\bf I}^{\prime},{\bf I})\in\widetilde{W}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
, i.e., such that the pregluing
u_{*}({\bf v},{\bf I}^{\prime},{\bf I})
as in Definition
7.1.2
exists. Observe that if
({\bf v},{\bf I}^{\prime},{\bf I})\in W_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
, then for any
r\in\{1,\dots,|E_{T}|\}
the gluing parameters
I_{r}^{\prime},{\bf I}
determine the remaining gluing parameters
I^{\prime}_{j}
, keeping in mind that the positions of curves of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type cannot be translated up/down in the
\mathbb{R}
-direction. Summarizing, we have the following:

Lemma 8.10.5
.

The manifold
W_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
admits an embedding
W^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}_{\overrightarrow{T}}\to\widetilde{W}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}_{\overrightarrow{T}}
and is covered by open subsets of the form
\mathcal{U}\times(R-\epsilon_{0},\infty)^{1+|G(S)-E_{T}|}
, where
\mathcal{U}\subset\times_{i}V_{T_{i}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
and the coordinates on
(R-\epsilon_{0},\infty)^{1+|G(S)-E_{T}|}
are
(R_{r}^{\prime},{\bf R})
for some
r
.

We then have the gluing map

(8.10.3)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}:W_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\to\mathcal{G}_{\delta,T}^{E^{\prime}},

obtained by the constructing the pregluing
u_{*}({\bf u},{\bf R}^{\prime},{\bf R})
and inverting all the errors simultaneously at each step of the contraction mapping as in Section
7.9.1
.

8.10.7.
Semi-global Kuranishi structure in the
\operatorname{(Cob)}
case

The definition of the semi-global Kuranishi structure in the
\operatorname{(Symp)}
case (Definition
8.3.1
) carries over to the
\operatorname{(Cob)}
case after replacing
(SC1)
by
1
and the gluing maps
\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
that are used in
(SC2)
by those in
1
.

Let
T
,
S
, and
E_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\to\widetilde{W}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}_{\overrightarrow{T}}
be as defined in Section
8.10.6
.

(1)

There exist global constants
R>0
and
\epsilon_{0}>0
small, “intermediate”
G_{T/\!\!/T}(T)
-invariant open subsets
V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
for all
T
satisfying

V_{T}\subset V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\subset V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}},\quad\overline{V}_{T}\subset V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2},\quad\overline{V}_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\subset V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}},

and embeddings
W^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}_{\overrightarrow{T}}\to\widetilde{W}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}_{\overrightarrow{T}}
satisfying Lemma
8.10.5
, such that, for any CH tree
T
with
|V(T)|\geq 2
and a good subforest
S
of
T
such that
G(S)\not=\varnothing
, there exists a
C^{1}
-bundle map
(\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}},\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}})
between bundles of the same rank

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}}

called the
gluing map
and which satisfies the following:

(a)

\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
is a
C^{1}
-map which is a homeomorphism onto its image.

(b)

\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}\circ(\overline{\partial}_{T_{1}}^{{\operatorname{en}}},\dots,\overline{\partial}_{T_{m}}^{{\operatorname{en}}})|_{W_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}}
and
\overline{\partial}_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}\circ\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}

are
C^{1}
-close and their difference goes to zero as
\operatorname{min}_{j=1}^{m-1}I_{j}\to\infty
, where
I_{j}
is the coordinate for the
j
th
(R-\epsilon_{0},\infty)
factor corresponding to an edge
e_{j}\in E(S)
.

(c)

Let
S_{j}
be a good subforest of
T_{j}
with
G(S_{j})\not=\varnothing
which we view as a subforest of
T
.
If
v_{i}\in V_{T_{i}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
,
(\mathbf{v}_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})},\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})})\in W_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}
, and
\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S)}\in(R-\epsilon_{0},\infty)^{m(S)-1}
, such that

(v_{1},\dots,v_{j-1},\mathbf{v}_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})},v_{j+1},\dots,v_{m},\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})},\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S)})\in W_{\overrightarrow{T}(S\cup S_{j})}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2},

then there exists

\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S)}^{\prime}=\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S)}^{\prime}(v_{1},\dots,v_{j-1},\mathbf{v}_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})},v_{j+1},\dots,v_{m},\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})},\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S)})\in(R-\epsilon_{0},\infty)^{m(S)-1}

such that the simultaneous and iterated gluing maps

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S\cup S_{j})}^{{\operatorname{en}}}(v_{1},\dots,v_{j-1},\mathbf{v}_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})},v_{j+1},\dots,v_{m},\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})},\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S)}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S)}^{{\operatorname{en}}}(v_{1},\dots,v_{j-1},\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})}^{{\operatorname{en}}}(\mathbf{v}_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})},\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})}),v_{j+1},\dots,v_{m},\mathbf{I}^{\prime}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S)})

are
C^{1}
-close with error
\to 0
and
\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S)}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S)}
are
C^{1}
-close with error
\to 0
as the minimum of all the components of
\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T_{j}}(S_{j})},\mathbf{I}_{\overrightarrow{T}(S)}\to\infty
.

Let
\overrightarrow{T}=\overrightarrow{T}(T_{\operatorname{max}})
, where
T_{\operatorname{max}}
is the maximal good subforest of
T
such that if
\overrightarrow{T}(T_{\operatorname{max}})=T_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{m}=
, then
Cl(T_{i})=T_{i}
(in
T
) for each
T_{i}
. Then we denote

\mathfrak{G}^{R_{0},T}:=\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}(\{({\bf v},{\mathbf{I}}_{\overrightarrow{T}})\in W_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}~|~{\mathbf{I}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}\in(R-\epsilon_{0},\infty)^{|G(T_{\operatorname{max}})|}-(R-\epsilon_{0},R_{0})^{|G(T_{\operatorname{max}})|}).

(d)

\mathfrak{G}^{R-\epsilon_{0},T}\supset\overline{V}_{T}
and
\overline{V}_{T}-\mathfrak{G}^{R_{0},T}
is compact for any
R_{0}\geq R-\epsilon_{0}
.

The following is analogous to Theorem
7.6.1
and is proved in the same way:

Theorem 8.10.6
.

1
(a) holds for
\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
and
\operatorname{Im}\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}\supset V_{T}
.

The analog of Theorem
6.5.3
becomes:

Theorem 8.10.7
.

1
(c) holds for the collection of gluing maps
\{\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}\}_{\overrightarrow{T}}
.

In
(SC2)
,
\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
is replaced by a map
W_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\to V_{T}^{{\operatorname{en}}}
.

8.10.8.
Definition of the multisection
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T}

The goal of this subsection is to give the definition of a multisection
\mathfrak{S}=\{\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T}:V^{\operatorname{en}}_{T}\to E^{\operatorname{en}}_{T}\}_{T}
of a semi-global Kuranishi structure
\mathscr{K}
of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type and to give a construction of a specific
\mathfrak{S}
, following Section
8.7.3
.

Definition 8.10.8

(Multisection of
\mathscr{K}
of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type)
.

A
multisection of
\mathscr{K}
of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type
is a collection
\mathfrak{S}=\{\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T}:V^{\operatorname{en}}_{T}\to E^{\operatorname{en}}_{T}\}_{T}
of slight enlargements of multisections satisfying (1) and (3) of Definition
8.3.6
and

(2’)

there exists
R_{0}\gg 0
such that for each good subforest
S
of
T
with
S\not=\varnothing
, if we write
\overrightarrow{T}=\overrightarrow{T}(S)=T_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{m(S)}
, then on

\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}(W_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\cap\{({\bf v},{\bf I})~|~{\bf I}\in(R_{0},\infty)^{m(S)-1}\}),

\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}\circ(\mathfrak{s}_{T_{1}}^{\operatorname{en}},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{T_{m(S)}}^{\operatorname{en}})\circ(\mathfrak{G}^{\operatorname{en}}_{\overrightarrow{T}})^{-1}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}

are
C^{1}
-close and their difference goes to zero as
\min_{j=1}^{m(S)-1}I_{j}\to\infty
, where
(I_{1},\dots,I_{m(S)-1})
are the
(R_{0},\infty)^{m(S)-1}
-coordinates.

We assume that
Condition (H)
from Section
8.6.3
holds; the modifications used in the
\chi\geq 0
case are analogous. Let
0<\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}\ll\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
as in Section
8.4
. Let
T
be a CH tree of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type.

We define the gluing maps
\mathfrak{G}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T,S}
as in Section
8.7.2
, where
S
is a good subforest of
T
and
Cl(S)=\sqcup_{j}\widetilde{S}_{j}
. Let

\displaystyle V^{\operatorname{en}}_{T,S}=(\times_{j}V^{\operatorname{en}}_{\widetilde{S}_{j}^{\star}})\times(\times_{i\in V(T)-V(Cl(S))}V^{\operatorname{en}}_{i}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\delta,T,S}^{E^{\prime}}=\mathcal{G}_{\delta}^{E^{\prime}}(\{V_{\widetilde{S}_{j}^{\star}}^{\operatorname{en}}\}_{j},\{V_{i}^{\operatorname{en}}\}_{i\in V(T)-V(Cl(S))}),

and
W^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}_{T,S}
be the subset of preglueable
({\bf v},{\bf I})\in V^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}_{T,S}\times(\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}-\tfrac{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2},\infty)^{|G(T)|-|G(Cl(S))|}
,
There is a gluing map

\mathfrak{G}_{T,S}^{\operatorname{en}}:W^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}_{T,S}\to\mathcal{G}_{\delta,T,S}^{E^{\prime}},\quad\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T,S}:E_{T,S}^{{\operatorname{en}}/2}\to E_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}

defined in the same way as in Definition
8.10.3
and Section
8.6.6
.

Definition 8.10.9

(Multisection
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T}
,
\operatorname{(Cob)}
case)
.

Suppose
T
satisfies:

(
\dagger\dagger
)

Either
T
is a
1
-vertex tree or the root vertex
v
of
T
is a
\operatorname{(Mixed)}
vertex and
Cl(\{v\})^{\star}=T
.

The multisection
\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
on
V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
is arbitrary, subject to consistency with
\mathfrak{s}_{T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}}
on adjacent regions satisfying (
\dagger\dagger
) and invariance under
G_{T/\!\!/T}
.

Assume that
{\mathfrak{s}}_{T^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{en}}
has been defined for all
T^{\prime}
with
c(T^{\prime})<c(T)
. Then the multisection
{\mathfrak{s}}_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
on
E_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}
is the melding of the following multisections with respect to

\mathfrak{nl}_{T}=(\mathfrak{nl}_{e})_{\{e\in G(T)\}}:V_{T}^{\operatorname{en}}\to[\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime},\infty)^{|G(T)|}

and the collections
\{R_{T,S,j}\}
and
\{R^{\prime}_{T,S,j}\}
,
j=1,2
, from Section
8.7.3
:

(1)

On
\mathfrak{nl}_{T}^{-1}(R^{\prime}_{T,S,1})
, take the stabilization
\operatorname{stab}_{T/\!\!/S,T}({\mathfrak{s}}_{T/\!\!/S}^{\operatorname{en}})
of
{\mathfrak{s}}_{T/\!\!/S}^{\operatorname{en}}
.

(2)

On
\mathfrak{nl}_{T}^{-1}(R^{\prime}_{T,S,2})
, where
T/\!\!/S
does not satisfy (
\dagger\dagger
), take the restriction of
(\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{T,S}^{\operatorname{en}})_{*}\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T,S}:=\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{T,S}^{\operatorname{en}}\circ\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T,S}\circ(\mathfrak{G}_{T,S}^{\operatorname{en}})^{-1}
, where

\mathfrak{s}^{\operatorname{en}}_{T,S}=(\mathfrak{s}_{\widetilde{S}_{j}^{\star}}^{\operatorname{en}})_{j}\times(\mathfrak{s}_{i}^{\operatorname{en}})_{i\in V(T)-V(S)}.

(3)

On
\mathfrak{nl}_{T}^{-1}(R^{\prime}_{T,S,2})
, where
T/\!\!/S
satisfies (
\dagger\dagger
), take an arbitrary multisection satisfying invariance under marker rotations and puncture reorderings.

When melding the multisections, we need to choose a consistent collection of branches that agree on the overlaps as we approach the “boundary”.

Observe that, in
2
, we have
\mathcal{L}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}>\mathfrak{nl}_{e}>\mathcal{L}
for all
e\in G(S)
, which in turn implies that
\mathfrak{nl}_{e}
is bounded above for all
e\in G(cl(S))
.

The analog of Lemma
8.7.5
also holds in the
\operatorname{(Cob)}
case.

Finally, the definition of
\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}(\mathcal{M}_{i}),\mathfrak{S})
is as given in Equation (
8.3.2
) and Proposition
8.3.7
holds for
\mathcal{M}_{i}
of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type after replacing
\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}
and
\operatorname{vdim}\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}
by
\mathcal{M}_{i}
and
\operatorname{vdim}\mathcal{M}_{i}
and taking one of
\mathcal{Z}_{j(T)}
or
\mathcal{Z}_{k(T)}
to be of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type and the other to be of
\operatorname{(Symp)}
type.

8.11.
Chain homotopy

Let
X
be a compact manifold with corners and let
\{(\widehat{W}^{\tau}=\widehat{W},\widehat{\alpha}^{\tau})\}_{\tau\in X}
be a family of completed exact symplectic cobordisms from
(M_{+},\alpha_{+})
to
(M_{-},\alpha_{-})
. For each
\tau\in X
, let
J^{\tau}
be an almost complex structure on
\widehat{W}^{\tau}
that restricts to
J_{\pm}
at the positive/negative ends of
\widehat{W}^{\tau}
. We assume that
(\widehat{\alpha}^{\tau},J^{\tau})
is an
(L_{+},L_{-})
-simple pair,
J^{\tau}
is
(L_{+},L_{-})
-end-generic. In the special case of interest
X=[0,1]
, we additionally assume that
(\widehat{\alpha}^{\tau},J^{\tau})
is independent of
\tau
on each of
\tau\in[0,\epsilon]
and
\tau\in[1-\epsilon,1]
, where
\epsilon>0
is small.

We use the ordering of moduli spaces from Section
8.10.1
. Let
\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\tau}
be
\mathcal{M}_{i}
with respect to
J^{\tau}
, let

\mathcal{M}_{i}^{X}=\{[\mathcal{F},u]\in\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\tau}~|~\tau\in X\},

and let
\Pi_{i}:\mathcal{M}_{i}^{X}\to X
be the projection that sends
\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\tau}
to
\tau
. We use
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small and
\ell>0
sufficiently large so that Theorem
5.1.2
holds simultaneously for all
J^{\tau}
,
\tau\in X
.

8.11.1.
Semi-global Kuranishi structure in the
\operatorname{(Par)}
case

The semi-global Kuranishi structure
\mathscr{K}^{X}=\mathscr{K}^{(L_{+},L_{-})}(\{(\widehat{\alpha}^{\tau},J^{\tau})\}_{\tau\in X})

of parametrized
\operatorname{(Par)}
type
is the parametrized version of a semi-global Kuranishi structure of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type.
If
T
is a CH tree of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type, then let

\pi_{T}^{X}:E_{T}^{X}\to V_{T}^{X}

be the parametric version of the obstruction bundle
\pi_{T}^{\tau}:E_{T}^{\tau}\to V_{T}^{\tau}
corresponding to
\tau
; the rest of the definition including the slight enlargements as given in Sections
8.10.4
,
8.10.6
, and
8.10.7
can be done parametrically. In particular, the
\overline{\partial}
-section is the parametric version of
\overline{\partial}_{J^{\tau}}
. When
X=[0,1]
, we assume that
\pi_{T}^{[0,1]}
satisfies the following conditions:

(
\tau_{1}
)

it is independent of
\tau
on
[0,\epsilon]
and on
[1-\epsilon,1]
; and

(
\tau_{2}
)

it agrees with
\pi_{T}^{\tau=0}
and
\pi_{T}^{\tau=1}
, which we assume have already been constructed for the chain maps corresponding to
J^{\tau=0}
and
J^{\tau=1}
.

For the boundary structure we have the parametrized version of
1
,
with the modification of replacing the product of charts by fiber product.
More precisely, we explain the modification using the case when
T
is a CH tree of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type with one
\operatorname{(Symp)}
vertex
i_{3}
, two
\operatorname{(Cob)}
vertices
i_{1}
and
i_{2}
, and two gluing edges
e_{1}
and
e_{2}.

Let
\overrightarrow{T}=i_{1}\sqcup i_{2}\sqcup i_{3}
be a degeneration of
T
.
The “pregluable” set
W_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{[0,1]}
is diffeomorphic to

V^{[0,1]}_{\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}\times V_{i_{3}}\times[R,\infty),

where

V^{[0,1]}_{\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}=V_{i_{1}}^{[0,1]}{}_{\pi_{i_{1}}^{[0,1]}}\times_{\pi_{i_{2}}^{[0,1]}}V_{i_{2}}^{[0,1]},

and
R>0
is some constant.
We denote the diffeomorphism by
\Psi.

Note that by Theorem
5.1.2
, the projection
\pi_{i_{k}}^{[0,1]}:V_{i_{k}}^{[0,1]}\to[0,1]
is submersive for
k\in\{1,2\},
and hence
V^{[0,1]}_{\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}
is smooth.

8.11.2.
Definition of the multisection
\mathfrak{S}

The definition of the multisection
\mathfrak{S}=\{\mathfrak{s}_{T}^{[0,1]}:V_{T}^{[0,1]}\to E_{T}^{[0,1]}\}
of
\mathscr{K}^{[0,1]}

will follow a parametric version of Definition
8.10.9
but will require a slight twist in order to achieve sufficient transversality. We continue using the example of Section
8.11.1
to explain the twist.
Consider the gluing maps
\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{[0,1]},\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{[0,1]}
obtained from precomposing the parametrized version of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}},\mathfrak{G}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{\operatorname{en}}
in
1
with
\Psi^{-1}
and dropping the notation
{\operatorname{en}}.

We have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{[0,1]}_{\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}\times E_{i_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{[0,1]}_{\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}\times V_{i_{3}}\times[R,\infty)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{T}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}}^{[0,1]}_{\overrightarrow{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{G^{\prime}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}^{[0,1]}}

We denote by
\mathfrak{s}_{\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}^{[0,1]}
the multisection of
E_{\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}^{[0,1]}\to V_{\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}^{[0,1]}.

As usual, we require

(1)

\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}}_{\overrightarrow{T}}\circ(\mathfrak{s}_{\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}^{[0,1]},\mathfrak{s}_{i_{3}})\circ(\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{\overrightarrow{T}})^{-1}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathfrak{s}_{T}

are
C^{1}
-close and their difference goes to zero as
I\in[R,\infty)\to\infty
,

(2)

multisection
\mathfrak{s}_{\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}^{[0,1]}=\mathfrak{s}_{i_{1}}^{[0,1]}\times\mathfrak{s}_{i_{2}}^{[0,1]}|_{V^{[0,1]}_{\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}}

when
\tau\in[0,\epsilon]\cup[1-\epsilon,1],

and

(3)

\mathfrak{s}_{\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}^{[0,1]}
is a transversal (to
\overline{\partial}
) section (not necessarily of product type).

Note that one can choose a generic pair
(\mathfrak{s}_{i_{1}}^{[0,1]},\mathfrak{s}_{i_{2}}^{[0,1]})
so that
\mathfrak{s}_{\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}^{[0,1]}
is of product-type, i.e.,
\mathfrak{s}_{\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}^{[0,1]}=\mathfrak{s}_{i_{1}}^{[0,1]}\times\mathfrak{s}_{i_{2}}^{[0,1]}|_{V^{[0,1]}_{\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}}
and
\mathfrak{s}_{\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}^{[0,1]}
is transversal. But then the pair
(\mathfrak{s}_{i_{1}}^{[0,1]},\mathfrak{s}_{\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}^{[0,1]})
is not a generic pair, which results in trouble in the construction of semi-global Kuranishi structures in higher energy strata.
Our choice of relaxing the multisection to be non-product type makes the construction of multisection in the
\operatorname{(Cob)}
case carry over to the
\operatorname{(Par)}
case straightforwardly,
but it is also the reason why the chain homotopy map
K
defined later is only a linear map.

The definition of the weighted branched submanifold
\mathcal{Z}^{[0,1]}(\mathscr{K}(\mathcal{M}_{i}),\mathfrak{S})
is as given in Equation (
8.3.2
) and Proposition
8.3.7
holds for
\mathcal{M}_{i}^{[0,1]}
of
\operatorname{(Cob)}
type after replacing
\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}
and
\operatorname{vdim}\mathcal{M}_{i}/\mathbb{R}
by
\mathcal{M}_{i}^{[0,1]}
and
\operatorname{vdim}\mathcal{M}_{i}^{[0,1]}=\operatorname{vdim}\mathcal{M}_{i}+1
and taking one of
\mathcal{Z}_{j(T)}
or
\mathcal{Z}_{k(T)}
to be of
\operatorname{(Par)}
type and the other to be of
\operatorname{(Symp)}
type.

9.
Contact homology

9.1.
The contact homology dga

9.1.1.
Definitions

Let
(M^{2n+1},\xi)
be a closed contact manifold. Given
L>0
, let
\alpha
be a contact form for
\xi
and
J
be an almost complex structure on
\mathbb{R}\times M
such that
(\alpha,J)
is an
L
-simple pair. For the above data we constructed a semi-global Kuranishi structure

\mathscr{K}^{L}(\alpha,J)=\{\mathscr{K}^{L}(\alpha,J;\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-};A)~|~\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}(\gamma_{+}),\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-})\leq L,A\in H_{2}(M;\mathbb{Z})\},

in Section
8
, together with a multisection
\mathfrak{S}=\{{\mathfrak{s}}_{T}\}_{T}
that is
C^{1}
-close to the
0
-section and such that
\overline{\partial}_{T}
is transverse to
\mathfrak{s}_{T}
for all
T
; see Section
9.1.2
for how to assign
A\in H_{2}(M;\mathbb{Z})
to
[\mathcal{F},u]
. Let
\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}^{L}(\alpha,J;\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-};A),\mathfrak{S})
be the weighted branched manifold for the full subcategory
\mathscr{K}^{L}(\alpha,J;\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-};A)
of
\mathscr{K}^{L}(\alpha,J)
and the multisection
\mathfrak{S}
.

Let

\mathfrak{A}^{L}=\mathfrak{A}^{L}(M,\alpha,J,\mathscr{K}^{L}(\alpha,J),\mathfrak{S},\mathcal{S}^{L}_{\alpha})

be the unital graded commutative algebra freely generated by
\mathcal{P}_{\alpha}^{L,\operatorname{good}}
over the group algebra
\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(M;\mathbb{Z})]
. The generators of the group algebra
\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(M;\mathbb{Z})]
will be written as
e^{A}
, where
A\in H_{2}(M;\mathbb{Z})
. In Section
9.1.2
we explain how to assign a
\mathbb{Q}
-grading
|\cdot|
to each Reeb orbit in
\mathcal{P}_{\alpha}^{L}
and to each
e^{A}
,
A\in H_{2}(M;\mathbb{Z})
, and give a definition of
\mathcal{S}^{L}_{\alpha}
.

We also define a
\mathbb{Z}/2
-grading
|\cdot|_{0}
on
\mathcal{P}_{\alpha}^{L}
: Given
\gamma\in\mathcal{P}_{\alpha}^{L}
, we set

|\gamma|_{0}\equiv\mu_{\tau}(\gamma)+n-3\mbox{ mod }2,

where
\mu_{\tau}(\gamma)
is the Conley-Zehnder index with respect to the trivialization
\tau
. Observe that the parity of
\mu_{\tau}(\gamma)
does not depend on the choice of
\tau
.

Definition 9.1.1
.

An ordered tuple of Reeb orbits
\boldsymbol{\gamma}
is
good
if all of its entries are good Reeb orbits. Otherwise,
\boldsymbol{\gamma}
is
bad
.

Next we define the differential
\partial:\mathfrak{A}^{L}\to\mathfrak{A}^{L}
. This makes
\mathfrak{A}^{L}
into a dga and we denote its homology by
HC(M,\alpha,J,\mathscr{K}^{L}(\alpha,J),\mathfrak{S},\mathcal{S}^{L}_{\alpha})
or
HC^{L}
. Fix an ordering
\vartheta
of
\mathcal{P}^{L}_{\alpha}
by the action as usual. We then set

(9.1.1)

\partial\gamma_{+}=\sum_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}}\sum_{A\in H_{2}(M;\mathbb{Z})}\frac{d_{\gamma_{+},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-},A}}{m_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}}}e^{A}\gamma_{-,k}^{i_{k}}\dots\gamma_{-,1}^{i_{1}},

where the sums are taken over all good
\vartheta
-sorted
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}
and
A\in H_{2}(M;\mathbb{Z})
with
|\gamma_{+}|-(|e^{A}|+|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}|)=1
. A sorted
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}
is written as

(9.1.2)

\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}=(\underbrace{\gamma_{-,1},\dots,\gamma_{-,1}}_{i_{1}\text{ copies }},\dots,\underbrace{\gamma_{-,k},\dots,\gamma_{-,k}}_{i_{k}\text{ copies }}),

d_{\gamma_{+},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-},A}
is the weighted signed count of elements in
\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}^{L}(\alpha,J;\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-};A),\mathfrak{S})
, and

m_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}}:=\Pi_{j=1}^{k}(i_{j}!m(\gamma_{-,j})^{i_{j}}).

(If
|\gamma_{+}|-(|e^{A}|+|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}|)\neq 1
, then we set
d_{\gamma_{+},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-},A}=0
.) In Section
9.1.2
we explain how to define the class
A
corresponding to an approximate
J
-holomorphic map
u
from
\gamma_{+}
to
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}
and in Section
9.1.3
we explain how to orient the moduli spaces. Then we extend
\partial
to
\mathfrak{A}^{L}
using the graded Leibniz rule, namely,

\partial(\gamma\gamma^{\prime})=(\partial\gamma)\gamma^{\prime}+(-1)^{|\gamma|_{0}}\gamma(\partial\gamma^{\prime}).

Sometimes we write an ordered tuple of Reeb orbits multiplicatively. Moreover, when there is no confusion, we do not distinguish an ordered tuple of Reeb orbits from the monomial associated to it in
\mathfrak{A}^{L}
.

If
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}=\gamma_{-,1}^{i_{1}}\dots\gamma_{-,k}^{i_{k}}
, then we denote the order-reversed monomial by
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}^{\dagger}=\gamma_{-,k}^{i_{k}}\dots\gamma_{-,1}^{i_{1}}.

Remark 9.1.2
.

In (
9.1.1
), we use
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}^{\dagger}
instead of
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}
. This is due to the fact that we use the coherent orientation from
[
BM
]
; see the proof of Proposition
9.1.4
.
18
18

18

We later find out that this is not necessary if one uses the choice of coherent orientation from
[
HT2
]
.

9.1.2.
Grading and
\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(M;\mathbb{Z})]
-coefficients

We follow the discussion in
[
Bo
]
. Let us write
H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})=F\oplus T
, where
F
is the free part and
T
is the torsion part. Pick representatives
C_{1},\dots,C_{a}
of a basis for
F
and representatives
D_{1},\dots,D_{b}
of a minimal generating set for
T
, and fix a trivialization
\tau
for
\xi
along each
C_{i}
and
D_{i}
. Given
\gamma\in\mathcal{P}^{L}_{\alpha}
, let us write
[\gamma]=[\sum c_{i}C_{i}+\sum d_{i}D_{i}]\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})
, where
d_{i}
are the smallest nonnegative coefficients for the
D_{i}
. Then choose a surface
S_{\gamma}
such that
\partial S_{\gamma}=\gamma-\sum_{i}c_{i}C_{i}-\sum_{i}d_{i}D_{i}
and use
S_{\gamma}
to extend the trivializations
\tau
along
{C_{i}}
and
{D_{i}}
to all of
S_{\gamma}
and hence to
\gamma
. If
[D_{i}]
has order
m_{i}
, then choose a spanning surface
S_{m_{i}D_{i}}
for
m_{i}D_{i}
. This gives a trivialization
\tau^{\prime}
along
m_{i}D_{i}
and we let
w_{i}
be the rotation number of
\tau
with respect to
\tau^{\prime}
along
m_{i}D_{i}
. We then set

|\gamma|=\mu_{\tau}(\gamma)+2\sum_{i=1}^{b}\frac{w_{i}d_{i}}{m_{i}}+n-3\in\mathbb{Q},

where
\mu_{\tau}
is the Conley-Zehnder index with respect to
\tau
. We also set

|e^{A}|=-2\langle c_{1}(\xi),A\rangle.

The collection

\mathcal{S}^{L}_{\alpha}=\{S_{\gamma}\}_{\gamma\in\mathcal{P}^{L}_{\alpha}}\cup\{S_{m_{i}D_{i}}\}_{i=1,\dots,b}

will be called a
complete set of trivializing surfaces for
\mathcal{P}^{L}_{\alpha}
.

Given a map
u
from
\gamma
to
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}
, we can cap off the projection
\pi_{M}\circ u
of
u
to
M
along
\gamma
by
S_{\gamma}
and along
\gamma_{-,j}
by
S_{\gamma_{-,j}}
and further cap off any extraneous
m_{i}D_{i}
by
S_{m_{i}D_{i}}
, which gives a closed surface
A
. Note that if

\partial S_{\gamma}=\gamma-\sum_{i}c_{i}C_{i}-\sum_{i}d_{i}D_{i},

where the
d_{i}
are the smallest nonnegative coefficients, then

\sum_{j}\partial S_{\gamma_{-,j}}=\sum_{j}\gamma_{-,j}-\sum_{i}c_{i}C_{i}-\sum_{i}d_{i}D_{i}+\zeta,

where
\zeta
is a sum of
m_{i}D_{i}
.

9.1.3.
Orientations

Following the orientation conventions of
[
BM
]
, we explain how to assign a sign to

\llbracket\mathcal{F},u\rrbracket\in\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}^{L}(\alpha,J;\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-};A),\mathfrak{S}).

Step 1.
For any
(\mathcal{F},u)\in\mathcal{B}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}
, let
L_{(\mathcal{F},u)}
be the full linearized
\overline{\partial}_{J}
-operator and

\operatorname{det}L_{(\mathcal{F},u)}=\wedge^{\operatorname{top}}\ker L_{(\mathcal{F},u)}\otimes\wedge^{\operatorname{top}}(\operatorname{coker}L_{(\mathcal{F},u)})^{*}

be its determinant line.

An
orientation

\mathfrak{o}(\mathcal{F},u)
of
(\mathcal{F},u)
is an orientation
\mathfrak{o}(\operatorname{det}L_{(\mathcal{F},u)})
of
\operatorname{det}L_{(\mathcal{F},u)}
, i.e., an equivalence class of nonzero vectors of
\operatorname{det}L_{(\mathcal{F},u)}
, where the equivalence relation is
v\sim v^{\prime}
if there exists
c>0
such that
v=cv^{\prime}
.

According to
[
BM
]
, an orientation
\mathfrak{o}(\mathcal{F},u)
can be chosen in a continuous and coherent way for all maps
(\mathcal{F},u)
in all the
\mathcal{B}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}
, where “coherent” means:

(O1)

if we exchange the
i
th and
(i+1)
st negative punctures of
(\mathcal{F},u)
, then the sign changes by
(-1)^{|\gamma_{-,i}|_{0}\cdot|\gamma_{-,i+1}|_{0}}
, where
u
is asymptotic to
\gamma_{-,j}
near the
j
th negative puncture
p_{-,j}
(
[
BM
, Theorem 2]
);

(O2)

the gluing/pregluing and disjoint union maps preserve orientations up to a specific sign change which arises from reordering the punctures; in particular, if
(\mathcal{F}_{2},u_{2})
above is glued/preglued to
(\mathcal{F}_{1},u_{1})
below along the last puncture of
u_{2}
, and the negative ends of the (pre-)glued curve are ordered by using the ordering for
u_{2}
first, followed by the ordering of
u_{1}
, then there is no sign correction (
[
BM
, Corollary 10]
);

(O3)

if
u
is asymptotic to
\gamma
near the puncture
p
and
m(\gamma)>1
, then cyclically rotating the asymptotic marker at
p
through an angle of
\frac{2\pi}{m(\gamma)}
preserves the orientation if and only if
\gamma
is good (
[
BM
, Theorem 3]
);

(O4)

precomposing with an automorphism of the domain which preserves the punctures and asymptotic markers preserves the orientation (
[
BM
, Proposition 11]
).

Remark 9.1.3
.

A coherent orientation exists for
all maps
(\mathcal{F},u)
in all the
\mathcal{B}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}
and results of
[
BM
]
, namely Theorems 2 and 3 and Corollaries 10 and 11, while stated only for moduli spaces of
J
-holomorphic maps, easily generalize to the case of semi-global Kuranishi structures.

Step 2.

We explain how the above choices of
\mathfrak{o}(\mathcal{F},u)
induce an orientation on the total space of
\pi:E\to V
of a semi-global Kuranishi chart. We treat the slightly easier
\operatorname{(Cob)}
case; the modifications in the
\operatorname{(Symp)}
case can be done using (
9.1.3
) below. Given
(\mathcal{F},u)\in V
, let
L_{(\mathcal{F},u)}
be the full linearized
\overline{\partial}_{J}
-operator at
(\mathcal{F},u)
as before and
L_{(\mathcal{F},u)}^{E}:T_{(\mathcal{F},u)}V\to E_{(\mathcal{F},u)}
its restriction to
T_{(\mathcal{F},u)}V=L^{-1}_{(\mathcal{F},u)}(E_{(\mathcal{F},u)})
. By
[
FO3
, Section 8.1.1]
, the orientation
\mathfrak{o}(\operatorname{det}L_{(\mathcal{F},u)})
induces an orientation
\mathfrak{o}(\operatorname{det}L_{(\mathcal{F},u)}^{E})
and, equivalently, an orientation
{\mathfrak{o}}(E)
on the total space of
E
. The group action
\Gamma
on
E
preserves
{\mathfrak{o}}(E)
by (O4). Hence the orientation descends to the total space
E
.

Step 3.
We explain how to orient
\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}^{L}(\alpha,J;\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-};A),\mathfrak{S})
: Let us write
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{Z}
for the set of
[\mathcal{F},u]
such that
\llbracket F,u\rrbracket\in\mathcal{Z}
. Let
{\mathfrak{s}}=[s_{1},\dots,s_{m}]
be a liftable multisection of
(E\to V,\Gamma)
that is transverse to
\overline{\partial}_{J}
. For
(\mathcal{F},u)\in\overline{\partial}_{J}^{-1}(s_{i})
,
i\in\{1,\dots,m\}
, the tangent space
T_{(\mathcal{F},u)}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{Z})
, before quotienting by domain automorphisms, can be identified with the kernel of the map

L_{(\mathcal{F},u)}^{E}-Ds_{i}(\mathcal{F},u):T_{(\mathcal{F},u)}V\to E_{(\mathcal{F},u)},

where
Ds_{i}(\mathcal{F},u)
is the derivative of
s_{i}
in the fiber direction (projected using some connection) and
L_{(\mathcal{F},u)}^{E}-Ds_{i}(\mathcal{F},u)
is surjective by the tranversality of
\overline{\partial}_{J}
and
s_{i}
. Hence we orient
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{Z}
using

\mathfrak{o}(L_{(\mathcal{F},u)}^{E}-Ds_{i}(\mathcal{F},u))\simeq\mathfrak{o}(L_{(\mathcal{F},u)}^{E}).

Finally we orient
\mathcal{Z}
at
\llbracket\mathcal{F},u\rrbracket\in\mathcal{Z}
using the canonical isomorphism

(9.1.3)

\mathbb{R}\otimes\wedge^{\text{top}}(T_{\llbracket\mathcal{F},u\rrbracket}\mathcal{Z})\cong\wedge^{\text{top}}(T_{(\mathcal{F},u)}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{Z}))\cong\wedge^{\text{top}}(T_{(\mathcal{F},u)}\overline{\partial}_{J}^{-1}(s_{i}))

and a choice of orientation of
\mathbb{R}
.
This definition of
\mathfrak{o}(\mathcal{Z})
is well-defined by
(O4)
, and
(O1)
and
(O3)
translate to
\mathfrak{o}(\mathcal{Z})
in a straightforward manner. The following is the reformulation of
(O2)
in this setting:

(O2’)

Consider the gluing map
\mathcal{Z}_{2}\times\mathcal{Z}_{1}\to\partial^{\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{Z}_{3}
as in Proposition
8.3.7
, where
\mathcal{Z}_{1}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{2}
are
0
-dimensional, representatives
(\mathcal{F}_{2},u_{2})
and
(\mathcal{F}_{1},u_{1})
of elements of
\mathcal{Z}_{2}
above and
\mathcal{Z}_{1}
below are glued along the last negative puncture of
(\mathcal{F}_{2},u_{2})
, and the ends of the glued curve are ordered by using the ordering for
u_{2}
first, followed by the ordering of
u_{1}
. Then the gluing map maps the product orientation of
\mathcal{Z}_{2}\times\mathcal{Z}_{1}
to the boundary orientation of
\partial^{\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{Z}_{3}
.

This can be seen by observing that before quotienting out the
\mathbb{R}
-translation, if we translate representatives
(\mathcal{F}_{2},u_{2})
and
(\mathcal{F}_{1},u_{1})
of
\mathcal{Z}_{2}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{1}
in the same direction, then the glued curve also translates in that direction, whereas if we translate the representatives in the opposite directions, say towards each other, then the glued curve moves toward the interior of
\mathcal{Z}_{3}
; see
[
BH
, Section 9.1.2]
for a similar calculation.

9.1.4.
Examples

To illustrate the definition of the differential, we consider two examples (here we use
\mathbb{Q}
-coefficients):

1. Suppose that
\gamma_{+},\gamma_{-,1},\gamma_{-,2}\in\mathcal{P}_{\alpha}^{L,\operatorname{good}}
and that
\gamma_{-,1},\gamma_{-,2}
are simple.
Also suppose that the
\vartheta
-sorted moduli space

\mathcal{M}:=\mathcal{M}(\gamma_{+};\underbrace{\gamma_{-,1},\dots,\gamma_{-,1}}_{3\text{ copies}},\underbrace{\gamma_{-,2},\dots,\gamma_{-,2}}_{5\text{ copies}})

is regular and that
\llbracket\mathcal{F},u\rrbracket\in\mathcal{M}/\mathbb{R}
is simple and is counted as
+1
with respect to the coherent orientation.
Then by relabeling the punctures in the domain of
u
we get
3!5!
elements in
\mathcal{M}/\mathbb{R}
and
3!5!
elements in

\mathcal{M}(\gamma_{+};\underbrace{\gamma_{-,2},\dots,\gamma_{-,2}}_{5\text{ copies}},\underbrace{\gamma_{-,1},\dots,\gamma_{-,1}}_{3\text{ copies}})/\mathbb{R};

the latter moduli space will not be counted since it is not
\vartheta
-sorted.
Depending on whether
|\gamma_{-,1}|_{0}
and
|\gamma_{-,2}|_{0}
are both odd, the total contribution to
\partial\gamma_{+}
from the image of
u
(modulo
\mathbb{R}
-translations) is either zero or
\gamma_{-,1}^{3}\gamma_{-,2}^{5}
.

2. Suppose that
\gamma_{+},\gamma^{\prime}_{+},\gamma_{-}\in\mathcal{P}_{\alpha}^{L,\operatorname{good}}
and that
\gamma_{-}
is simple. Also suppose that
\mathcal{M}(\gamma_{+};\gamma_{-},\gamma_{-})
is regular and that
[\mathcal{F},u]\in\mathcal{M}(\gamma_{+};\gamma_{-},\gamma_{-})
is a branched double cover of a simple curve
[\mathcal{F}^{\prime},u^{\prime}]\in\mathcal{M}(\gamma^{\prime}_{+};\gamma_{-})
. Since reordering the negative punctures of
[\mathcal{F},u]
gives the same curve but changes the sign by
(-1)^{|\gamma_{-}|_{0}\cdot|\gamma_{-}|_{0}}
, the existence of the coherent orientation system implies that
|\gamma_{-}|_{0}
must be even and that
\llbracket\mathcal{F},u\rrbracket
contributes
\pm 1
(depending on the coherent orientation) to
\mathcal{M}(\gamma_{+};\gamma_{-},\gamma_{-})/\mathbb{R}
and contributes
\pm\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{-}^{2}
to
\partial\gamma_{+}
.

9.1.5.
\partial^{2}=0

Proposition 9.1.4
.

\partial
is well-defined and
\mathfrak{A}^{L}
is a dga with differential given by
\partial
.

Proof.

By the construction of the semi-global Kuranishi structure, Proposition
8.3.7
, and the above discussion on coherent orientations, if
|\gamma_{+}|-(|e^{A}|+|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}|)=1
and
\gamma_{+},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}
are good, then
\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}^{L}(\alpha,J;\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-};A),\mathfrak{S})
is a compact oriented weighted branched
0
-dimensional manifold. Hence
\partial
is well-defined. We will abbreviate

\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}(\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-})):=\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}^{L}(\alpha,J;\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-};A),\mathfrak{S}),

assume that the homology classes have been chosen appropriately, and suppress homology classes from the notation. We will also write
\mathcal{Z}^{*}(\mathscr{K}(\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}))
, where
*
is a modifier such as “
\operatorname{ind}=k
”.

To show
\partial^{2}=0
, as usual we identify the terms in
\partial^{2}
as the signed weighted count of the boundary components of certain oriented compact weighted branched
1
-dimensional manifolds.

Let us write
\mathcal{P}_{\alpha}^{L,\operatorname{good}}=\{\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{m}\}
, written in
\vartheta
-sorted order.
For any
\vartheta
-sorted
\boldsymbol{\gamma}=\gamma_{1}^{c_{1}}\dots\gamma_{m}^{c_{m}}
with
c_{1},\dots,c_{m}\geq 0
, applying the definition of
\partial
from Equation (
9.1.1
) we obtain:

(9.1.4)

\displaystyle m_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\langle\partial^{2}\gamma_{+},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\dagger}\rangle=\sum_{A_{1},A_{2}\in H_{2}(M;\mathbb{Z})}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\subseteq\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\sum_{q=0}^{c_{k}-b_{k}}(-1)^{a+b}\frac{d_{\gamma_{+};(\boldsymbol{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{\beta})\sqcup\gamma_{k}}}{m_{(\boldsymbol{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{\beta})\sqcup\gamma_{k}}}\frac{d_{\gamma_{k};\boldsymbol{\beta}}}{m_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}e^{A_{1}+A_{2}}m_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{A_{1},A_{2}\in H_{2}(M;\mathbb{Z})}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\subseteq\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\sum_{q=0}^{c_{k}-b_{k}}(-1)^{a+b}{c_{1}\choose b_{1}}\dots{c_{m}\choose b_{m}}\frac{d_{\gamma_{+};(\boldsymbol{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{\beta})\sqcup\gamma_{k}}d_{\gamma_{k};\boldsymbol{\beta}}}{m(\gamma_{k})(c_{k}-b_{k}+1)}e^{A_{1}+A_{2}},

where
\boldsymbol{\beta}=\gamma_{1}^{b_{1}}\dots\gamma_{m}^{b_{m}}
is a
\vartheta
-sorted subtuple of
\boldsymbol{\gamma}
with
0\leq b_{i}\leq c_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,m
, and
the summands depend on the variable
q
only through
a
and
b
, defined below.

Now we explain the notation appearing in the summands of the above formula:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\dagger}=\gamma_{m}^{c_{m}}\dots\gamma_{1}^{c_{1}},\qquad\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\dagger}=\gamma_{m}^{b_{m}}\dots\gamma_{1}^{b_{1}},

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{\beta})\sqcup\gamma_{k}:=\gamma_{1}^{c_{1}-b_{1}}\dots\gamma_{k}^{c_{k}-b_{k}+1}\dots\gamma_{m}^{c_{m}-b_{m}},

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\dagger}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\dagger})\sqcup\gamma_{k}:=\gamma_{m}^{c_{m}-b_{m}}\dots\gamma_{k}^{c_{k}-b_{k}+1}\dots\gamma_{1}^{c_{1}-b_{1}},

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{\beta})\sqcup_{\gamma_{k},q}\boldsymbol{\beta}:=\gamma_{1}^{c_{1}-b_{1}}\dots\gamma_{k}^{q}(\gamma_{1}^{b_{1}}\dots\gamma_{m}^{b_{m}})\gamma_{k}^{c_{k}-b_{k}-q}\dots\gamma_{m}^{c_{m}-b_{m}},

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\dagger}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\dagger})\sqcup_{\gamma_{k},q}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\dagger}:=\gamma_{m}^{c_{m}-b_{m}}\dots\gamma_{k}^{c_{k}-b_{k}-q}(\gamma_{m}^{b_{m}}\dots\gamma_{1}^{b_{1}})\gamma_{k}^{q}\dots\gamma_{1}^{c_{1}-b_{1}},

and then
(-1)^{b}
is given by the equation

\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\dagger}=(-1)^{b}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\dagger}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\dagger})\sqcup_{\gamma_{k},q}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\dagger}

as supercommutative monomials and
a=|\gamma_{m}^{c_{m}-b_{m}}\dots\gamma_{k}^{c_{k}-b_{k}-q}|_{0}
.

We claim that, for each
\boldsymbol{\beta}\subseteq\boldsymbol{\gamma}
and
k\in\{1,\dots,m\}
with
|\gamma_{k}|-(|e^{A_{1}}|+|\boldsymbol{\beta}|)=1
and
|\gamma_{+}|-(|e^{A_{2}}|+|(\boldsymbol{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{\beta})\sqcup\gamma_{k}|)=1
and
q\in\{0,1,\dots,c_{k}-b_{k}\}
, there exist
\mathcal{L}\gg 0
and a map

G_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k,q}:\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}(\gamma_{k};\boldsymbol{\beta}))\times\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}(\gamma_{+};(\boldsymbol{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{\beta})\sqcup\gamma_{k}))\times O\to\partial^{\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}(\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma})),

where

O
is defined below and
\partial^{\mathcal{L}}
is the
\mathcal{L}
-boundary defined in Proposition
8.3.7
.

The proof of the claim essentially follows from Proposition
8.3.7
, but we work out the combinatorics more carefully with signs. The set
O
is the set of all isomorphism classes of two-vertex CH trees that contract to the one-vertex tree corresponding to the moduli space
\mathcal{M}(\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}),

but we give a more explicit equivalent description.
For any
k\in\{1,\dots,m\}
,
q\in\{0,1,\dots,c_{m}-b_{m}\}
, and

(\llbracket\mathcal{F}_{1},u_{1}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathcal{F}_{2},u_{2}\rrbracket)\in\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}(\gamma_{k};\boldsymbol{\beta}))\times\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}(\gamma_{+};(\boldsymbol{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{\beta})\sqcup\gamma_{k})),

we glue
(\mathcal{F}_{1},u_{1})
and
(\mathcal{F}_{2},u_{2})
so that the positive puncture of
\mathcal{F}_{1}
is glued to the
(q+1)
th negative puncture of
\mathcal{F}_{2}
amongst those that converge to
\gamma_{k}
and denote the glued curve by
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},u^{\prime})
. Note that the negative punctures of
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
have a natural ordering
\tilde{o}
coming from the gluing so that
\llbracket\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\tilde{o}},u^{\prime}\rrbracket\in\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}(\gamma_{+};(\boldsymbol{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{\beta})\sqcup_{\gamma_{k},q}\boldsymbol{\beta}))
. Next let
o
be an ordering of the negative punctures of
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
such that:

(1)

the induced orderings of the negative punctures of
\mathcal{F}_{2}
(minus the negative end that is glued) and
\mathcal{F}_{1}
agree with the initial orderings of the negative punctures of
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}
; and

(2)

the surface
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
with the ordering
o
, denoted by
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{o}
, has
\vartheta
-sorted negative punctures;

and let
O
be the set of such orderings
o
. It is immediate from the definition that
|O|={c_{1}\choose b_{1}}\dots{c_{m}\choose b_{m}}
. We then define

G_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k,q}(\llbracket\mathcal{F}_{1},u_{1}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathcal{F}_{2},u_{2}\rrbracket,o):=\llbracket\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{o},u=u^{\prime}\rrbracket.

We now claim that the weighted signed count of the boundary components of the
1
-dimensional branched manifold
\partial^{\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}(\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}))
is given by the second line of Equation (
9.1.4
).
This is a consequence of the following observations:

(1)

By
(O3)
, the components of
\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}(\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}))
that involve bad orbits do not contribute to the count.

(2)

To apply
(O2’)
, we first reorder the negative punctures of
\mathcal{F}_{2}
so that the
(q+1)
st puncture that converges to
\gamma_{k}
becomes the last puncture. More precisely, we apply the following three steps:

(a)

First consecutively swap this puncture with the negative puncture that is right after it. By
(O1)
, this results in a sign change of

(-1)^{|\gamma_{k}|_{0}\cdot|\gamma_{k}^{c_{k}-b_{k}-q}\cdots\gamma_{m}^{c_{m}-b_{m}}|_{0}}=(-1)^{a\cdot|\gamma_{k}|_{0}}.

(b)

Then glue
(\mathcal{F}_{1},u_{1})
and
(\mathcal{F}_{2},u_{2})
. By
(O2’)
there is no additional sign correction from the gluing.

(c)

Finally, consecutively swap the block of negative punctures that came from
\mathcal{F}_{1}
with the negative puncture right before it until the block takes the place of the
(q+1)
st negative puncture of
\mathcal{F}_{2}
. This step incurs a sign change of

(-1)^{a\cdot|\gamma_{m}^{b_{m}}\dots\gamma_{1}^{b_{1}}|_{0}}.

Hence the total sign change from the gluing is

(-1)^{a\cdot(|\gamma_{k}|_{0}+|\gamma_{m}^{b_{m}}\dots\gamma_{1}^{b_{1}}|_{0})}=(-1)^{a},

since
\partial
changes the degree by
-1
. The glued curve is denoted by
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\tilde{o}},u^{\prime})
, where
\tilde{o}
is the ordering of the negative ends of
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
coming from gluing.

(3)

We reorder the negative punctures of
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\tilde{o}}
to
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{o}
for some
o\in O,
and then we have
\llbracket\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{o},u^{\prime}\rrbracket\in\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}(\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}-))
. Note that the exact element of
o\in O
does not matter in terms of orientations. This is because in Equation (
9.1.4
) we only need to consider the case when there is no
k\in\{1,\cdots,m\}
such that
c_{k}>1
and
|\gamma_{k}|_{0}=1.
Then swapping two adjacent negative punctures that converge to the same orbit does not change the sign. Therefore, the reordering produces a sign, which is the same as the sign obtained from changing
(\boldsymbol{\gamma}-\boldsymbol{\beta})\sqcup_{\gamma_{k},q}\boldsymbol{\beta}
to
\boldsymbol{\gamma}
, and which in turn is the same as the sign
(-1)^{b}
obtained from changing
(\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\dagger}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\dagger})\sqcup_{\gamma_{k},q}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\dagger}
to
\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\dagger}
.

(4)

\operatorname{Im}G_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k,q}=\operatorname{Im}G_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k,q^{\prime}}
for any
q,q^{\prime}\in\{0,1,\dots,c_{m}-b_{m}\}
and this cancels the term
c_{k}-b_{k}+1
in the denominator of the second line of Equation (
9.1.4
).

(5)

There are
m(\gamma_{k})
ways to simultaneously change the asymptotic markers of
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}
such that
G_{\boldsymbol{\beta},k,q}(\llbracket\mathcal{F}_{1},u_{1}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathcal{F}_{2},u_{2}\rrbracket,o)
does not change; this cancels the term
m(\gamma_{k})
in the denominator of the second line of Equation (
9.1.4
).

∎

9.2.
dga morphisms induced by cobordisms

Let
(\widehat{W},\widehat{\alpha})
be the completion of the compact Liouville cobordism
(W,\alpha)
from
(M_{+},\alpha_{+})
to
(M_{-},\alpha_{-})
as in Section
8.10
. Given
L_{+}\leq L_{-}
, let
J
be an almost complex structure on
\widehat{W}
which restricts to
J_{+}
at the positive end and to
J_{-}
at the negative end.
Suppose
(\widehat{\alpha},J)
is an
(L_{+},L_{-})
-simple pair and
J
is
(L_{+},L_{-})
-end-generic.

Using the semi-global Kuranishi structures
\mathscr{K}^{L_{\pm}}:=\mathscr{K}^{L_{\pm}}(\alpha_{\pm},J_{\pm})
, multisections
\mathfrak{S}_{\pm}=\{{\mathfrak{s}}_{T_{\pm}}\}_{T_{\pm}}
, and complete sets
\mathcal{S}^{L_{\pm}}_{\alpha_{\pm}}
of trivializing surfaces for
\mathcal{P}^{L_{\pm}}_{\alpha_{\pm}}
, we can construct the dga’s

\mathfrak{A}^{L_{\pm}}:=\mathfrak{A}^{L_{\pm}}(M_{\pm},\alpha_{\pm},J_{\pm},\mathscr{K}^{L_{\pm}},\mathfrak{S}_{\pm},\mathcal{S}^{L_{\pm}}_{\alpha_{\pm}})

as in Section
9.1
. Here we are also using orderings
\vartheta_{\pm}
of
\mathcal{P}^{L_{\pm}}_{\alpha_{\pm}}
.
We can also construct a semi-global Kuranishi structure

\mathscr{K}^{(L_{+},L_{-})}:=\mathscr{K}^{(L_{+},L_{-})}(\widehat{\alpha},J)

that is compatible with
\mathscr{K}^{L_{\pm}}
, together with a collection of multisections
\mathfrak{S}=\{{\mathfrak{s}}_{T}\}_{T}
. We denote by the above collection of data by:

\mathcal{C}:=(M_{\pm},L_{\pm},\alpha_{\pm},J_{\pm},\mathscr{K}^{L_{\pm}},\mathfrak{S}_{\pm},\mathcal{S}^{L_{\pm}}_{\alpha_{\pm}},\widehat{W},\widehat{\alpha},J,\mathscr{K}^{(L_{+},L_{-})},\mathfrak{S}).

We now define the (unital) dga morphism

\Phi:\mathfrak{A}^{L_{+}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(M_{+};\mathbb{Z})]}\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(W;\mathbb{Z})]\to\mathfrak{A}^{L_{-}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(M_{-};\mathbb{Z})]}\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(W;\mathbb{Z})].

Here we are viewing
\mathfrak{A}^{L_{\pm}}
as
\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(W;\mathbb{Z})]
-modules via the algebra maps

\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(M_{\pm};\mathbb{Z})]\to\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(W;\mathbb{Z})]

induced by
H_{2}(M_{\pm};\mathbb{Z})\to H_{2}(W;\mathbb{Z})
.
For any
\gamma_{+}\in\mathcal{P}_{\alpha_{+}}^{L_{+}}
, let

(9.2.1)

\Phi(\gamma_{+})=\sum_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}}\sum_{A\in H_{2}(W;\mathbb{Z})}\frac{p_{\gamma_{+},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-},A}}{m_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}}}e^{A}\gamma_{-,k}^{i_{k}}\dots\gamma_{-,1}^{i_{1}},

where the sums are taken over all good
\vartheta_{-}
-sorted
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}
written in the form of Equation (
9.1.2
) and
A\in H_{2}(W;\mathbb{Z})
with
|\gamma_{+}|-(|e^{A}|+|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}|)=0
, and
p_{\gamma_{+},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-},A}
is the weighted signed count of elements in
\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}^{(L_{+},L_{-})}(\widehat{\alpha},J;\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-};A),\mathfrak{S})
. (If
|\gamma_{+}|\neq|e^{A}|+|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}|
, then we set
p_{\gamma_{+},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-},A}=0
.) The homology class
A
can be obtained from
(\mathcal{F},u)\in\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}^{(L_{+},L_{-})}(\widehat{\alpha},J;\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}),\mathfrak{S})
by capping off using
\mathcal{S}^{L_{\pm}}_{\alpha_{\pm}}
as in Section
9.1.2
.
\Phi
can then be extended to
\mathfrak{A}^{L_{+}}
as an algebra homomorphism.

Proposition 9.2.1
.

\Phi
is a well-defined (unital) dga morphism.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Proposition
9.1.4
.
∎

Let
\Phi_{*}:HC^{L_{+}}\to HC^{L_{-}}
be the induced map on homology.

Proposition 9.2.2
.

Let
(\widehat{W},\widehat{\alpha})
be a trivial symplectic cobordism.
Given
L_{+}\leq L_{-}
, let
J
be an
\mathbb{R}
-translation invariant almost complex structure on
\widehat{W}
such that
(\widehat{\alpha},J)
is an
(L_{+},L_{-})
-simple pair. Then
\Phi_{*}=\operatorname{id}
with respect to semi-global Kuranishi structures defined using
J
, multisections, and complete sets of trivializing surfaces.

Proof.

The
\mathbb{R}
-invariant almost complex structure
J
is automatically
(L_{+},L_{-})
-end-generic, with the exception of possibly branched covers of trivial cylinders. There are three types of moduli spaces
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-};A)
in the cobordism
\widehat{W}
: (i)
\mathcal{A}(\gamma_{+})>\mathcal{A}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-})
, (ii)
\mathcal{A}(\gamma_{+})=\mathcal{A}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-})
and the curves are (possibly multiple levels of) branched covers of trivial cylinders, and (iii) trivial cylinders.

In order to construct Kuranishi structures
\mathscr{K}^{(L_{+},L_{-})}(\widehat{\alpha},J;\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-};A)
with respect to
(\widehat{W},J)
using our prescription, it suffices to puncture (iii) trivial cylinders in view of Remark
5.1.6
, since curves of type (ii) have
\chi<0
and do not need to be punctured. Let
J^{\prime}
be a sufficiently small perturbation of
J
such that
J^{\prime}
is
(L_{+},L_{-})
-simple,
(L_{+},L_{-})
-end-generic, and compatible with
J=J_{\pm}
at the positive and negative ends. Since (iii) is regular and a sufficiently small perturbation of
J
to
J^{\prime}
preserves regularity, there exists a bijection between trivial cylinders
u:\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}\to(\widehat{W},J)
and
J^{\prime}
-holomorphic cylinders
u^{\prime}:\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}\to(\widehat{W},J^{\prime})
with the same ends for
J^{\prime}
sufficiently close to
J
. Hence may take the punctures
\mathbf{q}
for
u
and
\mathbf{q}^{\prime}
for
u^{\prime}
to be at the same
s
-height.
19
19

19

Roughly speaking,
\mathscr{K}^{(L_{+},L_{-})}(\widehat{\alpha},J;\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-};A)
and
\mathscr{K}^{(L_{+},L_{-})}(\widehat{\alpha},J^{\prime};\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-};A)
, as well as their multisections, can be taken to be “close”, so we can use either to compute
\Phi_{*}
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}^{(L_{+},L_{-})}(\widehat{\alpha},J;\gamma_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-};A),\{{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}_{T}}\}_{T})
. The key point is to take all the multisections
\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}_{T}
— besides the multisection corresponding to (iii), which we can take to be
0
— to be perturbations of
s
-invariant multisections. Then in Cases (i) and (ii)
\mathcal{Z}
is empty when
\operatorname{vdim}=0
, since we are effectively computing
\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}^{L}(\alpha,J;\gamma_{+},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-};A),\{{\mathfrak{s}_{T}}\}_{T})
in
\operatorname{(Symp)}
when
\operatorname{vdim}=0
. In Case (iii),
\mathcal{Z}
consists of only one element for each
\gamma_{+}
, the trivial cylinder.

After a sign count that is left to the reader, we obtain
\Phi_{*}=\operatorname{id}
.
∎

9.3.
Definition of contact homology

We are now in a position to define the contact homology algebra
HC(\mathcal{D})
. Given a closed cooriented contact manifold
(M,\xi)
, let

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}=

\displaystyle(\alpha,\{L_{i},\varphi_{i},J_{i},\mathscr{K}^{L_{i}}(\alpha_{i},J_{i}),\mathfrak{S}_{i},\mathcal{S}^{L_{i}}_{\alpha_{i}},\widehat{\alpha}_{i,i+1},J_{i,i+1},

\displaystyle\qquad\mathscr{K}^{(L_{i},L_{i+1})}(\widehat{\alpha}_{i,i+1},J_{i,i+1}),\mathfrak{S}_{i,i+1}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}})

be a collection that consists of the following data:

•

a nondegenerate contact form
\alpha
for
\xi
;

•

an increasing sequence
\{L_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
L_{i}\to\infty
;

and, for each
i\in\mathbb{N}
,

•

a positive function
\varphi_{i}
on
M
which is close to
1
, such that
\alpha_{i}:=\varphi_{i}\alpha
is an
L_{i}
-simple contact form and
\varphi_{i}<\varphi_{i+1}
;

•

an almost complex structure
J_{i}
on
\mathbb{R}\times M
such that
(\alpha_{i},J_{i})
is an
L_{i}
-simple pair;

•

a semi-global Kuranishi structure
\mathscr{K}^{L_{i}}(\alpha_{i},J_{i})
and multisections
\mathfrak{S}_{i}
;

•

a complete set
\mathcal{S}^{L_{i}}_{\alpha_{i}}
of trivializing surfaces for
\mathcal{P}^{L_{i}}_{\alpha_{i}}
;

•

a completion
(\widehat{W}_{i,i+1}=\mathbb{R}\times M,\widehat{\alpha}_{i,i+1})
of a compact Liouville cobordism from
(M,\alpha_{i})
to
(M,\alpha_{i+1})
; here
\widehat{\alpha}_{i,i+1}:=\varphi_{i,i+1}\alpha
, where
\varphi_{i,i+1}
interpolates from a positive multiple of
\varphi_{i}
to
\varphi_{i+1}
;

•

an almost complex structure
J_{i,i+1}
on
\widehat{W}_{i,i+1}
that tames
d\widehat{\alpha}_{i,i+1}
, restricts to
J_{i}
at the positive end and to
J_{i+1}
at the negative end, and is
(L_{i},L_{i+1})
-end-generic;

•

a semi-global Kuranishi structure
\mathscr{K}^{(L_{i},L_{i+1})}(\widehat{\alpha}_{i,i+1},J_{i,i+1})
that is compatible with
\mathscr{K}^{L_{i}}(\alpha_{i},J_{i})
and
\mathscr{K}^{L_{i+1}}(\alpha_{i+1},J_{i+1})
and multisections
\mathfrak{S}_{i,i+1}
.

Denote the map induced by the cobordism
(\widehat{W}_{i,i+1},\widehat{\alpha}_{i,i+1},J_{i,i+1})
and the semi-global Kuranishi structure
\mathscr{K}^{(L_{i},L_{i+1})}(\widehat{\alpha}_{i,i+1},J_{i,i+1}))
and multisections
\mathfrak{S}_{i,i+1}
by

\displaystyle\Phi_{i*}:HC^{L_{i}}

\displaystyle(\alpha_{i},J_{i};\mathscr{K}^{L_{i}}(\alpha_{i},J_{i}),\mathfrak{S}_{i})\to

\displaystyle HC^{L_{i+1}}(\alpha_{i+1},J_{i+1};\mathscr{K}^{L_{i+1}}(\alpha_{i+1},J_{i+1}),\mathfrak{S}_{i+1}).

Finally, we define the contact homology algebra by

H(\mathcal{D}):=\lim_{\longrightarrow}HC^{L_{i}}(\alpha_{i},J_{i};\mathscr{K}^{L_{i}}(\alpha_{i},J_{i}),\mathfrak{S}_{i}),

where the directed system is constructed using
\Phi_{i*}
. From now on we will often suppress the multisections from the notation.

9.4.
Invariance of contact homology

In this section we prove Propositions
9.4.1
and
9.4.2
and use them to prove that
HC(\mathcal{D})
is an invariant of the contact manifold
(M,\xi)
in Section
9.4.2
.

Let
\{(\widehat{W}^{\tau}=\widehat{W},\widehat{\alpha}^{\tau})\}_{0\leq\tau\leq 1}
be a family of completed compact Liouville cobordisms from
(M_{+},\alpha_{+})
to
(M_{-},\alpha_{-})
. For each
\tau\in[0,1]
, let
J^{\tau}
be an almost complex structure on
\widehat{W}^{\tau}
that restricts to
J_{\pm}
at the positive/negative ends of
\widehat{W}^{\tau}
. We assume that
(\widehat{\alpha}^{\tau},J^{\tau})
is an
(L_{+},L_{-})
-simple pair,
J^{\tau}
is
(L_{+},L_{-})
-end-generic, and
(\widehat{\alpha}^{\tau},J^{\tau})
is independent of
\tau
on each of
\tau\in[0,\epsilon]
and
\tau\in[1-\epsilon,1]
, where
\epsilon>0
is small.

Let
\mathscr{K}^{L_{\pm}}:=\mathscr{K}^{L_{\pm}}(\alpha_{\pm},J_{\pm})
be the semi-global Kuranishi structures constructed for
(M_{\pm},\alpha_{\pm},J_{\pm})
, let
\mathfrak{S}_{\pm}
be the multisections on
\mathscr{K}^{L_{\pm}}
, and let

\mathfrak{A}^{L_{\pm}}=\mathfrak{A}^{L_{\pm}}(M_{\pm},\alpha_{\pm},J_{\pm},\mathscr{K}^{L_{\pm}},\mathfrak{S}_{\pm},\mathcal{S}^{L_{\pm}}_{\alpha_{\pm}})

as before.

9.4.1.
Chain homotopy

For
\tau=0,1
, we have two collections of data

\mathcal{C}^{\tau}=(M_{\pm},L_{\pm},\alpha_{\pm},J_{\pm},\mathscr{K}^{L_{\pm}},\mathcal{S}^{L_{\pm}}_{\alpha_{\pm}},\widehat{W}^{\tau},\widehat{\alpha}^{\tau},J^{\tau},\mathscr{K}^{(L_{+},L_{-})})

as in Section
9.2
and chain maps

\Phi^{\tau}:\mathfrak{A}^{L_{+}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(M_{+};\mathbb{Z})]}\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(W;\mathbb{Z})]\to\mathfrak{A}^{L_{-}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(M_{-};\mathbb{Z})]}\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(W;\mathbb{Z})],

defined using the data
\mathcal{C}^{\tau}
.

Proposition 9.4.1

(Chain homotopy)
.

There is a degree
1

\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(W;\mathbb{Z})]
-module map

\Omega_{K}:\mathfrak{A}^{L_{+}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(M_{+};\mathbb{Z})]}\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(W;\mathbb{Z})]\to\mathfrak{A}^{L_{-}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(M_{-};\mathbb{Z})]}\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(W;\mathbb{Z})]

such that
\Phi^{1}-\Phi^{0}=\partial_{-}\circ\Omega_{K}-\Omega_{K}\circ\partial_{+}.
In particular,
\Phi^{0}_{*}=\Phi^{1}_{*}
.

Proof.

The discussion of Section
8.11
yields a semi-global Kuranishi structure

\mathscr{K}^{(L_{+},L_{-})}(\{(\widehat{\alpha}^{\tau},J^{\tau})\}_{\tau\in[0,1]})

so that it is independent of
\tau
on each of
\tau\in[0,\epsilon]
and
\tau\in[1-\epsilon,1]
and agrees with
\mathscr{K}^{(L_{+},L_{-})}(\widehat{\alpha}^{\tau},J^{\tau})
for
\tau=0,1
, as well as a collection
\mathfrak{S}^{[0,1]}=\{{\mathfrak{s}}_{T}^{[0,1]}\}_{T}
of multisections. We are using orderings
\vartheta_{\pm}
for
\mathcal{P}^{L_{\pm}}_{\alpha_{\pm}}
.

We define
\Omega_{K}
as follows: First let
\Omega_{K}(1)=0
. For any
k
-tuple
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+}
of good Reeb orbits, we set

(9.4.1)

\Omega_{K}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+})=\sum_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}}\sum_{A\in H_{2}(W;\mathbb{Z})}\frac{\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-},A}}{m_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-,1}}\dots m_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-,k}}}e^{A}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-,k}^{\dagger}\dots\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-,1}^{\dagger},

where the sums are taken over all
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}
which is a
k
-tuple of
\vartheta_{-}
-sorted tuples of good Reeb orbits, i.e.,

\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}=(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-,1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-,k})

and

(9.4.2)

\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-,j}^{\dagger}=(\underbrace{\gamma_{-,j,r_{j}},\dots,\gamma_{-,j,r_{j}}}_{i_{j,r_{j}}\text{ copies }},\dots,\underbrace{\gamma_{-,j,1},\dots,\gamma_{-,j,1}}_{i_{j,1}\text{ copies }}),

A\in H_{2}(W;\mathbb{Z})
with
|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+}|-(|e^{A}|+|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}|)=-1
, and
\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-},A}
is the weighted signed count of the elements in

\mathcal{Z}^{[0,1]}(\mathscr{K}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-},A)):=\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}^{(L_{+},L_{-})}(\{(\widehat{\alpha}^{\tau},J^{\tau})\}_{\tau\in[0,1]};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+};\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-};A),\mathfrak{S}^{[0,1]}).

In what follows we will usually suppress
A
from the notation.

Let
\Phi^{0},\Phi^{1}
be two dga morphisms

\mathfrak{A}^{L_{+}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(M_{+};\mathbb{Z})]}\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(W;\mathbb{Z})]\to\mathfrak{A}^{L_{-}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(M_{-};\mathbb{Z})]}\mathbb{Q}[H_{2}(W;\mathbb{Z})],

induced by the cobordism
(\widehat{W}^{\tau},\widehat{\alpha}^{\tau},J^{\tau})
,
\tau=0,1
, using the semi-global Kuranishi structure
\mathscr{K}^{(L_{+},L_{-})}(\widehat{\alpha}^{\tau},J^{\tau})
. We can verify that
\Omega_{K}
is well-defined and satisfies

(9.4.3)

\Phi^{1}-\Phi^{0}=\partial_{-}\circ\Omega_{K}-\Omega_{K}\circ\partial_{+}

as in the proof of Proposition
9.1.4
. The key point is to observe that, with the perturbation from Section
8.11
, each end of a
1
-dimensional (and hence
\operatorname{ind}=0
) branched manifold
\mathcal{Z}^{[0,1]}(\mathscr{K}(\gamma_{+},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}))
corresponds to:

(i)

a term contributing to
\Phi^{0}
or
\Phi^{1}
;

(ii)

the gluing of an element of some
\mathcal{Z}^{[0,1]}(\mathscr{K}(\gamma_{+},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}^{\prime}))
of index
-1
corresponding to
\Omega_{K}
and an element of some
\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}))
corresponding to
\partial_{-}
where all but one of the components is a trivial cylinder; or

(iii)

the gluing of an element of some
\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{K}(\gamma_{+},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+}^{\prime}))
of index
1
corresponding to
\partial_{+}
and an element of some
\mathcal{Z}^{[0,1]}(\mathscr{K}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}))
of index
-1
corresponding to
\Omega_{K}
. (In particular, if
\operatorname{ind}<-1
, then
\mathcal{Z}^{[0,1]}(\mathscr{K}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}))=\varnothing
and
\mathcal{Z}^{[0,1]}(\mathscr{K}(\gamma_{+},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{-}^{\prime}))=\varnothing
.)

The signs follow from the proof of Proposition
9.1.4
and
[
BH
, Section 9.2.2]
. In particular, the sign difference of the two terms on right-hand side of Equation (
9.4.3
